  1351	    def _drain_dispatch_snapshot(self) -> reads.ControlSnapshot:
  1352	        """Promote PENDING->ASSIGNED for Kubernetes and ride the drain.
  1353	
  1354	        The dispatch drain is the single DB write a Kubernetes backend needs
  1355	        before reconcile (the controller owns the write; the backend places tasks
  1356	        itself). It runs in its own write transaction, so that tick commits twice
  1357	        (drain + end-of-tick).
  1358	        """
  1359	        max_promotions = self._promotion_bucket.available
  1360	        with self._db.transaction() as cur:
  1361	            batch = dispatch.drain_for_dispatch(
  1362	                cur,
  1363	                max_promotions=max_promotions,
  1364	                defaults=self._config.user_budget_defaults,
  1365	            )
  1366	        if batch.tasks_to_run:
  1367	            self._promotion_bucket.try_acquire(len(batch.tasks_to_run))
  1368	        return reads.ControlSnapshot(
  1369	            worker_addresses={},
  1370	            reconcile_rows=[],
  1371	            timeout_rows=[],
  1372	            tasks_to_run=batch.tasks_to_run,
  1373	            running_tasks=batch.running_tasks,
  1374	        )
  1375	
  1376	    def _drain_pending_evictions(self) -> None:
  1377	        """Tear down workers queued by :meth:`request_worker_eviction`."""
  1378	        with self._pending_evictions_lock:
  1379	            if not self._pending_evictions:
  1380	                return
  1381	            drained = sorted(self._pending_evictions)
  1382	            self._pending_evictions.clear()
  1383	        reason = "address reused by newly-registered worker (recycled IP)"
  1384	        self.backend.teardown(drained, reason=reason)
  1385	
  1386	    def _drain_pending_kicks(self) -> list[PendingKick]:
  1387	        """Take the queued administrative kicks for this tick's commit."""
  1388	        with self._pending_kicks_lock:
  1389	            if not self._pending_kicks:
  1390	                return []
  1391	            drained = self._pending_kicks
  1392	            self._pending_kicks = []
  1393	        return drained
  1394	
  1395	    def _resolve_pending_kicks(self, cur: Tx, pending_kicks: list[PendingKick]) -> list[TerminalDecision]:
  1396	        """Turn queued kicks into terminal decisions, dropping superseded attempts.
  1397	
  1398	        A kick targeting a specific attempt is dropped if that attempt is no longer
  1399	        current (the task retried in the meantime); a kick with no attempt id takes
  1400	        whatever attempt is current. Reads ``cur`` to see this tick's earlier writes.
  1401	        """
  1402	        decisions: list[TerminalDecision] = []
  1403	        for kick in pending_kicks:
  1404	            if kick.attempt_id is not None:
  1405	                detail = reads.get_task_detail(cur, kick.task_id)
  1406	                if detail is None or detail.current_attempt_id != kick.attempt_id:
  1407	                    logger.info(
  1408	                        "Dropping kick for %s: attempt %d is no longer current",
  1409	                        kick.task_id.to_wire(),
  1410	                        kick.attempt_id,
  1411	                    )
  1412	                    continue
  1413	            decisions.append(TerminalDecision(kind=kick.kind, task_id=kick.task_id, reason=kick.reason))
  1414	        return decisions
  1415	
  1416	    def begin_checkpoint(self) -> tuple[str, CheckpointResult]:
  1417	        """Write a consistent SQLite checkpoint copy.
  1418	
  1419	        The backup runs through a dedicated read-only source connection
  1420	        (see ``ControllerDB.backup_to``), so writers proceed concurrently
  1421	        under WAL semantics. Heartbeat rounds apply their updates as
  1422	        atomic batches, so each SQLite snapshot already captures a
  1423	        consistent state without needing the heartbeat lock.
  1424	        """
  1425	        if self._config.dry_run:
  1426	            logger.info("[DRY-RUN] Skipping checkpoint write")
  1427	            return ("dry-run", CheckpointResult(created_at=Timestamp.now(), job_count=0, task_count=0, worker_count=0))
  1428	        backup = backup_databases(self._db)
  1429	        try:
  1430	            path, result = upload_checkpoint(self._db, backup, self._config.remote_state_dir)
  1431	        finally:
  1432	            backup.cleanup()
  1433	        log_event(
  1434	            "checkpoint_written",
  1435	            "controller",
  1436	            path=path,
  1437	            jobs=result.job_count,
  1438	            tasks=result.task_count,
  1439	            workers=result.worker_count,
  1440	        )
  1441	        return path, result

  1450	    def run_control_tick(self) -> None:
  1451	        """Run one complete control cycle synchronously before :meth:`start`.
  1452	
  1453	        This is the deterministic embedding boundary for callers that own the
  1454	        controller lifecycle themselves. It drives scheduling, reconciliation,
  1455	        and autoscaling once without starting background threads. A running
  1456	        controller already owns its control loop, so mixing the two modes is an
  1457	        error.
  1458	        """

  1461	        self._control_tick(
  1462	            woken=True,
  1463	            schedule_limiter=RateLimiter(interval_seconds=_SYNCHRONOUS_PHASE_INTERVAL),
  1464	            reconcile_limiter=RateLimiter(interval_seconds=_SYNCHRONOUS_PHASE_INTERVAL),
  1465	            autoscale_limiter=RateLimiter(interval_seconds=_SYNCHRONOUS_PHASE_INTERVAL),
  1466	            force_timeout_scan=True,
  1467	        )

   635	class FrayIrisClient:
   636	    """Iris cluster backend for fray.
   637	
   638	    Wraps iris.client.IrisClient to implement the fray Client protocol.
   639	    Jobs are submitted via Iris, actors are hosted as Iris jobs with endpoint
   640	    registration for discovery.
   641	    """
   642	
   643	    def __init__(
   644	        self,
   645	        controller_address: str,
   646	        workspace: Path | None = None,
   647	        bundle_id: str | None = None,
   648	    ):
   649	        logger.info(
   650	            "FrayIrisClient connecting to %s (workspace=%s, bundle_id=%s)",
   651	            controller_address,
   652	            workspace,
   653	            bundle_id,
   654	        )
   655	        self._iris = IrisClientLib.remote(controller_address, workspace=workspace, bundle_id=bundle_id)
   656	
   657	    @staticmethod
   658	    def from_iris_client(iris_client: IrisClientLib) -> "FrayIrisClient":
   659	        """Create a FrayIrisClient by wrapping an existing IrisClient.
   660	
   661	        This avoids creating a new connection when we already have an IrisClient
   662	        from the context (e.g., when running inside an Iris task).
   663	        """
   664	        instance = cast(FrayIrisClient, object.__new__(FrayIrisClient))
   665	        instance._iris = iris_client
   666	        return instance
   667	
   668	    def submit(self, request: JobRequest, adopt_existing: bool = True) -> IrisJobHandle:
   669	        iris_resources = convert_resources(request.resources)
   670	        iris_entrypoint = convert_entrypoint(request.entrypoint)
   671	        if request.processes_per_task > 1:
   672	            iris_entrypoint = wrap_multiprocess(iris_entrypoint, iris_resources, request.processes_per_task)
   673	        iris_entrypoint = wrap_nsys(iris_entrypoint)
   674	        iris_environment = convert_environment(request.environment, request.resources.device)
   675	        iris_constraints = convert_constraints(request.resources)
   676	
   677	        replicas = request.replicas or 1
   678	        coscheduling = resolve_coscheduling(request.resources, replicas)
   679	
   680	        policy = job_pb2.EXISTING_JOB_POLICY_KEEP if adopt_existing else job_pb2.EXISTING_JOB_POLICY_UNSPECIFIED
   681	        try:
   682	            job = self._iris.submit(
   683	                entrypoint=iris_entrypoint,
   684	                name=request.name,
   685	                resources=iris_resources,
   686	                environment=iris_environment,
   687	                constraints=iris_constraints if iris_constraints else None,
   688	                coscheduling=coscheduling,
   689	                replicas=replicas,
   690	                max_retries_failure=request.max_retries_failure,
   691	                max_retries_preemption=request.max_retries_preemption,
   692	                max_task_failures=request.max_task_failures,
   693	                existing_job_policy=policy,
   694	                task_image=request.resources.image,
   695	                priority_band=request.priority,
   696	            )
   697	        except IrisJobAlreadyExists as e:
   698	            raise FrayJobAlreadyExists(request.name) from e
   699	        return IrisJobHandle(job)
   700	
   701	    def host_actor(
   702	        self,
   703	        actor_class: type,
   704	        *args: Any,
   705	        name: str,
   706	        actor_config: ActorConfig = ActorConfig(),
   707	        **kwargs: Any,
   708	    ) -> HostedActor:
   709	        """Host an actor in the current process with Iris RPC serving."""
   710	        ctx = iris_ctx()
   711	        job_info = get_job_info()
   712	        if job_info is None:
   713	            raise RuntimeError("host_actor requires an Iris job context")
   714	
   715	        actor_name = f"{ctx.job_id}/{name}-0"
   716	        handle = IrisActorHandle(actor_name)
   717	        actor_ctx = ActorContext(handle=handle, index=0, group_name=name)
   718	        token = _set_current_actor(actor_ctx)
   719	        try:
   720	            instance = actor_class(*args, **kwargs)
   721	        finally:
   722	            _reset_current_actor(token)
   723	
   724	        server = ActorServer(host="0.0.0.0", port=0)
   725	        server.register(actor_name, instance)
   726	        actual_port = server.serve_background()
   727	
   728	        address = f"http://{job_info.advertise_host}:{actual_port}"
   729	        logger.info("host_actor: registered %s -> %s", actor_name, address)
   730	        ctx.registry.register(actor_name, address)

   311	    return hbm_bytes

   314	def tpu_hbm_capacity_bytes(tpu_type: str) -> int:
   315	    """Return the aggregate HBM capacity of a TPU slice in bytes."""
   316	    topology = get_tpu_topology(tpu_type)

   340	@dataclass(frozen=True)
   341	class GpuConfig:
   342	    """GPU device configuration."""
   343	
   344	    variant: GpuType
   345	    kind: DeviceKind = "gpu"
   346	    count: int = 1
   347	
   348	    def chip_count(self) -> int:
   349	        return self.count
   350	
   351	    def device_flops(self, dtype: str = "bf16") -> float:
   352	        flops = _device_flops(self.variant, dtype)
   353	        if flops is None:
   354	            raise ValueError(f"Unknown device/dtype: {self.variant}/{dtype}")
   355	        return flops
   356	
   357	    def total_flops(self, dtype: str = "bf16") -> float:
   358	        return self.device_flops(dtype) * self.count
   359	
   360	    def default_env_vars(self) -> dict[str, str]:
   361	        return {"JAX_PLATFORMS": ""}

   364	@dataclass(frozen=True)
   365	class TpuConfig:
   366	    """TPU device configuration.
   367	
   368	    Args:
   369	        variant: TPU accelerator type (e.g., "v5litepod-16", "v4-8")
   370	        topology: Optional topology specification (e.g., "2x2x1")
   371	    """
   372	
   373	    variant: TpuType
   374	    kind: DeviceKind = "tpu"
   375	    topology: str | None = None
   376	
   377	    def chip_count(self) -> int:
   378	        """Return the number of chips per VM for this TPU type."""
   379	        return get_tpu_topology(self.variant).chips_per_vm
   380	
   381	    def vm_count(self) -> int:
   382	        return get_tpu_topology(self.variant).vm_count
   383	
   384	    def device_flops(self, dtype: str = "bf16") -> float:
   385	        flops = _device_flops(self.variant, dtype)
   386	        if flops is None:
   387	            raise ValueError(f"Unknown device/dtype: {self.variant}/{dtype}")
   388	        return flops
   389	
   390	    def total_flops(self, dtype: str = "bf16") -> float:
   391	        return self.device_flops(dtype) * self.chip_count()
   392	
   393	    def default_env_vars(self) -> dict[str, str]:
   394	        defaults: dict[str, str] = {"JAX_PLATFORMS": ""}
   395	        if self.variant.startswith(("v5litepod-", "v5e-", "v5p-")):
   396	            defaults["LIBTPU_INIT_ARGS"] = "--xla_tpu_scoped_vmem_limit_kib=50000"
   397	        elif self.variant.startswith("v6e-"):
   398	            defaults["LIBTPU_INIT_ARGS"] = "--xla_tpu_scoped_vmem_limit_kib=98304"
   399	        return defaults

   404	
   405	# Sentinel region value meaning ANY: run anywhere; do NOT inherit the parent job's region.
   406	# Distinct from ``None`` (UNSET — inherit) and a concrete region list (PINNED). Pass it as the
   407	# sole element of ``ResourceConfig.regions`` (``regions=[ANY_REGION]``). Not a real region, so
   408	# it never collides with a GCP region name.
   409	ANY_REGION = "*"
   410	
   411	
   412	@dataclass
   413	class ResourceConfig:
   414	    """Resource requirements for a single task/replica.
   415	
   416	    `replicas` specifies gang-scheduled replica count (e.g. TPU slices for
   417	    multislice training). It is also on JobRequest; when both are set,
   418	    JobRequest.replicas takes precedence. This field exists here so that
   419	    convenience builders like `with_tpu(..., slice_count=4)` can carry the
   420	    replica count alongside the resource spec.
   421	
   422	    `image` is an optional override for the task container image. When None,
   423	    the backend uses its cluster-configured default. Used for jobs that need
   424	    a custom runtime (e.g. an image with runsc/skopeo for sandboxing
   425	    untrusted child workloads).
   426	
   427	    `target_cluster` routes an Iris job to a named peer cluster. Other backends
   428	    may ignore it.
   429	    """
   430	
   431	    cpu: float = 1
   432	    ram: str = "4g"
   433	    # Scratch budget for the task. On Kubernetes this becomes the pod's
   434	    # ephemeral-storage request and limit, which bills disk-backed emptyDir
   435	    # usage -- including /tmp -- and evicts the pod when the sum exceeds it.
   436	    disk: str = "64g"
   437	    device: DeviceConfig = field(default_factory=CpuConfig)
   438	    preemptible: bool = True
   439	    # Region state: UNSET (None, default) inherits the parent job's region; PINNED (a
   440	    # region list) restricts to those regions; ANY ([ANY_REGION]) runs anywhere without
   441	    # inheriting.
   442	    regions: Sequence[str] | None = None
   443	    zone: str | None = None
   444	    target_cluster: str | None = None
   445	    replicas: int = 1
   446	    device_alternatives: Sequence[str] | None = None
   447	    image: str | None = None
   448	
   449	    def chip_count(self) -> int:
   450	        """Total accelerator chips across all replicas."""
   451	        return self.device.chip_count() * self.replicas
   452	
   453	    def device_flops(self, dtype: str = "bf16") -> float:
   454	        return self.device.device_flops(dtype)
   455	
   456	    def total_flops(self, dtype: str = "bf16") -> float:
   457	        if isinstance(self.device, CpuConfig):
   458	            return 100e9
   459	        return self.device_flops(dtype) * self.chip_count()
   460	
   461	    def scale(
   462	        self,
   463	        factor: float | None = None,
   464	        /,
   465	        *,
   466	        cpu: float | None = None,
   467	        ram: float | None = None,
   468	        disk: float | None = None,
   469	    ) -> "ResourceConfig":
   470	        """Return a copy with cpu/ram/disk multiplied by the given factors.
   471	
   472	        ``rc.scale(2)`` multiplies cpu, ram, and disk by 2; ``rc.scale(0.5)``
   473	        halves all three. Keyword args ``cpu``, ``ram``, and ``disk`` are
   474	        multiplicative factors for individual dimensions (e.g. ``cpu=0.5``
   475	        halves CPU); omitted dimensions keep their current value. ``factor``
   476	        cannot be combined with keyword factors.
   477	        """
   478	        if factor is not None and (cpu is not None or ram is not None or disk is not None):
   479	            raise ValueError("Pass either a single factor or cpu/ram/disk keyword factors, not both.")
   480	        if factor is not None:
   481	            cpu_factor = ram_factor = disk_factor = factor
   482	        else:
   483	            cpu_factor = 1.0 if cpu is None else cpu
   484	            ram_factor = 1.0 if ram is None else ram
   485	            disk_factor = 1.0 if disk is None else disk
   486	
   487	        return replace(
   488	            self,
   489	            cpu=self.cpu * cpu_factor,
   490	            ram=_scale_byte_size(self.ram, ram_factor),
   491	            disk=_scale_byte_size(self.disk, disk_factor),
   492	        )
   493	
   494	    @staticmethod
   495	    def with_tpu(tpu_type: str | Sequence[str], *, slice_count: int = 1, **kwargs: Any) -> "ResourceConfig":
   496	        """Create a resource config for TPU(s).
   497	
   498	        When ``tpu_type`` is a list, the first entry is canonical (used for
   499	        chip_count, env_vars, resource sizing) and the rest are alternatives.
   500	        All types in a list must share both ``vm_count`` and ``chips_per_vm``:
   501	        a TPU VM is the atomic scheduling unit, so mixing variants with
   502	        different per-VM chip counts (e.g. ``v6e-4`` + ``v6e-8``) would let
   503	        the scheduler co-locate two partial-VM jobs onto a VM that cannot
   504	        actually be shared.
   505	
   506	        ``cpu``/``ram`` default to ``DEFAULT_TPU_HOST_FRACTION`` (50%) of the
   507	        primary type's per-VM host (see ``TPU_HOST_RESOURCES``), leaving
   508	        headroom for CPU-task multiplexing while giving training enough host
   509	        RAM for checkpoint serialization. Pass ``cpu=``/``ram=`` to override.
   510	        """

 succeeded in 0ms:
   250	        for plan, result in plan_results:
   251	            for update in self._reconcile_updates_for_plan(plan, result):
   252	                self._apply_update(update, now_ms, source=task.TransitionSource.WORKER_RECONCILE)
   253	
   254	        self._recompute_and_finalize(now_ms)
   255	        return self.overlay.effects
   256	
   257	    def record_updates(self, updates: list[TaskUpdate]) -> ControllerEffects:
   258	        """Apply a batch of task-state updates from a direct (e.g. Kubernetes) provider."""
   259	        now_ms = self._snapshot.now.epoch_ms()
   260	        # Direct providers manage their own hosts -> no build-failed reaping.
   261	        for update in updates:
   262	            self._apply_update(update, now_ms, source=task.TransitionSource.DISPATCH)
   263	        cascaded_jobs = self._recompute_and_finalize(now_ms)
   264	
   265	        if cascaded_jobs:
   266	            self.overlay.emit_log_event(LogEvent(action="dispatch_updates_applied", entity_id="direct"))
   267	            for job_id in cascaded_jobs:
   268	                basis = self.overlay.job_basis(job_id)
   269	                assert basis is not None
   270	                self.overlay.emit_log_event(
   271	                    LogEvent(
   272	                        action="job_terminated",
   273	                        entity_id=job_id.to_wire(),
   274	                        trigger="dispatch_updates_applied",
   275	                        details=(("reason", _terminal_job_reason(self.overlay, job_id, basis.state)),),
   276	                    )
   277	                )
   278	        return self.overlay.effects
   279	
   280	    def fail_workers(self, failures: list[tuple[WorkerId, str | None, str]]) -> ControllerEffects:
   281	        """Cascade a batch of worker failures against the shared overlay.
   282	
   283	        Active tasks on the failed workers are derived from the snapshot — the
   284	        loader (``load_closed_snapshot`` seeded by worker) closes them, so the
   285	        batch reads only the snapshot.
   286	        """
   287	        now_ms = self._snapshot.now.epoch_ms()
   288	        rows_by_worker = self._active_rows_by_failed_worker({wid for wid, _, _ in failures})
   289	
   290	        for worker_id, worker_address, error in failures:
   291	            for task_row in rows_by_worker.get(worker_id, []):
   292	                outcome = self._fail_one_task(task_row, worker_id, error, now_ms)
   293	                if outcome is not None:
   294	                    self._fan_out(outcome, child_reason="Parent task preempted", now_ms=now_ms)
   295	            # No health mutation here: the controller has already decided this
   296	            # worker is dead and forgets it once removal commits.
   297	            self.overlay.emit_log_event(
   298	                LogEvent(
   299	                    action="worker_failed",
   300	                    entity_id=str(worker_id),
   301	                    details=(("address", worker_address or "-"), ("error", error)),
   302	                )
   303	            )
   304	
   305	        self._recompute_and_finalize(now_ms)
   306	        return self.overlay.effects
   307	
   308	    def finalize_tasks(self, decisions: list[task.TerminalDecision]) -> ControllerEffects:
   309	        """Batched terminal-state assertions: preempt / timeout / unschedulable."""
   310	        if not decisions:
   311	            return self.overlay.effects
   312	        now_ms = self._snapshot.now.epoch_ms()
   313	
   314	        seen_tasks: set[JobName] = set()
   315	        ordered: list[task.TerminalDecision] = []
   316	        for decision in decisions:
   317	            if decision.task_id in seen_tasks:
   318	                continue
   319	            seen_tasks.add(decision.task_id)
   320	            ordered.append(decision)
   321	
   322	        # Batch timeout decisions together so the two-phase sibling dedup
   323	        # operates on the full set at once.
   324	        timeout_rows: list[ActiveTaskRow] = []
   325	        timeout_reason: str | None = None
   326	        for decision in ordered:
   327	            if decision.kind is not task.TerminalKind.TIMEOUT:
   328	                continue
   329	            row = task.active_row_from_snapshot(self._snapshot, decision.task_id)
   330	            if row is None:
   331	                continue
   332	            timeout_rows.append(row)
   333	            if timeout_reason is None:
   334	                timeout_reason = decision.reason
   335	        if timeout_rows and timeout_reason is not None:
   336	            self._cascade_timeouts(timeout_rows, timeout_reason, now_ms)
   337	
   338	        for decision in ordered:
   339	            if decision.kind is task.TerminalKind.PREEMPT:
   340	                self._apply_preempt_decision(decision, now_ms)
   341	            elif decision.kind is task.TerminalKind.UNSCHEDULABLE:
   342	                self._apply_unschedulable_decision(decision)
   343	            # TIMEOUT handled above.
   344	
   345	        self._recompute_and_finalize(now_ms)
   346	        return self.overlay.effects
   347	
   348	    def cancel_job(self, job_id: JobName, reason: str, now: Timestamp) -> ControllerEffects:
   349	        """Cancel ``job_id`` and its full transitive descendant subtree.
   350	
   351	        The subtree is derived from the snapshot's ``job_descendants`` — the
   352	        loader closes it, so the batch reads only the snapshot. Killing every
   353	        job's tasks covers all coscheduled siblings too (siblings always live in
   354	        the same job), so no separate peer cascade is needed: by the time a job's
   355	        tasks are killed, ``find_coscheduled_siblings`` would find none active.
   356	        """
   357	        descendants = self._snapshot.job_descendants.get(job_id)
   358	        if descendants is None:
   359	            return self.overlay.effects
   360	        subtree = [job_id, *descendants.descendants]
   361	        now_ms = now.epoch_ms()
   362	        finished_at = Timestamp.from_ms(now_ms)
   363	
   364	        for jid in subtree:
   365	            is_root = jid == job_id
   366	            job_reason = reason if is_root else PARENT_JOB_TERMINATED_REASON
   367	            action_reason = (
   368	                TaskActionReason.JOB_FINALIZED_TASK_KILLED if is_root else TaskActionReason.PARENT_JOB_TERMINATED
   369	            )
   370	            _kill_non_terminal_tasks(

    37	# ─── Inputs ───

    40	# direct-provider callers can build it without an aggregate cross-import.

    43	
    44	class TerminalKind(StrEnum):
    45	    """Which terminal transition a :class:`TerminalDecision` requests.
    46	
    47	    - ``PREEMPT``: the task should be preempted (retried if budget remains).
    48	    - ``TIMEOUT``: the task should fail without retry.
    49	    - ``UNSCHEDULABLE``: the task can never be placed.
    50	    """
    51	
    52	    PREEMPT = "preempt"
    53	    TIMEOUT = "timeout"
    54	    UNSCHEDULABLE = "unschedulable"
    55	
    56	
    57	class TransitionSource(StrEnum):
    58	    """Caller policy for side effects attached to task-state updates."""
    59	
    60	    WORKER_RECONCILE = "worker_reconcile"
    61	    DISPATCH = "dispatch"
    62	
    63	
    64	@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
    65	class TerminalDecision:
    66	    """One task → terminal assertion to be applied as part of a batch."""
    67	
    68	    kind: TerminalKind

    78	    """Result of one ``apply_one_transition`` call, consumed by batches.py.
    79	
    80	    Carries the information the orchestrator needs to drive cross-aggregate
    81	    cascades (peer cascade, job recompute) without re-reading state.
    82	    """
    83	
    84	    task_id: JobName
    85	    job_id: JobName
    86	    prior_state: int
    87	    new_task_state: int
    88	    cascade_to_peers: bool

    91	# ─── Snapshot lookups ───

    93	
    94	def active_row_from_snapshot(snapshot: TransitionSnapshot, task_id: JobName) -> ActiveTaskRow | None:
    95	    """Resolve the snapshot's active-task row for ``task_id``."""
    96	    task = snapshot.tasks.get(task_id)
    97	    if task is None:
    98	        return None
    99	    rows = snapshot.active_tasks_by_job.get(task.job_id, ())
   100	    for row in rows:
   101	        if row.task_id == task_id:
   102	            return row
   103	    return None
   104	
   105	
   106	# ─── Per-attempt transitions ───
   107	
   108	
   109	def _backfill_attempt_finished_at(state: Overlay, task_id: JobName, attempt_id: int, now_ms: int) -> None:
   110	    """Stamp ``finished_at_ms`` on an attempt whose overlay value is still NULL.
   111	
   112	    Producer-style terminations leave the attempt's ``finished_at_ms`` NULL,
   113	    expecting the worker's next terminal status push to land the timestamp. When
   114	    that push is dropped we backfill it here so the scheduler releases capacity.
   115	    No-op if the overlay already has a ``finished_at`` for the attempt.
   116	    """
   117	    if state.attempt_finished_at(task_id, attempt_id) is not None:
   118	        return
   119	    state.merge_attempt(
   120	        AttemptRowDelta(
   121	            task_id=task_id,
   122	            attempt_id=attempt_id,
   123	            finished_at=Timestamp.from_ms(now_ms),
   124	        )
   125	    )

   127	
   128	def merge_task_termination(
   129	    state: Overlay,
   130	    task_id: str,
   131	    attempt_id: int | None,
   132	    task_state: int,
   133	    error: str | None,
   134	    now_ms: int,
   135	    *,
   136	    stamp_attempt_finished: bool,
   137	    attempt_state: int | None = None,
   138	    exit_code: int | None = None,
   139	) -> None:
   140	    """Move a task to ``task_state`` and record its attempt.
   141	
   142	    ``stamp_attempt_finished`` controls whether the attempt's ``finished_at_ms``
   143	    is stamped: finalizing callers stamp it (the attempt is truly done);
   144	    producer-style terminations leave it NULL so the worker's next terminal
   145	    status update lands the timestamp.
   146	
   147	    No retry counter is carried: both the failure and preemption counts derive
   148	    from the attempt rows this records (see
   149	    ``iris.cluster.controller.attempt_counts``).
   150	
   151	    An already-terminal attempt is left untouched: killing a PENDING task (a
   152	    cancel or a job-failure cascade) must not overwrite the historical outcome of
   153	    its last, already-finished attempt (e.g. rewrite a FAILED attempt to KILLED),
   154	    which would both lose history and, since counts derive from attempt state,
   155	    miscount that terminal as a preemption instead of the failure it was.
   156	    """
   157	    now = Timestamp.from_ms(now_ms)
   158	    task_finished_at = None if task_state in ACTIVE_TASK_STATES or task_state == job_pb2.TASK_STATE_PENDING else now
   159	    effective_attempt_state = attempt_state if attempt_state is not None else task_state
   160	    task_name = JobName.from_wire(task_id)
   161	
   162	    if attempt_id is not None and attempt_id >= 0:
   163	        existing_attempt_state = state.attempt_state(task_name, attempt_id)
   164	        if existing_attempt_state is None or existing_attempt_state not in TERMINAL_TASK_STATES:
   165	            state.merge_attempt(
   166	                AttemptRowDelta(
   167	                    task_id=task_name,
   168	                    attempt_id=attempt_id,
   169	                    state=effective_attempt_state,
   170	                    finished_at=now if stamp_attempt_finished else None,

    60	def with_transport_remat_mode(model: GrugModelConfig) -> GrugModelConfig:
    61	    """Give the ragged transport the layer-carry offload that its scheduling posture depends on.
    62	
    63	    Staging the carry on pinned host lowered the measured HBM peak by 36 GiB, which is what makes
    64	    the latency-hiding scheduler fit. The other transports are unmeasured with the offload, which
    65	    is the only reason they do not take it.
    66	
    67	    The scheduler reads the remat mode, so a launcher that skips this keeps the scheduling
    68	    posture it was measured under, exactly as it did before the mode existed.
    69	    """
    70	    if model.moe_implementation != RAGGED_MOE_IMPLEMENTATION:
    71	        return model
    72	    return dataclasses.replace(model, remat_mode="offload_carry")

     1	# Copyright The Marin Authors
     2	# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
     3	
     4	"""Click bootstrap for experiment ``main``s that defer their artifact versions.
     5	
     6	An experiment's driver constructs a handle graph and then either prints its plan or builds it.
     7	This module makes that a one-liner while wiring up the deferred-version machinery
     8	(:mod:`marin.execution.build_context`):
     9	
    10	- :func:`build_options` is a Click decorator that adds ``--version`` (the run-wide default),
    11	  ``--override NAME=VERSION`` (per-artifact, repeatable), ``--run`` (build vs. print the plan), and
    12	  ``--max-concurrent``. It builds the wrapped function's handle graph **inside** a
    13	  :class:`~marin.execution.build_context.BuildContext` assembled from those options, so any builder
    14	  that deferred its version (omitted ``version=``) resolves against them.
    15	- :func:`experiment_main` wraps a nullary ``build()`` into a ready-to-run command.
    16	
    17	``--version`` is required — there is no silent ``dev`` default, because a deferred *dataset* at a
    18	mutable version rebuilds its (often multi-TB) cache on every run and, unlike a training checkpoint,
    19	is not namespaced per user. To iterate, pass ``--version dev`` explicitly; the printed plan flags
    20	every artifact that resolved to a mutable version and will rebuild.
    21	
    22	An experiment with its own options composes with :func:`build_options` by stacking it *below* the
    23	experiment's own ``@click.option``s and ``@click.command()`` on top::
    24	
    25	    @click.command()
    26	    @click.option("--device", ...)
    27	    @build_options
    28	    def main(device):
    29	        return build(device=device)   # version now deferred to --version / --override
    30	
    31	    if __name__ == "__main__":
    32	        main()
    33	
    34	Construct the whole handle graph inside ``build()``; a builder invoked lazily after it returns has
    35	no active context and raises. Handles are frozen at construction, so reusing one across two
    36	invocations keeps its first resolved version.
    37	"""
    38	
    39	import functools
    40	from collections.abc import Callable, Mapping
    41	from typing import Any
    42	
    43	import click
    44	
    45	from marin.execution.artifact import is_mutable_version, validate_version
    46	from marin.execution.build_context import BuildContext, VersionCodex, build_context
    47	from marin.execution.lazy import ArtifactStep, lower, run
    48	
    49	BuildResult = ArtifactStep | Mapping[str, ArtifactStep] | list[ArtifactStep] | tuple[ArtifactStep, ...]
    50	
    51	
    52	def _version_callback(ctx: click.Context, param: click.Parameter, value: str) -> str:
    53	    """Reject a malformed ``--version`` at parse time, as a Click error rather than a traceback."""
    54	    try:
    55	        validate_version(value)
    56	    except ValueError as e:
    57	        raise click.BadParameter(str(e), ctx=ctx, param=param) from e
    58	    return value
    59	
    60	
    61	def _override_callback(ctx: click.Context, param: click.Parameter, value: tuple[str, ...]) -> dict[str, str]:
    62	    """Parse repeated ``NAME=VERSION`` into a validated ``{name: version}`` map, as Click errors."""
    63	    overrides: dict[str, str] = {}
    64	    for item in value:
    65	        name, sep, version = item.partition("=")
    66	        if not sep or not name:
    67	            raise click.BadParameter(f"{item!r} is not NAME=VERSION", ctx=ctx, param=param)
    68	        if name in overrides:
    69	            raise click.BadParameter(f"duplicate override for {name!r}", ctx=ctx, param=param)
    70	        try:
    71	            validate_version(version)
    72	        except ValueError as e:
    73	            raise click.BadParameter(f"override {name!r}: {e}", ctx=ctx, param=param) from e
    74	        overrides[name] = version
    75	    return overrides
    76	
    77	
    78	def _as_handles(result: BuildResult) -> list[ArtifactStep]:
    79	    """Normalize a ``build()`` return — one handle, a list/tuple, or a name→handle mapping."""
    80	    if isinstance(result, ArtifactStep):
    81	        return [result]
    82	    if isinstance(result, Mapping):
    83	        return list(result.values())
    84	    if isinstance(result, list | tuple):
    85	        return list(result)
    86	    raise TypeError(
    87	        f"build() must return an ArtifactStep, a list/tuple, or a mapping of them, not {type(result).__name__}"
    88	    )
    89	
    90	
    91	def _graph_handles(handles: list[ArtifactStep]) -> list[ArtifactStep]:
    92	    """Every handle reachable from ``handles`` (deps included), deduped by identity, deps first."""
    93	    seen: set[int] = set()
    94	    order: list[ArtifactStep] = []
    95	
    96	    def walk(handle: ArtifactStep) -> None:
    97	        if id(handle) in seen:
    98	            return
    99	        seen.add(id(handle))
   100	        for dep in handle.deps:
   101	            walk(dep)
   102	        order.append(handle)
   103	
   104	    for handle in handles:
   105	        walk(handle)
   106	    return order
   107	
   108	
   109	def _print_plan(handles: list[ArtifactStep]) -> None:
   110	    """Print the lowered plan, then a summary of every artifact's resolved ``name@version``.
   111	
   112	    The summary flags each artifact that resolved to a mutable version inline, since a mutable
   113	    version rebuilds on every run — the thing a driver most wants to catch before ``--run``.
   114	    """
   115	    for handle in handles:
   116	        click.echo(lower(handle))
   117	    click.echo("\nResolved versions:")
   118	    for handle in _graph_handles(handles):
   119	        flag = "  (mutable — rebuilds every run)" if is_mutable_version(handle.version) else ""
   120	        click.echo(f"  {handle.name}@{handle.version}{flag}")
   121	
   122	
   123	def build_options(fn: Callable[..., BuildResult]) -> Callable[..., None]:
   124	    """Add the shared version/run options to a command that returns the experiment's handle(s).
   125	
   126	    ``fn`` returns one :class:`~marin.execution.lazy.ArtifactStep`, a list/tuple of them, or a
   127	    name→handle mapping; it is called inside a :class:`~marin.execution.build_context.BuildContext`
   128	    built from ``--version`` and ``--override``. Without ``--run`` the lowered plan is printed;
   129	    with it the handles are built. An ``--override`` naming an artifact that never deferred (a typo,
   130	    or one that hardcodes its version) is rejected, so a silently-ignored override cannot slip a run
   131	    under the wrong version.
   132	    """
   133	
   134	    @click.option(
   135	        "--version",
   136	        "default_version",
   137	        required=True,
   138	        callback=_version_callback,
   139	        help="Run-wide default version for artifacts that defer (omit version=). "
   140	        "A calendar version YYYY.MM.DD, or 'dev' to iterate.",
   141	    )
   142	    @click.option(
   143	        "--override",
   144	        "overrides",
   145	        multiple=True,
   146	        metavar="NAME=VERSION",
   147	        callback=_override_callback,
   148	        help="Per-artifact version override, keyed by the artifact's name. Repeatable.",
   149	    )
   150	    @click.option("--run", "do_run", is_flag=True, help="Build the handles (default: print the plan).")
   151	    @click.option("--max-concurrent", type=int, default=8, show_default=True, help="Max steps built concurrently.")
   152	    @functools.wraps(fn)
   153	    def wrapper(
   154	        *args: Any, default_version: str, overrides: dict[str, str], do_run: bool, max_concurrent: int, **kwargs: Any
   155	    ) -> None:
   156	        codex = VersionCodex(default=default_version, overrides=overrides)
   157	        with build_context(BuildContext(versions=codex)):
   158	            handles = _as_handles(fn(*args, **kwargs))
   159	        unused = codex.unused_overrides()
   160	        if unused:
   161	            raise click.BadParameter(
   162	                f"never applied to any artifact: {sorted(unused)}. The name is a typo, or that "
   163	                "artifact hardcodes its version (an explicit version wins over an override).",
   164	                param_hint="--override",
   165	            )
   166	        if not do_run:
   167	            _print_plan(handles)
   168	            return
   169	        run(*handles, max_concurrent=max_concurrent)
   170	
   171	    return wrapper
   172	
   173

    42	def aggregate_device_flops() -> float | None:
    43	    """Theoretical peak FLOPs summed over all local JAX devices, or ``None`` if unknown."""
    44	    flops_per_device = device_flops_for_jax_device(jax.devices()[0].device_kind)
    45	    if flops_per_device is None:
    46	        return None
    47	    return flops_per_device * jax.device_count()
    48	
    49	
    50	def compute_instant_throughput(
    51	    batch_size: int,
    52	    step_duration: float,
    53	    tokens_per_example: int,
    54	    flops_per_example: float | None = None,
    55	    theoretical_flops: float | None = None,
    56	) -> InstantThroughput:
    57	    """Compute examples/sec, tokens/sec, model FLOPs/sec, and MFU for one step."""
    58	    if step_duration <= 0.0:
    59	        return InstantThroughput()
    60	    examples_per_second = batch_size / step_duration
    61	    tokens_per_second = tokens_per_example * examples_per_second
    62	    model_flops_per_second = mfu = None
    63	    if flops_per_example is not None:
    64	        model_flops_per_second = flops_per_example / step_duration * batch_size
    65	        if theoretical_flops is not None:
    66	            mfu = model_flops_per_second / theoretical_flops * 100.0
    67	    return InstantThroughput(examples_per_second, tokens_per_second, model_flops_per_second, mfu)
    68

    70	def log_step_info(total_steps: Optional[int], batch_schedule: Optional[BatchSchedule] = None):
    71	    def log_step_info_inner(step: StepInfo):
    72	        metrics = {"train/loss": step.loss, "global_step": step.step}
    73	        if total_steps:
    74	            if batch_schedule is not None:
    75	                total_examples = batch_schedule.global_data_offset_by_step(total_steps)
    76	                current_examples = batch_schedule.global_data_offset_by_step(step.step + 1)
    77	                metrics["run_progress"] = current_examples / total_examples
    78	            else:
    79	                metrics["run_progress"] = step.step / total_steps
    80	        log_optimizer_hyperparams(step.opt_state, step=step.step, prefix="optim")
    81	        levanter.tracker.log(metrics, step=step.step)
    82	
    83	    return log_step_info_inner

    86	def log_performance_stats(
    87	    tokens_per_example: int,
    88	    batch_schedule: int | BatchSchedule,
    89	    flops_per_example: Optional[float] = None,
    90	    prefix: Optional[str] = "throughput",
    91	):
    92	    if isinstance(batch_schedule, int):
    93	        batch_schedule = BatchSchedule(batch_schedule)
    94	
    95	    def wrap_key(key):
    96	        if prefix:
    97	            return f"{prefix}/{key}"
    98	        return key
    99	
   100	    device_count = jax.device_count()
   101	    device = jax.devices()[0]
   102	
   103	    flops_per_device = device_flops_for_jax_device(device.device_kind)
   104	    theoretical_flops = flops_per_device * device_count if flops_per_device is not None else None
   105	    levanter.tracker.log_summary(
   106	        {
   107	            wrap_key("device_kind"): device.device_kind,
   108	        }
   109	    )
   110	
   111	    if flops_per_device is not None:
   112	        levanter.tracker.log_summary(
   113	            {
   114	                wrap_key("theoretical_flops_per_device"): flops_per_device,
   115	                wrap_key("theoretical_flops"): theoretical_flops,
   116	            }
   117	        )
   118	
   119	    if flops_per_example is not None:
   120	        levanter.tracker.log_summary({wrap_key("flops_per_example"): flops_per_example})
   121	
   122	    # Accumulate MFU samples over a trailing window for robust distribution stats.
   123	    # 500 steps is large enough for stable percentiles while bounded in memory.
   124	    mfu_window: collections.deque[float] = collections.deque(maxlen=500)
   125	
   126	    def log_performance_stats(step_info: StepInfo):
   127	        dict_to_log: dict[str, float | int] = {}
   128	
   129	        # log these totals because it's useful for comparing different seqlens, batch sizes, etc
   130	        # TODO: if we add seqlen schedules this will get even more complex
   131	        this_batch_size = batch_schedule.batch_size_at_step(step_info.step)
   132	        total_examples = batch_schedule.global_data_offset_by_step(step_info.step + 1)
   133	        total_tokens = tokens_per_example * total_examples
   134	        dict_to_log["total_tokens"] = total_tokens
   135	
   136	        if flops_per_example:
   137	            total_flops = flops_per_example * total_examples
   138	            dict_to_log["total_gflops"] = total_flops / 1e9
   139	
   140	        throughput = compute_instant_throughput(
   141	            this_batch_size, step_info.step_duration, tokens_per_example, flops_per_example, theoretical_flops
   142	        )
   143	        if throughput.examples_per_second is not None and throughput.tokens_per_second is not None:
   144	            dict_to_log["examples_per_second"] = throughput.examples_per_second
   145	            dict_to_log["tokens_per_second"] = throughput.tokens_per_second
   146	            dict_to_log["duration"] = step_info.step_duration
   147	        if throughput.model_flops_per_second is not None:
   148	            dict_to_log["gflops_per_second"] = throughput.model_flops_per_second / 1e9
   149	        if throughput.mfu is not None:
   150	            dict_to_log["mfu"] = throughput.mfu
   151	            mfu_window.append(throughput.mfu)
   152	
   153	        dict_to_log = {wrap_key(k): v for k, v in dict_to_log.items()}
   154	        levanter.tracker.log(dict_to_log, step=step_info.step)
   155	
   156	        if len(mfu_window) > 0:
   157	            n = len(mfu_window)
   158	            mean = statistics.mean(mfu_window)
   159	            med = statistics.median(mfu_window)
   160	            if n >= 2:
   161	                deciles = statistics.quantiles(mfu_window, n=10)
   162	                p10, p90 = deciles[0], deciles[8]
   163	                sd = statistics.stdev(mfu_window)
   164	            else:
   165	                p10 = p90 = mfu_window[0]
   166	                sd = 0.0
   167	            levanter.tracker.log_summary(
   168	                {
   169	                    wrap_key("p10_mfu"): p10,
   170	                    wrap_key("p50_mfu"): med,
   171	                    wrap_key("p90_mfu"): p90,
   172	                    wrap_key("mean_mfu"): mean,
   173	                    wrap_key("stddev_mfu"): sd,
   174	                    wrap_key("mfu_sample_count"): n,
   175	                }
   176	            )
   177	
   178	    return log_performance_stats

     4	from dataclasses import dataclass
     5	from typing import Any, Callable, Generic, TypeVar
     6	
     7	import jax
     8	from jaxtyping import PyTree
     9	
    10	from levanter.callbacks._core import Callback, LambdaCallback, ProgressEvent, StepInfo
    11	
    12	
    13	S = TypeVar("S")
    14	
    15	
    16	@dataclass(frozen=True)
    17	class CallbackStateView:
    18	    """Minimal state surface expected by generic callback hooks."""
    19	
    20	    step: jax.Array
    21	    model: PyTree
    22	    eval_model: PyTree
    23	    opt_state: PyTree
    24	
    25	
    26	@dataclass(frozen=True)
    27	class _Hook:
    28	    fn: Callback
    29	    every: int
    30	
    31	
    32	class StateCallbackRunner(Generic[S]):
    33	    """Run callback hooks against arbitrary state types via simple accessors."""
    34	
    35	    def __init__(
    36	        self,
    37	        *,
    38	        step_getter: Callable[[S], jax.Array],
    39	        model_getter: Callable[[S], PyTree],
    40	        eval_model_getter: Callable[[S], PyTree],
    41	        opt_state_getter: Callable[[S], PyTree],
    42	    ):
    43	        self._step_getter = step_getter
    44	        self._model_getter = model_getter
    45	        self._eval_model_getter = eval_model_getter
    46	        self._opt_state_getter = opt_state_getter
    47	        self._hooks: list[_Hook] = []
    48	
    49	    def add_hook(self, fn: Callable[[StepInfo], Any] | Callback, *, every: int = 1) -> None:
    50	        if every <= 0:
    51	            raise ValueError(f"Hook interval must be positive, got {every}")
    52	        callback = fn if isinstance(fn, Callback) else LambdaCallback(fn)
    53	        self._hooks.append(_Hook(callback, every))
    54	
    55	    def emit_event(self, event: ProgressEvent) -> None:
    56	        for hook in self._hooks:
    57	            hook.fn.on_event(event)
    58	
    59	    def run(self, state: S, *, loss: float | jax.Array, step_duration: float, force: bool = False) -> None:
    60	        # CallbackStateView is a structural stand-in supplying exactly the attributes StepInfo reads;
    61	        # it deliberately does not subclass TrainerState, so it cannot satisfy StepInfo's S: TrainerState bound.
    62	        # pyrefly: ignore[bad-specialization]
    63	        info = StepInfo(
    64	            state=CallbackStateView(
    65	                step=self._step_getter(state),
    66	                model=self._model_getter(state),
    67	                eval_model=self._eval_model_getter(state),
    68	                opt_state=self._opt_state_getter(state),
    69	            ),
    70	            loss=loss,  # type: ignore[arg-type]
    71	            step_duration=step_duration,
    72	            _event_handler=self.emit_event,
    73	        )
    74	        for hook in self._hooks:
    75	            if force or (info.next_step > 0 and info.next_step % hook.every == 0):
    76	                hook.fn.on_step(info, force=force)

     4	"""Per-user namespacing for training artifacts.
     5	
     6	A mutable (``dev``) checkpoint addresses identity on ``{name}/dev`` alone, so two people
     7	iterating on the same experiment write to the same path and clobber each other — and a
     8	resumption checkpointer would resume from whichever run last touched it.
     9	:func:`user_owned_name` prefixes an artifact unconditionally when its output is
    10	owned by the caller. :func:`user_namespaced_name` applies that prefix only to
    11	mutable training steps. Fixed checkpoints and datasets otherwise keep their
    12	shared names, so published runs stay citable and tokenized caches remain shared.
    13	"""
    14	
    15	from rigging.provenance import username_segment
    16	
    17	from marin.execution.artifact import is_mutable_version
    18	
    19	
    20	def user_owned_name(name: str) -> str:
    21	    """Return ``users/{username}/{name}`` for a user-owned artifact."""
    22	    return f"users/{username_segment()}/{name}"
    23	
    24	
    25	def user_namespaced_name(name: str, version: str) -> str:
    26	    """Return ``users/{username}/{name}`` for a mutable version, ``name`` unchanged otherwise.
    27	
    28	    A fixed (calendar) ``version`` stays in the shared namespace; a mutable
    29	    ``dev``/``<label>-dev`` version is isolated per user. Raises if no username resolves, so a
    30	    dev run never silently lands in a shared ``users/unknown/`` bucket.
    31	    """
    32	    if not is_mutable_version(version):
    33	        return name
    34	    return user_owned_name(name)
   171	class ArtifactStep(Generic[T]):
   172	    """A lazy, content-free handle to a versioned artifact and how to build it.
   173	
   174	    ``run(build_config(ctx))`` produces the artifact: ``build_config`` assembles the config from
   175	    the :class:`StepContext` (literals bear identity; pulled values do not), and ``run`` is the
   176	    plain callable that does the work, returning the produced ``artifact_type`` (or ``None`` for a
   177	    data step that writes to ``ctx.output_path``). ``deps`` are the handles this step builds on;
   178	    pass the same handles ``build_config`` reads via ``ctx.artifact_path`` so they materialize
   179	    first.
   180	    """
   181	
   182	    name: str
   183	    version: str
   184	    artifact_type: type[T]
   185	    run: Callable[[Any], "Artifact | None"]
   186	    build_config: Callable[[StepContext], Any]
   187	    deps: tuple["ArtifactStep", ...] = ()
   188	    runtime_args: Mapping[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
   189	    """Execution choices the config pulls via ``ctx.runtime_arg(key)``. Excluded from the
   190	    fingerprint, so changing one never forks identity."""
   191	    override_path: str | None = None
   192	    """Pin the artifact to an existing location instead of ``{name}/{version}``. A relative
   193	    pin is resolved against the prefix; an absolute one is used as-is. Writes no record.
   194	    Mutually exclusive with ``adopt_source``."""
   195	    adopt_source: str | None = None
   196	    """Adopt pre-existing data at this location as a managed ``name@version`` (set via
   197	    :meth:`ArtifactStep.adopt`). Consumers resolve to ``adopt_source``; running it writes a provenance
   198	    record at the canonical address. Mutually exclusive with ``override_path``."""
   199	    adopt_config: dict | None = None
   200	    """For an adopted artifact, the synthetic ``config`` to record (e.g. a tokenized cache's
   201	    tokenizer/format), so consumers read metadata the same way as for a produced artifact."""
   202	    expected_fingerprint: str | None = None
   203	    """Opt-in hard pin: when set, :func:`lower` raises :class:`FingerprintMismatchError` if the
   204	    config now fingerprints to something else, and the drift check raises rather than warns. Leave
   205	    ``None`` to opt out."""
   206	
   207	    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
   208	        validate_path_segment("name", self.name)
   209	        validate_version(self.version)
   210	        if self.adopt_source is not None and self.override_path is not None:
   211	            raise ValueError(f"{self.name}@{self.version}: an artifact cannot be both adopted and pinned")
   212	
   213	    def fingerprint_payload(self) -> str:
   214	        """The canonical config bytes this handle's fingerprint hashes. For a computed
   215	        artifact, the config built with dep versions for paths and placeholders for pulled
   216	        values; for an adopted one, its source location and recorded config."""
   217	        if self.adopt_source is not None:
   218	            return json.dumps({"adopt_source": self.adopt_source, "config": self.adopt_config}, sort_keys=True)
   219	        config = self.build_config(StepContext.for_fingerprint(self.runtime_args.keys(), self.deps))
   220	        return canonical_json(config)
   221	
   222	    def fingerprint(self) -> str:
   223	        """Stable id of *how* this artifact is built. Changes iff a config value or a dep
   224	        version changes; independent of the storage prefix/region."""
   225	        return fingerprint_hash(self.fingerprint_payload())
   226	
   227	    def path(self, prefix: str | None = None) -> str:
   228	        resolved_prefix = prefix or marin_prefix()
   229	        # An adopted artifact resolves to its source; a pin to its location; else the
   230	        # canonical name@version address.
   231	        location = self.adopt_source if self.adopt_source is not None else self.override_path
   232	        if location is not None:
   233	            if _is_relative_path(location):
   234	                return prefix_join(resolved_prefix, location)
   235	            return location
   236	        # The explicit, hash-free ``{prefix}/{name}/{version}`` address.
   237	        return prefix_join(resolved_prefix, f"{self.name}/{self.version}")
   238	
   239	    def lower(self) -> StepSpec:
   240	        """Lower this handle graph into a ``StepSpec`` graph the ``StepRunner`` can run.
   241	
   242	        A pure structural transform (no execution): captures provenance once and threads it
   243	        through the graph. See :func:`_lower`.
   244	        """
   245	        return _lower(self, Provenance.capture())
   246	
   247	    @classmethod
   248	    def adopt(
   249	        cls,
   250	        name: str,
   251	        version: str,
   252	        source: str,
   253	        *,
   254	        kind: type[T] = Artifact,  # pyrefly: ignore[bad-function-definition]
   255	        config: dict | None = None,
   285	    Resolves ``ctx.output_path`` / ``ctx.artifact_path(dep)`` against ``prefix`` and passes the
   286	    handle's real ``runtime_args`` (so a ``build_config`` that reads ``ctx.runtime_arg(...)`` does
   287	    not ``KeyError``) without running anything. Region is ``None``, so this is not identical to the
   288	    run-time config, which also binds a real region.
   289	    """
   290	    return handle.build_config(
   291	        StepContext.for_run(
   292	            output_path=handle.path(prefix), prefix=prefix, runtime_args=handle.runtime_args, deps=handle.deps
   293	        )
   294	    )
   295	
   296	
   297	def _output_path_spec(handle: "ArtifactStep") -> str:
   298	    """The relative output path StepSpec should use: the pin if set, else ``{name}/{version}``."""
   299	    return handle.override_path or f"{handle.name}/{handle.version}"
   300	
   301	
   302	def _lower(handle: "ArtifactStep", provenance: Provenance, memo: dict[int, StepSpec] | None = None) -> StepSpec:
   303	    """Lower a handle graph into a ``StepSpec`` graph, recording ``provenance`` on every step.
   304	
   305	    Each handle becomes a step addressed by its explicit ``{name}/{version}`` (or its pin); the
   306	    fingerprint, version, artifact type, and dep identities travel in ``hash_attrs`` so the runner
   307	    applies the drift check before serving a cached output, and the step fn writes a full
   308	    :class:`ArtifactRecord` on success (unless pinned). Pure transform of *structure* — it never
   309	    inspects ``handle.run``.
   310	
   311	    ``memo`` caches the spec of each handle by identity, so a handle several consumers share is
   312	    lowered once. Without it a diamond re-walks the shared subgraph — and re-runs its
   313	    ``build_config`` — once per path through it. Pass one ``memo`` across a whole ``run`` to share
   314	    it between terminals.
   315	
   316	    Raises :class:`FingerprintMismatchError` if ``expected_fingerprint`` is set and differs;
   317	    :class:`ValueError` if a fixed (non-``dev``) handle has a ``dev`` dep.
   318	    """
   319	    memo = {} if memo is None else memo
   320	    cached = memo.get(id(handle))
   321	    if cached is not None:
   322	        return cached
   323	    dep_specs = [_lower(dep, provenance, memo) for dep in handle.deps]
   324	    payload = handle.fingerprint_payload()
   325	    fingerprint = fingerprint_hash(payload)
   326	    if handle.expected_fingerprint is not None and fingerprint != handle.expected_fingerprint:
   327	        raise FingerprintMismatchError(
   328	            f"{handle.name}@{handle.version}: config fingerprint is {fingerprint}, but "
   329	            f"expected_fingerprint pins {handle.expected_fingerprint}. The recipe changed — update "
   330	            f"the pin, and bump the version if this is meant to be a different artifact."
   331	        )
   332	
   333	    if not is_mutable_version(handle.version):
   334	        for dep in handle.deps:
   335	            if is_mutable_version(dep.version):
   336	                raise ValueError(
   337	                    f"{handle.name}@{handle.version} is a fixed version but depends on mutable "
   338	                    f"{dep.name}@{dep.version}; a mutable dep would rebuild while the fixed parent stays "
   339	                    "cached. Make the parent dev, or pin the dep."
   340	                )
   341	
   342	    dep_refs = [f"{d.name}@{d.version}" for d in handle.deps]
   343	    rt_name = result_type_name(handle.artifact_type)
   344	    # A pin references existing data; it writes no record. Everything else records provenance.
   345	    record_provenance = handle.override_path is None
   346	
   347	    def fn(output_path: str, _handle: "ArtifactStep" = handle) -> Any:
   348	        if _handle.adopt_source is not None:
   349	            # Adoption registers pre-existing data: no compute, the record points at it.
   350	            source = _handle.path(marin_prefix())
   351	            fs = url_to_fs(source, use_listings_cache=False)[0]
   352	            if not fs.exists(source):
   353	                raise FileNotFoundError(f"cannot adopt {_handle.name}@{_handle.version}: no data at {source}")
   354	            result = None
   355	            config_json: dict[str, Any] | None = _handle.adopt_config
   356	            result_json: dict[str, Any] | None = None
   357	        else:
   358	            ctx = StepContext.for_run(
   359	                output_path=output_path,
   360	                prefix=marin_prefix(),
   361	                region=marin_region(),
   362	                runtime_args=_handle.runtime_args,
   363	                deps=_handle.deps,
   364	            )
   365	            config = _handle.build_config(ctx)
   366	            result = _handle.run(config)
   367	            source = None
   368	            config_json = json.loads(canonical_json(config))
   369	            result_json = result.result_payload() if isinstance(result, Artifact) else None
   370	        if record_provenance:
   371	            write_record(
   372	                ArtifactRecord(
   373	                    name=_handle.name,
   374	                    version=_handle.version,
   375	                    fingerprint=fingerprint,
   376	                    result_type=rt_name,
   377	                    output_path=output_path,
   378	                    deps=dep_refs,
   379	                    config=config_json,
   380	                    source=source,
   381	                    result=result_json,
   382	                    fingerprint_payload=payload,
   383	                    provenance=provenance,
   384	                )
   385	            )
   386	        return result
   387	
   388	    hash_attrs: dict[str, Any] = {
   389	        FINGERPRINT_KEY: fingerprint,
   390	        VERSION_KEY: handle.version,
   391	        RESULT_TYPE_KEY: rt_name,
   392	        "deps": dep_refs,
   393	    }
   394	    if handle.expected_fingerprint is not None:
   395	        hash_attrs[EXPECTED_FINGERPRINT_KEY] = handle.expected_fingerprint

   317	    eval_ema: bool = True
   318	    compute_bpb: bool = True
   319	    # For expert-parallel runs, also evaluate under the dropless local backend on an
   320	    # expert-collapsed mesh, logging a separate `eval_dropless` macro loss alongside the
   321	    # as-trained (with-drop) eval. No-op when the mesh has no expert parallelism.

   331	@dataclass(frozen=True)
   332	class GrugRunConfig:
   333	    """Top-level config for grug training."""
   334	
   335	    model: GrugModelConfig
   336	    data: LmDataConfig
   337	    resources: ResourceConfig
   338	    tensorstore_cache_bytes: int | None = None
   339	    optimizer: OptimizerConfig = field(default_factory=AdamConfig)
   340	    trainer: GrugTrainerConfig = field(default_factory=GrugTrainerConfig)
   341	    eval: GrugEvalConfig | None = field(default_factory=GrugEvalConfig)
   342	    # Stop after this many steps while `trainer.num_train_steps` still sizes the learning-rate
   343	    # schedule. Warmup and decay are fractions of `num_train_steps`, so training the head of a
   344	    # long schedule requires the two to differ. None runs the whole schedule.
   345	    stop_after_steps: int | None = None
   346	    # GPU processes per task: > 1 runs one JAX process per GPU (multi-controller)
   347	    # via the iris.hooks.multigpu_main supervisor instead of one process per node.
   348	    processes_per_task: int = 1
   349	    # Retry budgets for the training job. The two are separate gates and the job fails when either
   350	    # one trips, thus raise them together. The defaults make a failure terminal, which is what a run
   351	    # that cannot resume wants: a retry would repeat it from step 0. Only a run that both saves and
   352	    # restores checkpoints benefits from a deep budget.
   353	    max_retries_failure: int = 0
   354	    max_task_failures: int = 10

   356	
   357	def build_train_dataset(
   358	    data_config: LmDataConfig,
   359	    *,
   360	    max_seq_len: int,
   361	    batch_schedule: BatchSchedule,
   362	    key: PRNGKeyArray,
   363	) -> MixtureDataset[GrugLmExample]:
   364	    pos = Axis("position", max_seq_len)
   365	    mix_key, shuffle_key = jax.random.split(key)
   366	    weights = data_config.train_weights
   367	    if isinstance(weights, list):
   368	        weights = rescale_mixture_schedule_for_batch_schedule(weights, batch_schedule)
   369	
   370	    initial_batch_size = batch_schedule.batch_size_at_step(0)
   371	    datasets = data_config.train_sets(pos, key=shuffle_key, initial_batch_size=initial_batch_size)
   372	    return MixtureDataset(
   373	        datasets=datasets,
   374	        weights=weights,
   375	        stop_strategy=data_config.stop_strategy,
   376	        key=mix_key,
   377	        block_size=data_config.mixture_block_size,
   378	    )
