     1	# Copyright The Levanter Authors
     2	# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
     3	
     4	"""Terminate training after a step or the surrounding pipeline stops progressing."""
     5	
     6	import logging
     7	import os
     8	import threading
     9	from dataclasses import dataclass
    10	from datetime import timedelta
    11	from time import monotonic
    12	from typing import Any, Callable
    13	
    14	from levanter.callbacks._core import Callback, ProgressEvent, StepInfo
    15	
    16	
    17	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    18	
    19	_WATCHDOG_POLL_INTERVAL = 60.0
    20	_DEFAULT_STARTUP_GRACE_PERIOD = timedelta(hours=1)
    21	STALLED_TRAINING_EXIT_CODE = 124
    22	
    23	
    24	@dataclass(frozen=True)
    25	class ProgressTimeout:
    26	    """Progress deadline that triggered watchdog termination."""
    27	
    28	    event: ProgressEvent
    29	    elapsed: float
    30	    timeout: float
    31	
    32	
    33	class ProgressWatchdog(Callback[Any]):
    34	    """Watch explicit training lifecycle events with step and process deadlines."""
    35	
    36	    def __init__(
    37	        self,
    38	        *,
    39	        step_timeout: timedelta | None,
    40	        process_timeout: timedelta | None,
    41	        startup_timeout: timedelta | None = None,
    42	        startup_grace_period: timedelta = _DEFAULT_STARTUP_GRACE_PERIOD,
    43	        diagnostic: Callable[[ProgressTimeout], None] | None = None,
    44	        diagnostic_timeout: timedelta | None = None,
    45	        poll_interval: float = _WATCHDOG_POLL_INTERVAL,
    46	    ) -> None:
    47	        if step_timeout is None and process_timeout is None and startup_timeout is None:
    48	            raise ValueError("at least one watchdog timeout must be set")
    49	        if startup_timeout is not None and startup_timeout.total_seconds() <= 0:
    50	            raise ValueError("startup_timeout must be positive")
    51	        if step_timeout is not None and step_timeout.total_seconds() <= 0:
    52	            raise ValueError("step_timeout must be positive")
    53	        if process_timeout is not None and process_timeout.total_seconds() <= 0:
    54	            raise ValueError("process_timeout must be positive")
    55	        if startup_grace_period.total_seconds() < 0:
    56	            raise ValueError("startup_grace_period must be non-negative")
    57	        if diagnostic is not None and diagnostic_timeout is None:
    58	            raise ValueError("diagnostic_timeout is required when diagnostic is set")
    59	        if diagnostic_timeout is not None and diagnostic_timeout.total_seconds() <= 0:
    60	            raise ValueError("diagnostic_timeout must be positive")
    61	        if poll_interval <= 0:
    62	            raise ValueError("poll_interval must be positive")
    63	
    64	        self._startup_timeout = startup_timeout.total_seconds() if startup_timeout is not None else None
    65	        self._step_timeout = step_timeout.total_seconds() if step_timeout is not None else None
    66	        self._process_timeout = process_timeout.total_seconds() if process_timeout is not None else None
    67	        self._startup_grace_period = startup_grace_period.total_seconds()
    68	        self._diagnostic = diagnostic
    69	        self._diagnostic_timeout = diagnostic_timeout.total_seconds() if diagnostic_timeout is not None else None
    70	        self._poll_interval = poll_interval
    71	        self._lock = threading.Lock()
    72	        self._created_at = monotonic()
    73	        self._completed_training_step = False
    74	        self._training_started_at: float | None = None
    75	        self._active_step_started_at: float | None = None
    76	        self._last_progress: tuple[ProgressEvent, float] | None = None
    77	        self._stop = threading.Event()
    78	        self._thread = threading.Thread(target=self._run, name="levanter-progress-watchdog", daemon=True)
    79	        self._thread.start()
    80	
    81	    def on_step(self, info: StepInfo[Any], force: bool = False) -> None:
    82	        del info, force
    83	
    84	    def on_event(self, event: ProgressEvent) -> None:
    85	        if event is ProgressEvent.TRAINING_FINISHED:
    86	            self.stop()
    87	            return
    88	
    89	        now = monotonic()
    90	        with self._lock:
    91	            if self._stop.is_set():
    92	                return
    93	
    94	            if event is ProgressEvent.TRAIN_STEP_STARTED and self._training_started_at is None:
    95	                self._training_started_at = now
    96	
    97	            if event is ProgressEvent.TRAIN_STEP_STARTED and self._completed_training_step:
    98	                self._active_step_started_at = now
    99	            else:
   100	                self._active_step_started_at = None
   101	
   102	            if event is ProgressEvent.TRAIN_STEP_FINISHED:
   103	                self._completed_training_step = True
   104	
   105	            if self._completed_training_step:
   106	                self._last_progress = (event, now)
   107	
   108	    def stop(self) -> None:
   109	        self._stop.set()
   110	        self._thread.join(timeout=self._poll_interval * 2)
   111	
   112	    def _run(self) -> None:
   113	        while not self._stop.wait(self._poll_interval):
   114	            now = monotonic()
   115	            with self._lock:
   116	                completed_training_step = self._completed_training_step
   117	                training_started_at = self._training_started_at
   118	                active_step_started_at = self._active_step_started_at
   119	                last_progress = self._last_progress
   120	
   121	            if not completed_training_step:
   122	                # No step has completed, so the process is still restoring, building caches, or
   123	                # compiling its first step. Elapsed time is all that bounds this: the step deadline
   124	                # stays unarmed until a step completes, and the process deadline has no progress
   125	                # event to measure from.
   126	                if self._startup_timeout is not None and now - self._created_at >= self._startup_timeout:
   127	                    self._terminate(ProgressEvent.PROCESS_STARTED, now - self._created_at, self._startup_timeout)
   128	                    return
   129	                continue
   130	
   131	            if training_started_at is None or now - training_started_at < self._startup_grace_period:
   132	                continue
   133	
   134	            if (
   135	                active_step_started_at is not None
   136	                and self._step_timeout is not None
   137	                and now - active_step_started_at >= self._step_timeout
   138	            ):
   139	                self._terminate(ProgressEvent.TRAIN_STEP_STARTED, now - active_step_started_at, self._step_timeout)
   140	                return
   141	
   142	            if last_progress is not None and self._process_timeout is not None:
   143	                event, event_time = last_progress
   144	                if now - event_time >= self._process_timeout:
   145	                    self._terminate(event, now - event_time, self._process_timeout)
   146	                    return
   147	
   148	    def _terminate(self, event: ProgressEvent, elapsed: float, timeout: float) -> None:
   149	        self._stop.set()
   150	        try:
   151	            logger.critical(
   152	                "No progress after %s for %.1f seconds (timeout %.1f); terminating with exit code %d",
   153	                event.value,
   154	                elapsed,
   155	                timeout,
   156	                STALLED_TRAINING_EXIT_CODE,
   157	            )
   158	            if self._diagnostic is not None:
   159	                assert self._diagnostic_timeout is not None
   160	                diagnostic = threading.Thread(
   161	                    target=self._run_diagnostic,
   162	                    args=(ProgressTimeout(event, elapsed, timeout),),
   163	                    name="levanter-progress-diagnostic",
   164	                    daemon=True,
   165	                )
   166	                diagnostic.start()
   167	                diagnostic.join(self._diagnostic_timeout)
   168	                if diagnostic.is_alive():
   169	                    logger.error("Progress diagnostic exceeded its %.1f second budget", self._diagnostic_timeout)
   170	        finally:
   171	            os._exit(STALLED_TRAINING_EXIT_CODE)
   172	
   173	    def _run_diagnostic(self, timeout: ProgressTimeout) -> None:
   174	        try:
   175	            assert self._diagnostic is not None
   176	            self._diagnostic(timeout)
   177	        except Exception:
   178	            logger.exception("Progress diagnostic failed")
   179	
   180	
   181	@dataclass(frozen=True)
   182	class ProgressWatchdogConfig:
   183	    """Configure the startup grace period and training progress deadlines."""
   184	
   185	    step_timeout: timedelta | None = None
   186	    process_timeout: timedelta | None = None
   187	    startup_timeout: timedelta | None = None
   188	    startup_grace_period: timedelta = _DEFAULT_STARTUP_GRACE_PERIOD
   189	    diagnostic_timeout: timedelta | None = None
   190	
   191	    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
   192	        if self.step_timeout is not None and self.step_timeout.total_seconds() <= 0:
   193	            raise ValueError("step_timeout must be positive")
   194	        if self.startup_timeout is not None and self.startup_timeout.total_seconds() <= 0:
   195	            raise ValueError("startup_timeout must be positive")
   196	        if self.process_timeout is not None and self.process_timeout.total_seconds() <= 0:
   197	            raise ValueError("process_timeout must be positive")
   198	        if self.startup_grace_period.total_seconds() < 0:
   199	            raise ValueError("startup_grace_period must be non-negative")
   200	        if self.diagnostic_timeout is not None and self.diagnostic_timeout.total_seconds() <= 0:
   201	            raise ValueError("diagnostic_timeout must be positive")
   202	
   203	    def create(
   204	        self,
   205	        *,
   206	        process_index: int = 0,
   207	        diagnostic: Callable[[ProgressTimeout], None] | None = None,
   208	    ) -> ProgressWatchdog | None:
   209	        if self.step_timeout is None and self.process_timeout is None and self.startup_timeout is None:
   210	            return None
   211	        if self.diagnostic_timeout is not None and process_index != 0:
   212	            return None
   213	        if self.diagnostic_timeout is not None and diagnostic is None:
   214	            raise ValueError("diagnostic is required when diagnostic_timeout is set")
   215	        return ProgressWatchdog(
   216	            step_timeout=self.step_timeout,
   217	            process_timeout=self.process_timeout,
   218	            startup_timeout=self.startup_timeout,
   219	            startup_grace_period=self.startup_grace_period,
   220	            diagnostic=diagnostic if self.diagnostic_timeout is not None else None,
   221	            diagnostic_timeout=self.diagnostic_timeout,
   222	        )
   850	
   851	@dataclass
   852	class TrainerConfig:
   853	    seed: int = 0  # random seed
   854	    mp: jmp.Policy = jmp.get_policy("f32")  # mixed precision policy
   855	    quantization: Optional[QuantizationConfig] = None
   856	    model_averaging: ModelAveragingConfig | None = None
   857	
   858	    wandb: Optional[WandbConfig] = None
   859	    log_dir: Path = Path("logs/")
   860	    id: Optional[str] = None  # run id. if None, will be set to a random string
   861	
   862	    tracker: TrackerConfig | Tuple[TrackerConfig, ...] = field(default_factory=WandbConfig)
   863	    watch: WatchConfig = WatchConfig()
   864	    profiler: ProfilerConfig = ProfilerConfig()
   865	    progress_watchdog: ProgressWatchdogConfig = ProgressWatchdogConfig()
   866	    """Optional deadlines for training-step and whole-process progress events."""
   867	
   868	    log_jaxprs: bool = True
   869	    """Whether to log the jaxpr of the training step. This is useful for debugging and understanding the model."""
   870	    log_xla_hlo: bool = True
   871	    """Whether to log the XLA HLO of the training step. This is useful for debugging and understanding the model."""
   872	
   873	    # helpful checks
   874	    crash_on_nan: bool = True
   875	    crash_on_inf: bool = True
   876	
   877	    # config related to partitioning
   878	    mesh: MeshConfig = MeshConfig()
   879	    use_explicit_mesh_axes: bool = False
   880	    """If True, build the device mesh with `AxisType.Explicit` axes.
   881	
   882	    This is required for code paths that call `jax.sharding.reshard(..., PartitionSpec(...))`,
   883	    because JAX disallows using named `PartitionSpec`s under `AxisType.Auto`/`AxisType.Manual` meshes.
   884	    """
   885	
   886	    @property
   887	    def batch_axis_name(self) -> str | None:
   888	        return self.mesh.batch_axis_name
   889	
   890	    # Config related to batch sizes
   891	    train_batch_size: int | IntSchedule = 512
   892	    per_device_parallelism: int = -1
   893	    """how many examples to process in parallel on each device. -1 (default) means train_batch_size/num_devices"""
   894	
   895	    per_device_eval_parallelism: int = -1
   896	    """how many examples to process in parallel on each device. -1 (default) means same as per_device_parallelism"""
   897	
   898	    allow_nondivisible_batch_size: bool = False
   899	    """
   900	    Allow batch sizes to be non-divisible by the number of devices (or data axis size).
   901	
   902	    This is typically used when you want a specific batch size but have a weird number of devices.
   903	    """
   904	
   905	    # Config related to duration
   906	    num_train_steps: int = 400_000  # number of training steps
   907	    steps_per_eval: int = 1_000  # how often to evaluate
   908	    max_eval_batches: Optional[int] = None  # max number of batches to evaluate on. None means all batches
   909	
   910	    checkpointer: CheckpointerConfig = field(default_factory=CheckpointerConfig)
   911	    load_checkpoint: Optional[bool] = None
   912	    """if None (default), we'll load a checkpoint if it exists. If true, we must load a checkpoint"""
   913	    load_checkpoint_path: Optional[str | list[str]] = None
   914	    """One checkpoint root/path, or ordered roots searched for the newest checkpoint.
   915	
   916	    If None, search the checkpointer's permanent and temporary roots.
   917	    """
   918	
   919	    def checkpoint_search_paths(self, run_id: str) -> list[str]:
   920	        if isinstance(self.load_checkpoint_path, str):
   921	            return [self.load_checkpoint_path]
   922	        if self.load_checkpoint_path is not None:
   923	            return list(self.load_checkpoint_path)
   924	
   925	        paths = [self.checkpointer.expanded_path(run_id)]
   926	        temp_path = self.checkpointer.expanded_temporary_path(run_id)
   927	        if temp_path is not None:
   928	            paths.append(temp_path)
   929	        return paths
   930

   932	    """Load and continue training from a checkpoint. If None, will initialize from model_init."""
   933	    allow_partial_checkpoint: bool = False
   934	    """If True, we allow loading a checkpoint that doesn't have all the parameters in the model.
   935	        Missing parameters are initialized from the model_init function."""
   936	
   937	    jax_config: Mapping[str, JsonAtom] = field(
   938	        default_factory=lambda: copy.deepcopy(DEFAULT_JAX_CONFIG)
   939	    )  # config to pass to jax.config.update
   940	    jax_compilation_cache_dir: Optional[str] = None
   941	
   942	    distributed: DistributedConfig = DistributedConfig()
   943	
   944	    # whether or not to require an accelerator (e.g. TPU or GPU).
   945	    # default depends on the platform: on macos False, else True

   500	    data_config: LmDataConfig,
   501	    max_seq_len: int,
   502	    mesh: Mesh,
   503	    eval_cfg: GrugEvalConfig,
   504	    mp: jmp.Policy,
   505	    model_transform: Callable[[Transformer], Transformer] | None = None,
   506	) -> TaggedEvaluator[LmExample | GrugLmExample, Transformer] | None:
   507	    pos = Axis("position", max_seq_len)
   508	    tagged_eval_sets = data_config.tagged_eval_sets(pos)
   509	    if len(tagged_eval_sets) == 0:
   510	        logger.warning("No evaluation datasets provided.")
   511	        return None
   512	
   513	    max_examples_per_dataset = None
   514	    if eval_cfg.max_eval_batches is not None:
   515	        max_examples_per_dataset = eval_cfg.max_eval_batches * eval_cfg.eval_batch_size
   516	
   517	    tokenizer = data_config.the_tokenizer if eval_cfg.compute_bpb else None
   518	    # `compact_grug_mesh` always carries (replica_dcn, data, expert, model); length-1 axes

   523	
   524	    def eval_loss_fn(model: Transformer, batch: LmExample | GrugLmExample) -> tuple[jax.Array, jax.Array, jax.Array]:
   525	        # Evaluate at the compute dtype, as the train step does at `mp.cast_to_compute(params)`.
   526	        # Parameters are stored float32, and `gpu_fa4_cute` accepts only bf16/fp16, so without this
   527	        # every eval raises `TypeError: ... supports only bf16/fp16, got float32` on Blackwell. The
   528	        # reference attention path takes float32, which hid this on H100.
   529	        model = mp.cast_to_compute(model)
   530	        if model_transform is not None:
   531	            model = model_transform(model)
   532	        if isinstance(batch, LmExample):
   533	            batch = grug_lm_example_from_named(batch)
   534	        per_pos_loss = model.next_token_loss(
   535	            batch.tokens,
   536	            batch.loss_weight,
   537	            mask=batch.attn_mask,
   538	            reduction="none",
   539	            logsumexp_weight=None,
   540	        )
   541	        per_pos_loss = jax.sharding.reshard(per_pos_loss, eval_array_sharding)
   542	        per_pos_weight = jax.sharding.reshard(batch.loss_weight, eval_array_sharding)
   543	        per_pos_token_id = jnp.pad(batch.tokens[:, 1:], ((0, 0), (0, 1)))
   544	        return per_pos_loss, per_pos_weight, per_pos_token_id
   545	
   546	    return TaggedEvaluator(
   547	        EvalBatch=eval_batch,
   548	        tagged_eval_sets=tagged_eval_sets,
   549	        loss_fn=eval_loss_fn,
   550	        tokenizer=tokenizer,
   551	        device_mesh=mesh,
   552	        axis_mapping=eval_axis_mapping,
   553	        max_examples_per_dataset=max_examples_per_dataset,
   554	    )

   556	
   557	def _compute_flops(
   558	    *,
   559	    model_config: GrugModelConfig,
   560	) -> tuple[float, dict[str, float]]:
   561	    flops_per_token = lm_flops_per_token(
   562	        hidden_dim=model_config.hidden_dim,
   563	        intermediate_dim=model_config.intermediate_dim,
   564	        shared_intermediate_dim=model_config.shared_expert_intermediate_dim,
   565	        num_layers=model_config.num_layers,
   566	        num_kv_heads=model_config.num_kv_heads,
   567	        num_heads=model_config.num_heads,
   568	        seq_len=model_config.max_seq_len,
   569	        vocab_size=model_config.vocab_size,
   570	        glu=True,
   571	        num_experts=model_config.num_experts,
   572	        num_shared_experts=model_config.num_shared_experts if model_config.shared_expert_intermediate_dim > 0 else 0,
   573	        num_experts_per_tok=model_config.num_experts_per_token,
   574	        sliding_window=model_config.sliding_window,
   575	        global_every=model_config.global_every,
   576	        local_kv_heads=model_config.local_kv_heads,
   577	        global_kv_heads=model_config.global_kv_heads,
   578	    )
   579	    # `lm_flops_per_token` prices every matmul at `hidden_dim`. Under LatentMoE the routed experts
   580	    # live at `latent_dim` instead, and two projections are added per layer, so correct both terms
   581	    # or MFU is overstated by roughly the compression ratio.
   582	    if model_config.latent_dim is not None:
   583	        latent, hidden = model_config.latent_dim, model_config.hidden_dim
   584	        # Matches the routed term in `lm_flops_per_token`: 2 * 3 * width * intermediate * top_k.
   585	        routed_delta = 2 * 3 * model_config.intermediate_dim * model_config.num_experts_per_token * (latent - hidden)
   586	        # W_down (hidden -> latent) and W_up (latent -> hidden), once per token each.
   587	        projection = 2 * 2 * hidden * latent
   588	        flops_per_token += model_config.num_layers * (routed_delta + projection)
   589	
   590	    flops_per_example = 3 * flops_per_token * model_config.max_seq_len
   591	
   592	    flops_summary: dict[str, float] = {
   593	        "throughput/flops_per_token_analytic": flops_per_token,
   594	        "throughput/flops_per_example_analytic": flops_per_example,
   595	    }
   596	
   597	    return flops_per_example, flops_summary
   598	
   599	
   600	def log_device_memory(step_info) -> None:
   601	    """Log this process's local-device HBM peak, live bytes, and allocator limit in GiB.
   602	
   603	    The EP hero had no peak-HBM telemetry, which makes a whole class of result unreadable: XLA's
   604	    ``HloRematerialization`` engages only when peak crosses the allocator limit, so a config change
   605	    that moves peak across that boundary produces an MFU step change that has nothing to do with the
   606	    change itself. Issue #8054 traced its own +9.08% headline to exactly this -- 3.69 GiB of peak
   607	    took it under the limit and switched remat off -- and the win fell to +3.31% once one process
   608	    per GPU put 8.79 GiB back. Any ablation that moves activation memory needs this logged, or its
   609	    rungs cannot be told apart from allocator-limit crossings.
   610	
   611	    Ported from ``experiments/grug/moe_hero_fsdp/train.py``.
   612	    """
   613	    stats = jax.local_devices()[0].memory_stats()
   614	    levanter.tracker.log(
   615	        {
   616	            "memory/peak_gib": stats["peak_bytes_in_use"] / 1024**3,
   617	            "memory/in_use_gib": stats["bytes_in_use"] / 1024**3,
   618	            "memory/limit_gib": stats["bytes_limit"] / 1024**3,
   619	        },
   620	        step=step_info.step,
   621	    )
   622	
   623	
   624	def _make_mixture_stage_callback(train_dataset: MixtureDataset, batch_schedule: BatchSchedule):
   625	    last_mixture_stage = -1
   626	
   627	    def log_mixture_stage(step_info):
   628	        nonlocal last_mixture_stage
   629	        seq_index = batch_schedule.global_data_offset_by_step(step_info.step)
   630	        block_id = seq_index // train_dataset.block_size
   631	        stage = train_dataset._get_stage_for_block(block_id)
   632	        if stage == last_mixture_stage:
   633	            return
   634	
   635	        weights = train_dataset.weight_stages[stage][1]
   636	        mixture_log = {f"mixture/weight/{name}": weight for name, weight in weights.items()}
   637	        mixture_log["mixture/stage"] = stage
   638	        levanter.tracker.log(mixture_log, step=step_info.step)
   639	        last_mixture_stage = stage
   640	
   641	    return log_mixture_stage
   642	
   643	
   644	@register_dataclass
   645	@dataclass(frozen=True)
   646	class GrugTrainState:
   647	    step: jax.Array
   648	    params: Transformer
   649	    master_params: Transformer | None
   650	    opt_state: optax.OptState
   651	    ema_params: Transformer | None
   652	    pending_qb_betas: jax.Array
   653	
   654	
   655	def _apply_qb_betas(model: Transformer, qb_betas: jax.Array) -> Transformer:
   656	    """Set router biases from QB betas (computed on previous step)."""
   657	    new_bias = -qb_betas
   658	    new_bias = new_bias - jnp.mean(new_bias, axis=-1, keepdims=True)
   659	    return eqx.tree_at(lambda t: t.stacked_blocks.stacked.mlp.router_bias, model, new_bias)
   660	
   661	
   662	def _tree_to_memory_kind(tree, memory_kind: str):
   663	    """Move named-sharded arrays to a JAX memory kind."""
   664	
   665	    def _move(leaf):
   666	        if not isinstance(leaf, jax.Array):
   667	            return leaf
   668	        sharding = jax.typeof(leaf).sharding
   669	        mesh = getattr(sharding, "mesh", None)
   670	        if mesh is None or len(getattr(mesh, "axis_names", ())) == 0:
   671	            if jax.sharding.get_abstract_mesh().empty:
   672	                return leaf
   673	            # Scalar optimizer metadata has no operand from which to inherit the active mesh.
   674	            # Bind it before changing memory kind so the initial and updated states have the
   675	            # same JIT input signature.
   676	            leaf = jax.sharding.reshard(leaf, P())
   677	            sharding = jax.typeof(leaf).sharding
   678	        return jax.device_put(leaf, sharding.with_memory_kind(memory_kind))
   679	
   680	    return jax.tree.map(_move, tree)
   681	
   682	
   683	def initial_state(
   684	    model_config: GrugModelConfig,
   685	    *,
   686	    optimizer: optax.GradientTransformation,
   687	    mp: jmp.Policy,
   688	    key: PRNGKeyArray,
   689	    ema_beta: float | None,
   690	    offload_opt_state: bool = False,
  1080	            every=log_every,
  1081	        )
  1082	        state_callbacks.add_hook(callbacks.pbar_logger(total=stop_step), every=log_every)
  1083	        state_callbacks.add_hook(callbacks.log_step_info(stop_step), every=log_every)
  1084	        if profiler_enabled:
  1085	            state_callbacks.add_hook(
  1086	                profiler_cfg.build(
  1087	                    str(trainer.log_dir / run_id / "profiler"),
  1088	                    run_id=run_id,
  1089	                    num_steps=profiler_num_steps,
  1090	                ),
  1091	                every=1,
  1092	            )
  1093	        if train_dataset is not None:
  1094	            state_callbacks.add_hook(_make_mixture_stage_callback(train_dataset, batch_schedule), every=1)
  1095	        state_callbacks.add_hook(log_device_memory, every=1)
  1096	        if evaluator is not None and eval_cfg is not None:
  1097	            interval = eval_cfg.steps_per_eval
  1098	            eval_ema = eval_cfg.eval_ema and config.trainer.ema_beta is not None
  1099	            if eval_cfg.eval_current or eval_ema:
  1100	                tagged_eval_hook = cb_tagged_evaluate(
  1101	                    evaluator,
  1102	                    prefix=eval_cfg.prefix,
  1103	                    eval_current=eval_cfg.eval_current,
  1104	                    eval_ema=eval_ema,
  1105	                )
  1106	                eval_hooks: list[Callable[..., None]] = [tagged_eval_hook]
  1107	                if dropless_evaluator is not None and dropless_eval_mesh is not None:
  1108	                    # The training loop runs under `set_mesh(mesh)` (expert-parallel). The dropless
  1109	                    # evaluator runs under the expert-collapsed mesh, so the model params -- sharded on
  1110	                    # the train mesh -- must be resharded onto the eval mesh before its eval jit (JAX
  1111	                    # does not auto-reshard across explicit meshes), then the local backend sees
  1112	                    # expert=1. PGLE is disabled for the eval module as in `cb_tagged_evaluate`.
  1113	                    dropless_prefix = f"{eval_cfg.prefix}_dropless"
  1114	
  1115	                    def dropless_eval_hook(
  1116	                        step, *args, _mesh=dropless_eval_mesh, _ev=dropless_evaluator, _prefix=dropless_prefix, **kwargs
  1117	                    ):
  1118	                        step_count = int(step.step)
  1119	                        if step_count < 0:
  1120	                            return
  1121	                        # `model` must stay a local. The eval mesh has expert=1, so a leaf sharded on
  1122	                        # the expert axis lands replicated, and the copy is much larger than the
  1123	                        # train-mesh params. The train step needs almost the whole device budget for
  1124	                        # its temporary buffer, thus this copy must die before the next step.
  1125	                        with set_mesh(_mesh):
  1126	                            model = _reshard_tree_to_mesh(step.model, _mesh)
  1127	                            with jax_config.enable_pgle(False):
  1128	                                log_dict = eval_model(_ev, model, prefix=_prefix)
  1129	                            levanter.tracker.log(log_dict, step=step_count)
  1130	
  1131	                    eval_hooks.append(dropless_eval_hook)
  1132	
  1133	                if interval is not None and interval > 0:
  1134	                    for hook in eval_hooks:
  1135	                        state_callbacks.add_hook(hook, every=interval)
  1136	
  1137	                # Baseline point at the start of the loss curve. The periodic cadence first fires at
  1138	                # `steps_per_eval` (step 3000 on the hero), thus a fresh run gets no early point.
  1139	                # These run after the first optimization step, not before it: the first train step
  1140	                # then allocates against a clean pool, and the eval-to-train handoff gets a gate at
  1141	                # step 2 instead of first at step 3000. `every=1` is the only interval that covers
  1142	                # the first step, and `_first_step_only` makes the hook fire once. A resumed run
  1143	                # starts above step 1, thus it never fires. The hooks log at `StepInfo.step`, which
  1144	                # is 0 there, so the point lands at step 0 on the curve.
  1145	                if eval_cfg.eval_at_first_step:
  1146	                    for hook in eval_hooks:
  1147	                        state_callbacks.add_hook(_first_step_only(hook), every=1)
  1148	
  1149	        last_loss: float | jax.Array = 0.0
  1150	        last_step_duration = 0.0
  1151	
  1152	        # Main optimization loop.
  1153	        try:
  1154	            while int(state.step) < stop_step:
  1155	                with jax.profiler.TraceAnnotation("load_batch"):
  1156	                    batch = next(iterator)
  1157	                current_step = int(state.step)
  1158	                watch_due = (
  1159	                    watch_config.is_enabled and watch_config.interval > 0 and current_step % watch_config.interval == 0
  1160	                )
  1161	                if watch_due and diagnostic_watch_step is not None:
  1162	                    watch_stats = diagnostic_watch_step(state.params, batch, state.pending_qb_betas)
  1163	                    jax.block_until_ready(watch_stats)
  1164	                else:
  1165	                    watch_stats = None
  1166	                step_start = time.perf_counter()

    20	CBInfo = TypeVar("CBInfo")
    21	
    22	
    23	class ProgressEvent(StrEnum):
    24	    """Lifecycle events that represent forward progress through training."""
    25	
    26	    PROCESS_STARTED = "process_started"
    27	    TRAIN_STEP_STARTED = "train_step_started"
    28	    TRAIN_STEP_FINISHED = "train_step_finished"
    29	    EVALUATION_STARTED = "evaluation_started"
    30	    EVALUATION_FINISHED = "evaluation_finished"
    31	    CHECKPOINT_STARTED = "checkpoint_started"
    32	    CHECKPOINT_FINISHED = "checkpoint_finished"
    33	    TRAINING_FINISHED = "training_finished"
    34	
    35	
    36	def _ignore_progress_event(event: ProgressEvent) -> None:
    37	    del event
    38	
    39	
    40	@contextmanager
    41	def progress_event_scope(
    42	    emit_event: Callable[[ProgressEvent], None],
    43	    started: ProgressEvent,
    44	    finished: ProgressEvent,
    45	) -> Iterator[None]:
    46	    """Emit paired lifecycle events around a block, including when it raises."""
    47	    emit_event(started)
    48	    try:
    49	        yield
    50	    finally:
    51	        emit_event(finished)
    52	
    53	
    54	@dataclass
    55	class StepInfo(Generic[S]):
    56	    """
    57	    Information about a step that was just completed. This includes the trainer state, the loss, and the duration of the
    58	    step.
    59	
    60	    Note that the step is 0-indexed, so if you want the next step, use `next_step`.
    61	    """
    62	
    63	    state: S
    64	    loss: float
    65	    step_duration: float
    66	    _event_handler: Callable[[ProgressEvent], None] = field(
    67	        default=_ignore_progress_event,
    68	        repr=False,
    69	        compare=False,
    70	    )
    71	
    72	    model = property(lambda self: self.state.model)
    73	    opt_state = property(lambda self: self.state.opt_state)
    74	    eval_model = property(lambda self: self.state.eval_model)
    75	
    76	    step = property(lambda self: int(self.state.step) - 1)
    77	    """
    78	    The step that was just completed. If you want the next step, use `next_step`.
    79	    """
    80	
    81	    next_step = property(lambda self: int(self.state.step))
    82	
    83	    def emit_event(self, event: ProgressEvent) -> None:
    84	        """Notify event-aware callbacks about progress inside a step callback."""
    85	        self._event_handler(event)
    86	
    87	
    88	class Callback(ABC, Generic[S]):
    89	    """
    90	    A callback that can be called at the end of a step. This is useful for logging, profiling, and other side effects.
    91	    """
    92	
    93	    @abc.abstractmethod
    94	    def on_step(self, info: StepInfo[S], force: bool = False): ...
    95	
    96	    def on_event(self, event: ProgressEvent) -> None:
    97	        del event
    98	
    99	
   100	class LambdaCallback(Callback[S]):
   101	    def __init__(self, fn: Callable[..., Any]):
   102	        self.fn = fn
   103	        self._supports_force = self._callable_accepts_force(fn)
   104	
   105	    def on_step(self, info: StepInfo[S], force: bool = False):
   106	        if self._supports_force:
   107	            self.fn(info, force=force)
   108	        else:
   109	            self.fn(info)
   110	
   111	    @staticmethod
   112	    def _callable_accepts_force(fn: Callable[..., Any]) -> bool:
   113	        try:
   114	            sig = inspect.signature(fn)
   115	        except (TypeError, ValueError):
   116	            return False
   117	
   118	        params = sig.parameters
   119	        if "force" in params and params["force"].kind in (
   120	            inspect.Parameter.POSITIONAL_OR_KEYWORD,
   121	            inspect.Parameter.KEYWORD_ONLY,
   122	        ):
   123	            return True
   124	
   125	        return any(p.kind == inspect.Parameter.VAR_KEYWORD for p in params.values())
   126	
   127	
   128	class JitCallback(ABC, Generic[S, M, CBInfo]):
   129	    """
   130	    A callback that gets called in two phases: inside the step (inside jit), and after the step (outside jit).
   131	    You have access to [levanter.trainer_state.InsideJitInfo][] inside the step, which allows you to track gradients,
   132	    updates, etc.
   133	
   134	    Note that JitCallbacks tend to be quite heavy, and you should try to line up  all jit callbacks to
   135	    proc on the same steps.
   136	
   137	    The basic flow of a JitCallback is as follows:
   138	    1. `inside_step` is called inside the JIT-compiled function. This is where you can compute gradients, updates,
   139	       and other information that you want to track. You should return a PyTree of information that you want to log or use later.
   140	    2. The returned information from `inside_step` is passed to `on_step` after the JIT-compiled function has completed.
   141	    """
   142	
   143	    @abc.abstractmethod
   144	    def inside_step(self, state: S, inside_info: InsideJitInfo[M]) -> CBInfo:
   145	        """
   146	        This function is called inside the JIT-compiled function. You have access to the `inside_info` which contains
   147	        information about the gradients, updates, and other information that was computed during the step.
   148	        Args:
   149	            state:
   150	            inside_info:
   151	
   152	        Returns:
   153	
   154	        """
   155	        ...
    55	    def emit_event(self, event: ProgressEvent) -> None:
    56	        for hook in self._hooks:
    57	            hook.fn.on_event(event)
    58	
    59	    def run(self, state: S, *, loss: float | jax.Array, step_duration: float, force: bool = False) -> None:
    60	        # CallbackStateView is a structural stand-in supplying exactly the attributes StepInfo reads;
    61	        # it deliberately does not subclass TrainerState, so it cannot satisfy StepInfo's S: TrainerState bound.
    62	        # pyrefly: ignore[bad-specialization]
    63	        info = StepInfo(
    64	            state=CallbackStateView(
    65	                step=self._step_getter(state),
    66	                model=self._model_getter(state),
    67	                eval_model=self._eval_model_getter(state),
    68	                opt_state=self._opt_state_getter(state),
    69	            ),
    70	            loss=loss,  # type: ignore[arg-type]
    71	            step_duration=step_duration,
    72	            _event_handler=self.emit_event,
    73	        )
    74	        for hook in self._hooks:
    75	            if force or (info.next_step > 0 and info.next_step % hook.every == 0):
    76	                hook.fn.on_step(info, force=force)

   448	            )
   449	        else:
   450	            return
   451	        self.overlay.emit_task_event(
   452	            TaskActionEvent(
   453	                task_id=outcome.task_id,
   454	                attempt_id=attempt_id,
   455	                ts=Timestamp.from_ms(now_ms),
   456	                reason=reason,
   457	                message=message,
   458	                severity=severity,
   459	            )
   460	        )
   461	
   462	    def _apply_update(self, update: TaskUpdate, now_ms: int, *, source: task.TransitionSource) -> None:
   463	        """Apply pass for one worker/provider task update.
   464	
   465	        Applies the per-update transition, runs the peer cascade unconditionally
   466	        (so later updates see requeued/terminated siblings), but gates the
   467	        recompute work-list note + deferred child cascade on an actual state
   468	        change: ``apply_one_transition`` emits no-op outcomes (new data, unchanged
   469	        state) that must not touch the work-list.
   470	        """
   471	        outcome = task.apply_one_transition(self.overlay, self._snapshot, update, now_ms, source=source)
   472	        if outcome is None:
   473	            return
   474	        if outcome.new_task_state != outcome.prior_state:
   475	            reported_state = job_pb2.TaskState.Name(update.new_state).removeprefix("TASK_STATE_")
   476	            self._emit_task_transition_action(
   477	                outcome,
   478	                attempt_id=update.attempt_id,
   479	                message_prefix=f"Backend reported {reported_state}",
   480	                now_ms=now_ms,
   481	            )
   482	        _cascade_to_peers(self.overlay, outcome, now_ms)
   483	        if outcome.new_task_state != outcome.prior_state:
   484	            self._note(outcome.job_id)
   485	            self._defer_pending_child_cascade(outcome, "Parent task retried")
   486	
   487	    def _fan_out(self, outcome: task.TransitionOutcome, *, child_reason: str, now_ms: int) -> None:
   488	        """Apply pass for one controller-asserted transition (failure / preempt / timeout).
   489	
   490	        Same three steps as :meth:`_apply_update`, but the note/defer is
   491	        unconditional: controller-asserted callers only build an outcome for a
   492	        real transition, so there is no no-op outcome to gate against.
   493	        """
   494	        task_row = self._snapshot.tasks.get(outcome.task_id)
   495	        if task_row is not None:
   496	            self._emit_task_transition_action(
   497	                outcome,
   498	                attempt_id=task_row.current_attempt_id,
   499	                message_prefix=child_reason,
   500	                now_ms=now_ms,
   501	            )
   502	        _cascade_to_peers(self.overlay, outcome, now_ms)
   503	        self._note(outcome.job_id)
   504	        self._defer_pending_child_cascade(outcome, child_reason)
   505	
   506	    def _recompute_and_finalize(self, now_ms: int) -> set[JobName]:
   507	        """Recompute pass: recompute each touched job once; finalize terminals; drain cascades.
   508	
   509	        Returns the set of jobs that went terminal in this batch. PENDING
   510	        rollbacks that did not take the job terminal still get their
   511	        ``TERMINATE_CHILDREN`` descendant cascade here, skipping any job already
   512	        finalized above so children never cascade twice.
   513	        """
   514	        cascaded_jobs: set[JobName] = set()
   515	        for job_id in self.touched:
   516	            new_job_state = job.recompute_state(self.overlay, job_id)
   517	            if new_job_state in TERMINAL_JOB_STATES:
   518	                _finalize_terminal_job(self.overlay, job_id, new_job_state, now_ms)
   519	                cascaded_jobs.add(job_id)
   520	        for job_id, reason in self.pending_child_cascades.items():
   521	            if job_id in cascaded_jobs:
   522	                continue
   523	            _cascade_to_children(
   524	                self.overlay,
   525	                job_id,
   526	                now_ms,
   527	                reason,
   528	                action_reason=TaskActionReason.JOB_FINALIZED_TASK_KILLED,
   529	            )
   530	        return cascaded_jobs
   531	
   532	    def _note(self, job_id: JobName) -> None:
   533	        """Add ``job_id`` to the deduped, insertion-ordered recompute work-list."""
   534	        if job_id not in self._touched_seen:
   535	            self._touched_seen.add(job_id)
   536	            self.touched.append(job_id)
   537	
   538	    def _defer_pending_child_cascade(self, outcome: task.TransitionOutcome, reason: str) -> None:
   539	        """Queue the descendant cascade for a PENDING rollback under TERMINATE_CHILDREN.
   540	
   541	        Drained by :meth:`_recompute_and_finalize` so it runs once per job after
   542	        every sibling in the batch has settled.
   543	        """
   544	        if outcome.new_task_state != job_pb2.TASK_STATE_PENDING:
   545	            return
   546	        if self.overlay.job_preemption_policy(outcome.job_id) == job_pb2.JOB_PREEMPTION_POLICY_TERMINATE_CHILDREN:
   547	            self.pending_child_cascades.setdefault(outcome.job_id, reason)
   548	
   549	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
   550	    # reconcile() helpers
   551	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
   552	
   553	    def _reconcile_updates_for_plan(
   554	        self,
   555	        plan: worker.WorkerReconcilePlan,
   556	        result: worker.WorkerReconcileResult,
   557	    ) -> list[TaskUpdate]:
   558	        """Derive the task updates one worker's reconcile result contributes."""
   559	        worker_id = plan.worker_id
   560	
   561	        if result.error is not None:
   562	            self.overlay.emit_log_event(
   563	                LogEvent(
   564	                    action="reconcile_rpc_failed",
   565	                    entity_id=str(worker_id),
   566	                    details=(("error", result.error),),
   567	                )
   568	            )
   569	            candidates: list[tuple[JobName, int]] = []
   570	            for desired in plan.request.desired:
   571	                if not desired.HasField("run") or not desired.run.HasField("request"):
   572	                    continue
   573	                req_proto = desired.run.request
   574	                cand_task_id = JobName.from_wire(req_proto.task_id)
   575	                # Overlay-aware gate: a sibling already requeued to PENDING earlier
   576	                # in this same batch is no longer ASSIGNED, so it must not be
   577	                # fabricated into a synthetic WORKER_FAILED (split-slice corruption).
   578	                # ``assigned_updates_from_plan`` re-checks the snapshot, but that
   579	                # read is blind to same-batch overlay mutations.
   580	                if self.overlay.task_state(cand_task_id) != job_pb2.TASK_STATE_ASSIGNED:
   581	                    continue
   582	                candidates.append((cand_task_id, req_proto.attempt_id))
   583	            if not candidates:
   584	                return []
   585	            return worker.assigned_updates_from_plan(self._snapshot, candidates, result.error)
   586	
   587	        if worker_id not in self._snapshot.active_workers:
   588	            logger.warning(
   589	                "reconcile: worker %s no longer present; dropping %d observations",
   590	                worker_id,
   591	                len(result.observations),
   592	            )
   593	            return []
   594	
   595	        observations = worker.filter_observations_to_plan(plan, result.observations, worker_id)
   596	        if not observations:
   597	            return []
   598	        return worker.observations_to_updates(self._snapshot, observations)
   599	
   600	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
   601	    # fail_workers() helpers
   602	    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
   603	
   604	    def _active_rows_by_failed_worker(self, failed_worker_ids: set[WorkerId]) -> dict[WorkerId, list[ActiveTaskRow]]:
   605	        """Group the snapshot's active task rows by their failed ``current_worker_id``.
   606	
   607	        Only ACTIVE rows carry a worker id; PENDING rows are unassigned (NULL
   608	        worker) and so are naturally excluded. Per-worker order follows the
   609	        snapshot's ``active_tasks_by_job`` ordering.
   610	        """
   611	        rows_by_worker: dict[WorkerId, list[ActiveTaskRow]] = {wid: [] for wid in failed_worker_ids}
   612	        for rows in self._snapshot.active_tasks_by_job.values():
   613	            for row in rows:
   614	                wid = row.current_worker_id
   615	                if wid is not None and wid in failed_worker_ids:
   616	                    rows_by_worker[wid].append(row)
   617	        return rows_by_worker

   620	        self,

   915	
   916	    def _control_tick(
   917	        self,
   918	        *,
   919	        woken: bool,
   920	        schedule_limiter: RateLimiter,
   921	        reconcile_limiter: RateLimiter,
   922	        autoscale_limiter: RateLimiter,
   923	        force_timeout_scan: bool = False,
   924	    ) -> None:
   925	        """Run one control tick: one read snapshot and one write transaction.
   926	
   927	        Phase order is schedule -> reconcile -> autoscale. A wake runs a
   928	        schedule-only mini-tick; autoscale always pairs with a fresh schedule so
   929	        it provisions against this tick's residual demand.
   930	        """
   931	        now = Timestamp.now()
   932	
   933	        # Dry-run: the schedule phase computes and logs intended assignments but
   934	        # writes nothing; reconcile and autoscale are suppressed entirely.
   935	        if self._config.dry_run:
   936	            self._run_scheduling()
   937	            return
   938	
   939	        self._drain_pending_evictions()
   940	        pending_kicks = self._drain_pending_kicks()
   941	
   942	        run_autoscale = autoscale_limiter.should_run()
   943	        run_schedule = woken or run_autoscale or schedule_limiter.should_run()
   944	        run_reconcile = self._force_reconcile or reconcile_limiter.should_run()
   945	        self._force_reconcile = False
   946	        scan_timeouts = run_reconcile and (force_timeout_scan or self._timeout_rate_limiter.should_run())
   947	
   948	        inputs = self._build_tick_inputs(
   949	            now=now,
   950	            run_schedule=run_schedule,
   951	            run_reconcile=run_reconcile,
   952	            scan_timeouts=scan_timeouts,
   953	        )
   954	
   955	        sched_result: ScheduleResult | None = None
   956	        backend_pins: list[tuple[JobName, str]] = []
   957	        if run_schedule:
   958	            sched = self._schedule_phase(inputs)
   959	            sched_result, backend_pins = sched.result, sched.pins
   960	
   961	        # Federation pass: assign queued federated jobs to peers that have room. A pure
   962	        # decision over the tick's snapshot + the manager's reservation ledger; the
   963	        # promotions commit (conditionally) in the same end-of-tick transaction. Runs in
   964	        # the single scheduling thread right after local scheduling, so every scheduling
   965	        # decision — local placement and peer selection — flows through one place.
   966	        federation_promotions: list[Promotion] = []
   967	        if run_schedule and inputs.queued_federation:
   968	            federation_promotions = self._federation.plan_federation(inputs.queued_federation)
   969	
   970	        recon_result: ReconcileResult | None = None
   971	        timeout_decisions: list[TerminalDecision] = []
   972	        if run_reconcile:
   973	            timeout_decisions = self._timeout_decisions(inputs.timeout_rows, now.epoch_ms())
   974	            recon_result = self.backend.reconcile(inputs.reconcile_request)
   975	
   976	        auto_result: AutoscaleResult | None = None
   977	        if run_autoscale:
   978	            residual_demand = sched_result.residual_demand if sched_result is not None else []
   979	            auto_result = self.backend.autoscale(AutoscaleRequest(residual_demand=residual_demand))
   980	
   981	        confirmed_promotions = self._commit_tick(
   982	            sched_result=sched_result,
   983	            backend_pins=backend_pins,
   984	            recon_result=recon_result,
   985	            timeout_decisions=timeout_decisions,
   986	            pending_kicks=pending_kicks,
   987	            auto_result=auto_result,
   988	            federation_promotions=federation_promotions,
   989	            expired_queued_federation=inputs.expired_queued_federation,
   990	            now=now,
   991	        )
   992	
   993	        # Charge the reservation ledger only for promotions whose CAS committed, so a
   994	        # promotion raced by a cancel does not hold phantom peer capacity. The sync
   995	        # loop delivers each newly-PENDING handle on its next pass.
   996	        if confirmed_promotions:
   997	            self._federation.confirm_promotions(confirmed_promotions)
   998	            logger.info(
   999	                "Federation: promoted %d queued job(s): %s",
  1000	                len(confirmed_promotions),
  1001	                ", ".join(f"{p.job_id.to_wire()}->{p.peer_id}" for p in confirmed_promotions),
  1002	            )
  1003	
  1004	        # Force the next reconcile so workers are told to stop the kicked attempts
  1005	        # promptly instead of waiting a full reconcile interval.
  1006	        if pending_kicks:
  1007	            self._force_reconcile = True
  1008	            self._tick_wake.set()
  1009	
  1010	        # Post-commit, in-memory: cache scheduling diagnostics, request a prompt
  1011	        # dispatch follow-up for fresh assignments.
  1012	        if sched_result is not None:
  1013	            self._scheduling_diagnostics = sched_result.diagnostics
  1014	            if sched_result.assignments:
  1015	                self._force_reconcile = True

   137	def _validate_user(user: str, source: str) -> str:
   138	    """Strip and validate a user value, naming the config source in errors."""
   139	    user = user.strip()
   140	    if not user:
   141	        raise ValueError(f"{source} must not be empty")
   142	    if "/" in user:
   143	        raise ValueError(f"{source} must not contain '/': {user!r}")
   144	    return user
   145	
   146	
   147	def resolve_job_user(explicit_user: str | None = None) -> str:
   148	    """Resolve the submitting user for a new top-level job.
   149	
   150	    Resolution order: the explicit argument, the ``IRIS_USER`` env var, the
   151	    current job's user, the OS user, and finally ``root``.
   152	    """
   153	    if explicit_user is not None:
   154	        return _validate_user(explicit_user, "Job user")
   155	
   156	    env_user = os.environ.get("IRIS_USER")
   157	    if env_user is not None:
   158	        return _validate_user(env_user, "IRIS_USER")
   159	
   160	    info = get_job_info()
   161	    if info is not None:
   162	        return info.user
   163	
   164	    try:
   165	        resolved = getpass.getuser()
   166	    except (OSError, KeyError, ImportError) as exc:
   167	        logger.warning("Falling back to default Iris job user 'root': could not resolve local user (%s)", exc)
   168	        return "root"
   169	
   170	    if not resolved or not resolved.strip():
   171	        logger.warning("Falling back to default Iris job user 'root': local user was empty")
   172	        return "root"
   173	    return resolved

  1009	                    or environment.pip_packages
  1010	                    or environment.sync_packages

   182	
   183	    def child(self, name: str) -> "JobName":
   184	        """Create a child job name."""
   185	        return JobName((*self._parts, name))
   186	
   187	    def task(self, index: int) -> "JobName":
   188	        """Create a task name for this job.
   189	
   190	        Tasks are job names with a numeric suffix.
   191	
   192	        Example:
   193	            JobName.from_string("/alice/my-job").task(0) -> JobName(("alice", "my-job", "0"))
   194	        """
   195	        return JobName((*self._parts, str(index)))
   196	
   197	    @property
   198	    def parent(self) -> "JobName | None":
   199	        """Get parent job name, or None if this is a root job."""
   200	        if self.is_root:
   201	            return None
   202	        return JobName(self._parts[:-1])
   203	
   204	    @property
   205	    def user(self) -> str:
   206	        """Get the submitting user."""
   207	        return self._parts[0]
   208	
   209	    @property
   210	    def root_job(self) -> "JobName":
   211	        """Get the root job for this hierarchy."""
   212	        return JobName(self._parts[:2])
   213	
   214	    @property
   215	    def namespace(self) -> str:
   216	        """Get the actor namespace (user/root job) for actor isolation."""
   217	        return "/" + "/".join(self.root_job._parts)
   218	
   219	    @property
   220	    def name(self) -> str:
   221	        """Get the local name (last component)."""
   222	        return self._parts[-1]
   223	
   224	    @property
   225	    def is_root(self) -> bool:
   226	        """True if this is a root job (no parent)."""
   227	        return len(self._parts) == 2
   228	
   229	    @property
   230	    def task_index(self) -> int | None:
   231	        """If this is a task (last component is numeric), return the index."""
   232	        if len(self._parts) < 3:
   233	            return None
   234	        try:
   235	            return int(self._parts[-1])
   236	        except ValueError:
   237	            return None
   238	
   239	    @property
   240	    def is_task(self) -> bool:
   241	        """True if this is a task (last component is numeric)."""
   242	        return self.task_index is not None
   243	
   244	    @property
   245	    def depth(self) -> int:
   246	        """Depth in the job hierarchy. Root jobs have depth 1.
   247	
   248	        Tasks inherit their parent job's depth (the task index
   249	        is not counted as a depth level).
   250	
   251	        Examples:
   252	            /alice/root -> 1
   253	            /alice/root/child -> 2
   254	            /alice/root/child/grandchild -> 3
   255	            /alice/root/0 (task) -> 1
   256	            /alice/root/child/0 (task) -> 2
   257	        """
   258	        if self.is_task:
   259	            return len(self._parts) - 2
   260	        return len(self._parts) - 1
   261	
   262	    def is_ancestor_of(self, other: "JobName", *, include_self: bool = True) -> bool:
   263	        """True if this job name is an ancestor of another job name."""
   264	        if include_self and self == other:
   265	            return True
   266	        if len(self._parts) >= len(other._parts):
   267	            return False
   268	        return other._parts[: len(self._parts)] == self._parts
   269	
   270	    def to_safe_token(self) -> str:
   271	        """Return a filesystem/tag-safe token derived from this name.
   272	
   273	        Uses ``<user>-<sha256-hex>`` so the token stays short even for deeply
   274	        nested job hierarchies (avoids ``ENAMETOOLONG`` on workdir creation).
   275	        The full canonical name is hashed to preserve uniqueness.
   276	        """
   277	        digest = hashlib.sha256(str(self).encode()).hexdigest()
   278	        return f"{self.user}-{digest}"
   279	
   280	    def require_task(self) -> tuple["JobName", int]:
   281	        """Return (parent_job, task_index) for task names.
   282	
   283	        Raises:
   284	            ValueError: If this name is not a task or has no parent.
   285	        """
   286	        task_index = self.task_index
   287	        if task_index is None:
   288	            raise ValueError(f"JobName is not a task: {self}")
   289	        if self.parent is None:
   290	            raise ValueError(f"Task has no parent job: {self}")
   291	        return (self.parent, task_index)

   700	    def _apply_unschedulable_decision(self, decision: task.TerminalDecision) -> None:
   701	        # A still-PENDING task is the normal unschedulable target, so (unlike
   702	        # preempt) don't require an active state — only skip a task an earlier
   703	        # same-batch decision already drove terminal.
   704	        effective_state = self.overlay.task_state(decision.task_id)
   705	        if effective_state is not None and effective_state in TERMINAL_TASK_STATES:
   706	            return
   707	        job_id = task.unschedulable_one(self.overlay, self._snapshot, decision.task_id, decision.reason)
   708	        if job_id is None:
   709	            return
   710	        self._note(job_id)
   711	        self.overlay.emit_log_event(
   712	            LogEvent(
   713	                action="task_unschedulable",

   715	                details=(("reason", decision.reason),),

   719	    def _cascade_timeouts(self, rows: list[ActiveTaskRow], reason: str, now_ms: int) -> None:
   720	        """Two-phase timeout cascade with sibling dedup.
   721	
   722	        Notes touched jobs; the recompute pass finalizes any that go terminal.
   723	        """
   724	        if not rows:
   725	            return
   726	        direct_task_wires: set[str] = set()
   727	        siblings_by_job: dict[str, list[ActiveTaskRow]] = {}
   728	
   729	        for row in rows:
   730	            direct_task_wires.add(row.task_id.to_wire())
   731	            job_id_wire = row.job_id.to_wire()
   732	            if not row.has_coscheduling:
   733	                continue
   734	            siblings = peers.find_coscheduled_siblings(self.overlay, row.job_id, row.task_id)
   735	            if siblings:
   736	                siblings_by_job.setdefault(job_id_wire, []).extend(siblings)
   737	
   738	        # Deduplicate: drop siblings that will already be terminated as direct
   739	        # victims; dedupe across multiple trigger tasks within the same job.
   740	        for job_id_wire, siblings in siblings_by_job.items():
   741	            seen: set[str] = set()
   742	            deduped: list[ActiveTaskRow] = []
   743	            for sib in siblings:
   744	                sib_wire = sib.task_id.to_wire()
   745	                if sib_wire not in direct_task_wires and sib_wire not in seen:
   746	                    seen.add(sib_wire)
   747	                    deduped.append(sib)
   748	            siblings_by_job[job_id_wire] = deduped
   749	
   750	        task_ids_to_log: set[JobName] = set()
   751	
   752	        for row in rows:
   753	            task_ids_to_log.add(row.task_id)
   754	            task.timeout_one(self.overlay, row, reason, now_ms)
   755	            self._note(row.job_id)
   756	
   757	        for job_id_wire, siblings in siblings_by_job.items():
   758	            if not siblings:
   759	                continue
   760	            cause_tid = next(r.task_id for r in rows if r.job_id.to_wire() == job_id_wire)
   761	            peers.terminate_coscheduled_siblings(self.overlay, siblings, cause_tid, now_ms)
   762	            for sib in siblings:
   763	                task_ids_to_log.add(sib.task_id)
   764	            self._note(JobName.from_wire(job_id_wire))
   765	
   766	        for tid in task_ids_to_log:
   767	            self.overlay.emit_log_event(
   768	                LogEvent(action="task_timeout", entity_id=tid.to_wire(), details=(("reason", reason),))
   769	            )
     1	# Copyright The Marin Authors
     2	# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
     3	
     4	"""The reconcile kernel facade: snapshot in → ``ControllerEffects`` out.
     5	
     6	:class:`ReconcileState` is one batch session over a closed
     7	:class:`TransitionSnapshot`. Its public methods are the controller's state
     8	operations — ``reconcile``, ``fail_workers``, ``record_updates``,
     9	``finalize_tasks``, ``cancel_job`` — each of which runs the same
    10	two-pass contract and returns the accumulated effects:
    11	
    12	* **apply pass** — per task-update or controller-asserted outcome: apply the
    13	  per-task mutation, cascade to coscheduled peers (so later items in the batch
    14	  observe the terminate/requeue), and record the job on a touched-jobs work-list
    15	  (plus any deferred PENDING-rollback child cascade).
    16	* **recompute pass** (``_recompute_and_finalize``) — recompute each touched job
    17	  once, finalize the ones that go terminal, then drain the deferred child
    18	  cascades. Folding recompute out of the per-update loop makes a batch
    19	  order-independent and keeps ``job.recompute_state`` (which rescans a job's
    20	  whole task histogram) off the O(tasks_per_job²) per-dispatch path.
    21	
    22	The cross-aggregate primitives below the facade (``_kill_non_terminal_tasks``,
    23	``_cascade_to_children``, ``_finalize_terminal_job``, ``_cascade_to_peers``) stay
    24	free functions over an :class:`Overlay` so they remain unit-testable in isolation.
    25	"""
    26	
    27	import logging
    28	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
    29	
    30	from rigging.timing import Timestamp
    31

    72	def _finish_non_terminal_tasks(
    73	    overlay: Overlay,
    74	    job_id: JobName,
    75	    *,
    76	    task_state: int,
    77	    error: str | None,
    78	    exit_code: int | None,
    79	    message: str,
    80	    action_reason: TaskActionReason,
    81	    severity: TaskEventSeverity,
    82	    now_ms: int,
    83	) -> None:
    84	    """Finish all non-terminal tasks for a job and stop their active attempts."""
    85	    for row in overlay.active_tasks_for_job(job_id, states=NON_TERMINAL_TASK_STATES):
    86	        task.merge_task_termination(
    87	            overlay,
    88	            row.task_id.to_wire(),
    89	            row.current_attempt_id,
    90	            task_state,
    91	            error,
    92	            now_ms,
    93	            stamp_attempt_finished=False,
    94	            exit_code=exit_code,
    95	        )
    96	        overlay.emit_task_event(
    97	            TaskActionEvent(
    98	                task_id=row.task_id,
    99	                attempt_id=row.current_attempt_id,
   100	                ts=Timestamp.from_ms(now_ms),
   101	                reason=action_reason,
   102	                message=message,
   103	                severity=severity,
   104	            )
   105	        )

   108	def _kill_non_terminal_tasks(

   116	    """Kill all non-terminal tasks for a single job and delete endpoints."""
   117	    _finish_non_terminal_tasks(

   119	        job_id,

   121	        error=reason,

   130	def _cascade_to_children(
   131	    overlay: Overlay,
   132	    job_id: JobName,
   133	    now_ms: int,
   134	    reason: str,
   135	    *,
   136	    action_reason: TaskActionReason,
   137	) -> None:
   138	    """Kill descendant jobs (not the job itself) on a parent terminal/preempt."""
   139	    descendants = overlay.job_descendants(job_id)
   140	    for child_job_id in descendants:
   141	        _kill_non_terminal_tasks(
   142	            overlay,
   143	            child_job_id,
   144	            reason,
   145	            now_ms,
   146	            action_reason=action_reason,
   147	        )
   148	        overlay.merge_cascade_kill(
   149	            JobRowDelta(
   150	                job_id=child_job_id,
   151	                state=job_pb2.JOB_STATE_KILLED,
   152	                error=reason,
   153	                finished_at=Timestamp.from_ms(now_ms),
   154	                is_cascade_kill=True,
   155	            )
   156	        )

   159	def _terminal_job_reason(overlay: Overlay, job_id: JobName, terminal_state: int) -> str:
   160	    return overlay.job_delta_error(job_id) or TERMINAL_STATE_FALLBACK_REASONS.get(terminal_state, "Job finalized")
   161	
   162	
   163	def _finalize_terminal_job(overlay: Overlay, job_id: JobName, terminal_state: int, now_ms: int) -> None:
   164	    """Kill remaining tasks and optionally cascade to children when a job goes terminal."""
   165	    reason = _terminal_job_reason(overlay, job_id, terminal_state)
   166	    _kill_non_terminal_tasks(
   167	        overlay,
   168	        job_id,
   169	        reason,
   170	        now_ms,
   171	        action_reason=TaskActionReason.JOB_FINALIZED_TASK_KILLED,
   172	    )
   173	    should_cascade = True
   174	    if terminal_state != job_pb2.JOB_STATE_SUCCEEDED:
   175	        policy = overlay.job_preemption_policy(job_id)
   176	        should_cascade = policy == job_pb2.JOB_PREEMPTION_POLICY_TERMINATE_CHILDREN
   177	    if should_cascade:
   178	        _cascade_to_children(
   179	            overlay,
   180	            job_id,
   181	            now_ms,
   182	            PARENT_JOB_TERMINATED_REASON,
   183	            action_reason=TaskActionReason.PARENT_JOB_TERMINATED,
   184	        )

   186	
   187	def _cascade_to_peers(overlay: Overlay, outcome: task.TransitionOutcome, now_ms: int) -> None:
   188	    """Coscheduled-sibling cascade for one transition. No job recompute."""
   189	    if not outcome.cascade_to_peers:
   190	        return
   191	    siblings = peers.find_coscheduled_siblings(overlay, outcome.job_id, outcome.task_id)
   192	    if outcome.new_task_state in FAILURE_TASK_STATES:
   193	        peers.terminate_coscheduled_siblings(overlay, siblings, outcome.task_id, now_ms)
   194	    else:
   195	        peers.requeue_coscheduled_siblings(overlay, siblings, outcome.task_id, now_ms)

   198	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   199	# The batch facade
   200	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   201	
   202	
   203	@dataclass
   204	class ReconcileState:
   205	    """One batch session over a closed snapshot.
   206	
   207	    Holds the mutable :class:`Overlay` plus the two cross-batch accumulators the
   208	    two-pass contract threads through every operation:
   209	
   210	    * ``touched`` — insertion-ordered, deduped work-list of jobs with a
   211	      state-changing task transition. The recompute pass recomputes each once.
   212	    * ``pending_child_cascades`` — jobs whose parent task rolled back to PENDING
   213	      under a ``TERMINATE_CHILDREN`` policy; their descendant cascade is deferred
   214	      (deduped per job) so it runs once after every sibling in the batch settled.
   215	    """
     1	# Copyright The Marin Authors
     2	# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
     3	
     4	"""Pure rules for the job aggregate: state recomputation."""
     5	
     6	from iris.cluster.controller.reconcile.effects import JobRowDelta
     7	from iris.cluster.controller.reconcile.overlay import Overlay
     8	from iris.cluster.controller.reconcile.policy import ERROR_STATES
     9	from iris.cluster.types import (
    10	    TERMINAL_JOB_STATES,
    11	    TERMINAL_TASK_STATES,
    12	    JobName,
    13	)
    14	from iris.rpc import job_pb2
    15	
    16	
    17	def recompute_state(state: Overlay, job_id: JobName) -> int | None:
    18	    """Recompute job state from the prospective task histogram.
    19	
    20	    Returns the new state (which may equal current). Returns ``None`` when
    21	    the job basis is not in the snapshot (out-of-slice). Records a job-state
    22	    delta when the state changes.
    23	    """
    24	    basis = state.job_basis(job_id)
    25	    if basis is None:
    26	        return None
    27	    current_state = basis.state
    28	    max_task_failures = basis.max_task_failures
    29	    if current_state in TERMINAL_JOB_STATES:
    30	        return current_state
    31	    counts = basis.task_state_counts
    32	    total = sum(counts.values())
    33	    new_state = current_state
    34	    error = basis.first_task_error
    35	    now = state.now
    36	    if total > 0 and counts.get(job_pb2.TASK_STATE_SUCCEEDED, 0) == total:
    37	        new_state = job_pb2.JOB_STATE_SUCCEEDED
    38	    elif basis.total_failures > max_task_failures:
    39	        # Cumulative failure budget: ``total_failures`` is the derived count of the
    40	        # job's FAILED attempts — the committed base the loader summed plus this
    41	        # batch's FAILED attempt writes (see Overlay.job_basis). It counts every hard
    42	        # task failure, including those retried back to PENDING and, for coscheduled
    43	        # gangs, the one FAILED attempt per crashed round (siblings go COSCHED_FAILED,
    44	        # which is neither a failure nor a preemption). Failing on this cumulative
    45	        # count — rather than the instantaneous number of tasks currently in FAILED —
    46	        # stops a gang from crash-looping forever when each round's failure lands on a
    47	        # different task and no single task ever exhausts its per-task retry budget.
    48	        # Preemptions are retried by Iris and never counted here, so they are excluded.
    49	        new_state = job_pb2.JOB_STATE_FAILED
    50	        error = (
    51	            "Cumulative failed task attempts exceeded max_task_failures: "
    52	            f"failures={basis.total_failures}, limit={max_task_failures}"
    53	        )
    54	        if basis.first_task_error:
    55	            error = f"{error}; {basis.first_task_error}"
    56	    elif counts.get(job_pb2.TASK_STATE_UNSCHEDULABLE, 0) > 0:
    57	        new_state = job_pb2.JOB_STATE_UNSCHEDULABLE
    58	    elif counts.get(job_pb2.TASK_STATE_KILLED, 0) > 0:
    59	        new_state = job_pb2.JOB_STATE_KILLED
    60	    elif (
    61	        total > 0
    62	        and (
    63	            counts.get(job_pb2.TASK_STATE_WORKER_FAILED, 0)
    64	            + counts.get(job_pb2.TASK_STATE_PREEMPTED, 0)
    65	            + counts.get(job_pb2.TASK_STATE_COSCHED_FAILED, 0)
    66	        )
    67	        > 0
    68	        and all(s in TERMINAL_TASK_STATES for s in counts)
    69	    ):
    70	        new_state = job_pb2.JOB_STATE_WORKER_FAILED
    71	    elif total > 0 and all(s in TERMINAL_TASK_STATES for s in counts):
    72	        # All tasks terminal but not all SUCCEEDED, none of the harder terminal
    73	        # states above (worker_failed/preempted/cosched/unschedulable/killed),
    74	        # and within the max_task_failures threshold: at least one task exhausted
    75	        # its retries and is terminally FAILED. A task that can never succeed
    76	        # fails the whole job. (max_task_failures only controls early abort at the
    77	        # cumulative-failures-over-threshold branch above; once every task is
    78	        # terminal a lone tolerated FAILED still fails the job.) Without this
    79	        # branch the job falls through to the started_at branch and hangs RUNNING.
    80	        new_state = job_pb2.JOB_STATE_FAILED
    81	    elif (
    82	        counts.get(job_pb2.TASK_STATE_ASSIGNED, 0) > 0
    83	        or counts.get(job_pb2.TASK_STATE_BUILDING, 0) > 0
    84	        or counts.get(job_pb2.TASK_STATE_RUNNING, 0) > 0
    85	    ):
    86	        new_state = job_pb2.JOB_STATE_RUNNING
    87	    elif basis.started_at is not None:
    88	        new_state = job_pb2.JOB_STATE_RUNNING
    89	    elif total > 0:
    90	        new_state = job_pb2.JOB_STATE_PENDING
    91	    if new_state == current_state:
    92	        return new_state
    93	    state.merge_job_state(
    94	        JobRowDelta(
    95	            job_id=job_id,
    96	            state=new_state,
    97	            started_at=now if new_state == job_pb2.JOB_STATE_RUNNING else None,
    98	            finished_at=now if new_state in TERMINAL_JOB_STATES else None,
    99	            error=error if new_state in ERROR_STATES else None,
   100	        )
   101	    )
   102	    return new_state

   924	        max_retries_preemption: int = 1000,
   925	        max_task_failures: int = 0,
   926	        timeout: Duration | None = None,
   927	        user: str | None = None,
   928	        preemption_policy: job_pb2.JobPreemptionPolicy = job_pb2.JOB_PREEMPTION_POLICY_UNSPECIFIED,
   929	        existing_job_policy: job_pb2.ExistingJobPolicy = job_pb2.EXISTING_JOB_POLICY_UNSPECIFIED,
   930	        task_image: str | None = None,
   931	        priority_band: job_pb2.PriorityBand = job_pb2.PRIORITY_BAND_INHERIT,
   932	        container_profile: job_pb2.ContainerProfile = job_pb2.CONTAINER_PROFILE_UNSPECIFIED,
   933	        submit_argv: list[str] | None = None,
   934	    ) -> Job:
   935	        """Submit a job with automatic job_id hierarchy.
   936	
   937	        Args:
   938	            entrypoint: Job entrypoint (callable + args/kwargs)
   939	            name: Job name (cannot contain '/')
   940	            resources: Resource requirements
   941	            environment: Environment configuration
   942	            ports: Port names to allocate (e.g., ["actor", "metrics"])
   943	            scheduling_timeout: Maximum time to wait for scheduling (None = no timeout)
   944	            constraints: Constraints for filtering workers by attribute
   945	            coscheduling: Configuration for atomic multi-task scheduling
   946	            replicas: Number of tasks to create for gang scheduling (default: 1).
   947	                Multi-process GPU execution within a task is composed into the command
   948	                (``python -m iris.hooks.multigpu_main --nproc N -- <cmd>``), not a submit arg.
   949	            max_retries_failure: Max retries per task on failure (default: 0)
   950	            max_retries_preemption: Max retries per task on preemption (default: 100)
  1040	        # Drop it before the wire so it does not exclude workers without region metadata.
  1041	        if constraints:
  1042	            constraints = [c for c in constraints if not is_any_region_marker(c)]
  1043	
  1044	        # Convert to wire format
  1045	        resources_proto = resources.to_proto()
  1046	        environment_proto = environment.to_proto() if environment else None
  1047	        constraints_proto = [c.to_proto() for c in constraints or []]
  1048	        coscheduling_proto = coscheduling.to_proto() if coscheduling else None
  1049	
  1050	        try:
  1051	            canonical_id = self._cluster_client.submit_job(
  1052	                job_id=job_id,
  1053	                entrypoint=entrypoint,
  1054	                resources=resources_proto,
  1055	                environment=environment_proto,
  1056	                ports=ports,
  1057	                scheduling_timeout=scheduling_timeout,
  1058	                constraints=constraints_proto,
  1059	                coscheduling=coscheduling_proto,
  1060	                replicas=replicas,
  1061	                max_retries_failure=max_retries_failure,
  1062	                max_retries_preemption=max_retries_preemption,
  1063	                max_task_failures=max_task_failures,
  1064	                timeout=timeout,
  1065	                preemption_policy=preemption_policy,
  1066	                existing_job_policy=existing_job_policy,
  1067	                task_image=task_image,
  1068	                priority_band=priority_band,
  1069	                container_profile=container_profile,
  1070	                submit_argv=submit_argv,
  1071	            )
  1072	        except ConnectError as e:
  1073	            if e.code == Code.ALREADY_EXISTS:
  1074	                raise JobAlreadyExists(str(e)) from e
  1075	            raise
  1076	
  1077	        return Job(self, canonical_id)
  1078	
  1079	    def job_status(self, job_id: JobName) -> JobStatus:
  1080	        """Return the current snapshot for a logical Job name."""
