/etc/profiles/per-user/marin/bin/bash -lc 'nl -ba autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/score.py' in /home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	# Copyright The Marin Authors
     2	# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
     3	
     4	"""Score one arm of the ragged EP64 tuning loop from its W&B history.
     5	
     6	The metric is mean ``throughput/mfu`` over a fixed step window. The window is fixed for the whole
     7	loop: comparing arms scored over different windows compares warmup fractions, not transports.
     8	
     9	Also emits the two guard quantities that come from the same run -- the worst drop fraction and the
    10	loss at the window's last step -- so a faster arm that quietly dropped tokens or changed the model
    11	cannot be recorded as an improvement.
    12	
    13	usage: uv run --with wandb python score.py <run-id> [--lo 5] [--hi 19]
    14	"""
    15	
    16	import argparse
    17	import json
    18	import statistics
    19	
    20	import wandb
    21	
    22	MFU_KEY = "throughput/mfu"
    23	LOSS_KEY = "train/loss"
    24	DROP_KEY = "moe/drop_fraction"
    25	TOKENS_KEY = "throughput/tokens_per_second"
    26	
    27	
    28	def main() -> None:
    29	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    30	    parser.add_argument("run_id")
    31	    parser.add_argument("--lo", type=int, default=5, help="first scored step")
    32	    parser.add_argument("--hi", type=int, default=19, help="last scored step")
    33	    parser.add_argument("--project", default="marin-community/marin_moe")
    34	    parser.add_argument(
    35	        "--relative",
    36	        action="store_true",
    37	        help="Read lo/hi as offsets from the run's first logged step. A restored run resumes at "
    38	        "the checkpoint's step, so an absolute window would miss it entirely.",
    39	    )
    40	    args = parser.parse_args()
    41	
    42	    run = wandb.Api().run(f"{args.project}/{args.run_id}")
    43	    rows = list(run.scan_history())
    44	    steps = [int(r["_step"]) for r in rows if r.get("_step") is not None]
    45	    by_step: dict[int, dict] = {}
    46	    for row in rows:
    47	        step = row.get("_step")
    48	        if step is None:
    49	            continue
    50	        by_step.setdefault(int(step), {}).update({k: v for k, v in row.items() if v is not None})
    51	
    52	    first_step = min(by_step) if by_step else 0
    53	    lo, hi = (first_step + args.lo, first_step + args.hi) if args.relative else (args.lo, args.hi)
    54	
    55	    def series(key: str) -> list[float]:
    56	        return [float(by_step[s][key]) for s in sorted(by_step) if lo <= s <= hi and key in by_step[s]]
    57	
    58	    mfu = series(MFU_KEY)
    59	    drops = series(DROP_KEY)
    60	    losses = [(s, by_step[s][LOSS_KEY]) for s in sorted(by_step) if lo <= s <= hi and LOSS_KEY in by_step[s]]
    61	    tokens = series(TOKENS_KEY)
    62	
    63	    result = {
    64	        "run_id": args.run_id,
    65	        "state": run.state,
    66	        "max_step": max(steps) if steps else -1,
    67	        "window": [lo, hi],
    68	        "first_step": first_step,
    69	        "scored_points": len(mfu),
    70	        # A window with holes in it is not the window the loop agreed to compare, so say so loudly
    71	        # rather than averaging whatever happens to be there.
    72	        "window_complete": len(mfu) == hi - lo + 1,
    73	        "mfu_mean": round(statistics.fmean(mfu), 4) if mfu else None,
    74	        "mfu_stdev": round(statistics.stdev(mfu), 4) if len(mfu) > 1 else None,
    75	        "tokens_per_second_mean": round(statistics.fmean(tokens), 1) if tokens else None,
    76	        # Drops are an outcome here, not only a guard: the whole point of the ragged transport is
    77	        # moving tokens the pooled one clips, so a throughput win that costs drops is not a win.
    78	        "drop_fraction_max": max(drops) if drops else None,
    79	        "drop_fraction_mean": round(statistics.fmean(drops), 6) if drops else None,
    80	        # Guard-series completeness: a killed run can leave the last rows of less-frequent keys
    81	        # unflushed, so a "last loss" alone can silently come from an earlier step. Emit the full
    82	        # series; the loop compares max |delta| against the paired control (data is deterministic
    83	        # across arms at a fixed restore step, so the series pair pointwise).
    84	        "drop_points": len(drops),
    85	        "loss_points": len(losses),
    86	        "loss_series": [[s, round(float(v), 6)] for s, v in losses],
    87	        "loss_last": float(losses[-1][1]) if losses else None,
    88	        "loss_last_step": losses[-1][0] if losses else None,
    89	    }
    90	    print(json.dumps(result, indent=2))
    91	
    92	
    93	if __name__ == "__main__":
    94	    main()

+extra arms are cheap (stay courteous to other rack users). The expensive
+failure is a WRONG CONCLUSION: being sent in a bad direction, or prematurely
+closing a promising one, because a flawed experiment misled the loop. Review

+second. Concretely: every treatment arm pre-declares an ENGAGEMENT CHECK

+flag, an HLO property, or a metric that cannot move unless the lever did);

+near-threshold results get more draws rather than a coin-flip call.

     1	# Campaign: ragged EP hero MFU loop (loop-260828-8753mfu)
     2	
     3	Goal: raise hero MFU beyond the #8753 stack's frontier without fidelity loss
     4	(no added quantization; loss trajectory equivalent or better) and without
     5	breaking hero-checkpoint compatibility (every keep must hot-swap into the live
     6	hero run; checkpoint migration OK, restore from hero checkpoints must work).
     7	
     8	Cost model (user, 2026-08-29): rack capacity is use-it-or-lose-it — failed or
     9	extra arms are cheap (stay courteous to other rack users). The expensive
    10	failure is a WRONG CONCLUSION: being sent in a bad direction, or prematurely
    11	closing a promising one, because a flawed experiment misled the loop. Review
    12	cycles and arm design optimize for inferential validity first, rack frugality
    13	second. Concretely: every treatment arm pre-declares an ENGAGEMENT CHECK
    14	(evidence the lever actually engaged — a kernel name in a profile, an echoed
    15	flag, an HLO property, or a metric that cannot move unless the lever did);
    16	"no effect" results are only recorded as dead after engagement is confirmed;
    17	near-threshold results get more draws rather than a coin-flip call.
    18	
    19	## Base
    20	
    21	- Branch `research/mcwitt/8753-mfu-loop` @ 04026e94fb = PR #8753 head
    22	  (`mcwitt/8317-hero-step-optims`, based on xla-fork/#8684).
    23	- Contents: offload_carry remat + latency-hiding scheduler (forced
    24	  overlap-limit 1), bitcast pair-interleave, FA4 128x64 wide forward tile as
    25	  `gpu_fa4_cute_wide` (landed by implementation name; dispatch has passed unit
    26	  tests but has NOT yet run on a rack — the iteration-0 control validates it).
    27	- PJRT wheel: `jax_cuda13_pjrt-0.11.1+marin.c9526e8c0272` pinned in
    28	  pyproject/uv.lock (GitHub release, marin-xla-pjrt-20260828). No wheel
    29	  overlay in arms.
    30	- Reference numbers: 24k-restore same-night pair (2026-08-28): control (#8753
    31	  stack sans tile) 23.06/23.05, +tile 23.32/23.37 MFU. 30k-restore cross-ref:
    32	  plain-#8684 gate 22.31 median. Protocols are not comparable; this campaign
    33	  re-baselines at 30k.
    34	
    35	## Fixed protocol (every arm)
    36	
    37	- One NVL72 rack, cw-us-east-08a, `experiments.grug.moe_hero_ep.launch_diagnostics`,
    38	  one arm on the rack at a time.
    39	- Restore from the latest hero permanent checkpoint
    40	  `s3://marin-us-east-02a/marin/grug/hero-12d8b6f0-dee637/2026.08.19.2/checkpoints/step-30000`
    41	  (verified complete: manifest.json present). `--num-steps 30030` (ABSOLUTE
    42	  stop step: checkpoint step + 30; `--num-steps 30` exits vacuously right
    43	  after restore).
    44	- Mixture data (trained-router routing), cf 1.15, `ragged_all_to_all`,
    45	  `--master-params device` (this branch renamed the master-less mode
    46	  `disabled` -> `device`; in-process migration reads a master-bearing
    47	  checkpoint's fp32 master directly into device params), schedule-steps
    48	  4470000, no checkpoints/evals. Profiler off on scored arms EXCEPT an
    49	  allowed profile tail: `PROFILE_STEPS` few, `PROFILE_START_STEP` ABSOLUTE
    50	  (e.g. 30021) so it lands past the scoring window — profiler start_step is
    51	  a global step; a relative value like 5 silently never fires on a restore.
    52	- `JAX_COMPILATION_CACHE_DIR` rotated per run id (clique-init deadlock dodge)
    53	  → every arm cold-compiles ~9.5 min; pre-phase (env sync + restore +
    54	  migration) ~19 min cold, less when the object cache is warm.
    55	- Priority: production allowed (user 2026-08-28) with job runtime capped
    56	  under 1 hour: iris `--timeout 3500`. Watchdog: COMPILE_CEILING=1800 from
    57	  first W&B-run existence, STEP_BUDGET=1200 (20-min post-compile cap, user),
    58	  early release at +19 steps, cancel on preemption/failure/coord death;
    59	  QUEUE_CEILING bounds pending time (queueing doesn't count against the 1h).
    60	- SERIALIZATION: one arm on the rack at a time, and arm N+1 is submitted only
    61	  after arm N's watchdog exits — the coordinator's iris `--timeout 3500`
    62	  clock starts when the coordinator starts (it schedules instantly and waits
    63	  on its child), so an arm queued behind another arm burns its 1h budget in
    64	  the queue and gets killed just as its train job starts.
    65	- RESUBMISSION: an invalid arm (watchdog kill, preemption, incomplete
    66	  window) is resubmitted with a FRESH RID and VERSION, never reused — a
    67	  reused RID merges W&B histories (silent window mixing), reuses the
    68	  leader-populated compile cache (the documented clique-init deadlock
    69	  recipe), and can vacuously reuse the artifact layer.
    70	- Hero liveness: the live hero is NOT currently training (step-30000 is the
    71	  newest permanent checkpoint; no hero job in iris). Before each arm, check
    72	  the rack queue; if the hero ever reappears on this cluster, pause the
    73	  campaign and reassess priorities rather than contending with it.
    74	- Rack etiquette: queue arms back-to-back while work is pending; do not
    75	  submit anything if the loop is blocked >1h on analysis.
    76	- Timestamp bookkeeping (calibration): for each arm record W&B-run-created
    77	  -> first-step wall time. The compile ceiling starts at run creation, which
    78	  is BEFORE restore + master migration + compile; a ceiling kill on a cold
    79	  arm is not evidence against the arm until these timings say the ceiling
    80	  actually fits the work.
    81	
    82	## Metric and guards
    83	
    84	- Metric: mean `throughput/mfu` over scored steps +5..+19 relative to the
    85	  first logged step (`score.py --relative`). Higher is better.
    86	- Guards (from the same run): `moe/drop_fraction` max/mean not worse than the
    87	  paired control; `train/loss` at the window's last step equivalent to
    88	  control (bf16-noise band; 24k arms showed 1.29964-65 across four arms);
    89	  `window_complete` must be true.
    90	- Noise model: only ONE NVL72 rack exists, so there is no placement variance
    91	  (user, 2026-08-29) — residual noise is neighbor jobs, clocks/thermals, and
    92	  run-to-run scheduling jitter. Iteration 0 sizes it empirically: >=3 control
    93	  draws; the keep bar is max(0.15 MFU, 3 x the measured control sd). KEEP
    94	  decisions still use interleaved control/treatment draws (the control also
    95	  re-verifies the kept stack), and near-threshold deltas get additional
    96	  draws instead of a call. Periodic re-control draws guard against slow
    97	  drift across the campaign.
    98	- Fidelity gate for keeps: paired-window comparison; the loss guard compares
    99	  the FULL 15-step loss series against the paired control (max |delta|
   100	  within the bf16-noise band), not just the last step — data is
   101	  deterministic at a fixed restore step so the series pair pointwise. Any
   102	  keep that changes numerics beyond scheduling/layout gets flagged and needs
   103	  explicit reasoning about why the trajectory is unchanged.
   104

   107	Per user: only hypotheses that reach the rack count toward the 24-iteration

   109	whichever comes LAST. Iteration 0 = 30k-protocol baseline (control draw(s)).

   163	
   164	## Decision rules
   165	
   166	- keep: same-night pair shows treatment > control by >0.15 MFU with guards
   167	  clean → commit stays, becomes the new control for later iterations.
   168	- discard: delta <= noise or guards fail → revert commit.
   169	- crash/invalid: watchdog kill, preemption, incomplete window → arm is
   170	  invalid, does not consume the hypothesis; resubmit once before discarding.
   171	- Every keep is re-verified implicitly by later same-night controls (the
   172	  control arm always runs the current kept stack).
   173	
   174	## Review protocol
   175	
   176	Before rack time each experiment's design gets adversarial review from (a) a
   177	fable agent and (b) `codex exec` CLI, both against the pinned worktree
   178	(`codex exec` needs stdin closed; `--base` incompatible with a custom
   179	prompt). Findings addressed or refuted in writing in the logbook before
   180	submission. Reviews of iteration N+1 overlap with rack time of iteration N.

   660	
   661	
   662	def _tree_to_memory_kind(tree, memory_kind: str):
   663	    """Move named-sharded arrays to a JAX memory kind."""
   664	
   665	    def _move(leaf):
   666	        if not isinstance(leaf, jax.Array):
   667	            return leaf
   668	        sharding = jax.typeof(leaf).sharding
   669	        mesh = getattr(sharding, "mesh", None)
   670	        if mesh is None or len(getattr(mesh, "axis_names", ())) == 0:
   671	            if jax.sharding.get_abstract_mesh().empty:
   672	                return leaf
   673	            # Scalar optimizer metadata has no operand from which to inherit the active mesh.
   674	            # Bind it before changing memory kind so the initial and updated states have the
   675	            # same JIT input signature.
   676	            leaf = jax.sharding.reshard(leaf, P())
   677	            sharding = jax.typeof(leaf).sharding
   678	        return jax.device_put(leaf, sharding.with_memory_kind(memory_kind))
   679	
   680	    return jax.tree.map(_move, tree)
   681	
   682	
   683	def initial_state(
   684	    model_config: GrugModelConfig,
   685	    *,
   686	    optimizer: optax.GradientTransformation,
   687	    mp: jmp.Policy,
   688	    key: PRNGKeyArray,
   689	    ema_beta: float | None,
   690	    offload_opt_state: bool = False,
   691	    master_param_mode: MasterParamMode = MasterParamMode.DEVICE,
   692	) -> GrugTrainState:
   693	    initialized_params = Transformer.init(model_config, key=key)
   694	    num_moe_layers = model_config.num_layers
   695	    if master_param_mode == MasterParamMode.FP32_PINNED_HOST:
   696	        master_params = _FP32_POLICY.cast_to_param(initialized_params)
   697	        params = mp.cast_to_param(master_params)
   698	        opt_state = optimizer.init(master_params)
   699	        master_params = _tree_to_memory_kind(master_params, "pinned_host")
   700	    else:
   701	        params = mp.cast_to_param(initialized_params)
   702	        master_params = None
   703	        opt_state = optimizer.init(params)
   704	    if offload_opt_state:
   705	        opt_state = _tree_to_memory_kind(opt_state, "pinned_host")
   706	    return GrugTrainState(
   707	        step=jnp.array(0, dtype=jnp.int32),
   708	        params=params,
   709	        master_params=master_params,
   710	        opt_state=opt_state,
   711	        ema_params=params if ema_beta is not None else None,
   712	        pending_qb_betas=jnp.zeros((num_moe_layers, model_config.num_experts)),
   713	    )
   714	
   715	
   716	def _drop_metrics(
   717	    dropped_assignments: jax.Array,
   718	    sender_dropped_assignments: jax.Array,
   719	    receiver_dropped_assignments: jax.Array,
   720	    *,
   721	    batch_size: int,
   722	    sequence_length: int,
   723	    top_k: int,
   724	    num_layers: int,
   725	) -> dict[str, int | float]:
   726	    # Per-layer int32 counts summed over layers in int64 on the host: the global totals exceed int32 at
   727	    # large batch (jax_enable_x64 is off, so an in-device sum would overflow), and float32 would round them.
   728	    def _sum_int64(per_layer: jax.Array) -> int:
   729	        return int(np.asarray(per_layer).astype(np.int64).sum())

   108	
   109	def restore_grug_state_from_checkpoint(
   110	    state: StateT,
   111	    *,
   112	    checkpoint_search_paths: Sequence[str],
   113	    load_checkpoint_setting: bool | None,
   114	    mesh: jax.sharding.Mesh | None,
   115	    allow_partial: bool,
   116	    template_for_candidate: Callable[[str], StateT] | None = None,
   117	    _load_fn: Callable[..., StateT] = load_checkpoint,
   118	) -> StateT:
   119	    """Restore the newest loadable checkpoint under the search paths, else return ``state``.
   120	
   121	    ``template_for_candidate`` maps a candidate path to the exemplar it is read with. An exemplar
   122	    is the pytree whose leaves name what restore loads. Pass the hook when the state layout
   123	    differs between checkpoint generations, so each candidate is read with an exemplar matching
   124	    its layout; ``None`` reads every candidate with ``state``. Raising ``FileNotFoundError`` from
   125	    the hook skips to the next-older candidate; any other exception aborts the restore, so a hook
   126	    can refuse a checkpoint outright rather than quietly fall past it.
   127	    """
   128	    if not checkpoint_search_paths:
   129	        if load_checkpoint_setting:
   130	            raise FileNotFoundError("load_checkpoint=True but no checkpoint search paths are configured.")
   131	        return state
   132	
   133	    if load_checkpoint_setting is False:
   134	        return state
   135	
   136	    candidates = _checkpoint_candidates(checkpoint_search_paths)
   137	    # A bare search root is always a candidate, so this is what separates "nothing has been
   138	    # written yet" from "checkpoints exist here".
   139	    written = [candidate for candidate in candidates if candidate not in checkpoint_search_paths]
   140	    last_error: FileNotFoundError | None = None
   141	
   142	    for candidate in candidates:
   143	        try:
   144	            loaded = _load_candidate_state(
   145	                state=state if template_for_candidate is None else template_for_candidate(candidate),
   146	                candidate=candidate,
   147	                mesh=mesh,
   148	                allow_partial=allow_partial,
   149	                load_fn=_load_fn,
   150	            )
   151	            barrier_sync_named(RESTORE_COMPLETE_BARRIER, timeout=RESTORE_BARRIER_TIMEOUT)
   152	            if candidate not in checkpoint_search_paths:
   153	                logger.info("Loaded checkpoint from %s while searching %s", candidate, checkpoint_search_paths)
   154	            return loaded
   155	        except FileNotFoundError as exc:
   156	            last_error = exc
   157	            logger.warning(
   158	                "Checkpoint candidate %s could not be loaded (%s). Trying an older checkpoint.", candidate, exc
   159	            )
   160	
   161	    search_path_summary = ", ".join(checkpoint_search_paths)
   162	    if load_checkpoint_setting is True:
   163	        attempted = ", ".join(candidates)
   164	        if last_error is None:
   165	            raise FileNotFoundError(f"Could not find checkpoint under any of: {search_path_summary}")
   166	        raise FileNotFoundError(
   167	            f"Could not load a checkpoint from search paths {search_path_summary}. Attempted: {attempted}"
   168	        ) from last_error
   169	
   170	    if written:
   171	        # An optional resume that finds checkpoints and reads none of them is a failed resume, not
   172	        # a first launch. Restarting at step 0 would overwrite them and still report a plausible MFU.
   173	        raise FileNotFoundError(
   174	            f"{len(written)} checkpoint(s) exist under {search_path_summary} but none could be loaded: "
   175	            f"{', '.join(written)}"
   176	        ) from last_error
   177	
   178	    logger.info("No checkpoint under %s. Starting from scratch.", checkpoint_search_paths)
   179	    return state
   180	
   181	
   182	def init_weights_only_from_checkpoint(
   183	    state: GrugStateT,
   184	    checkpoint_path: str,
   185	    *,

    26	# The barrier runs one clock, started by the first rank to arrive, so this bounds the spread
    27	# between arrivals rather than the length of a restore. A gang whose object-store caches are only
    28	# partly warm spreads widest, since re-reading a checkpoint is an order of magnitude faster than
    29	# reading it cold, and killing a stalled gang is what leaves a fleet in that state. Expiring
    30	# aborts the attempt for the scheduler to retry, rather than holding every rank behind one that
    31	# never arrives.

    51	def _get_fs_and_plain_path(path: str) -> tuple[AbstractFileSystem, str]:
    52	    fs, _, (plain_path,) = fsspec.get_fs_token_paths(path)
    53	    return fs, plain_path
    54	
    55	
    56	def _checkpoint_candidates(checkpoint_search_paths: Sequence[str]) -> list[str]:
    57	    candidates: list[tuple[int, str, str]] = []
    58	    for search_path in checkpoint_search_paths:
    59	        candidates.extend(_scan_checkpoint_root(search_path))
    60	
    61	    candidates.sort(key=lambda item: (item[0], item[1]), reverse=True)
    62	    ordered_candidates = [candidate for _, _, candidate in candidates]
    63	
    64	    for search_path in checkpoint_search_paths:
    65	        if search_path not in ordered_candidates:
    66	            ordered_candidates.append(search_path)
    67	    return ordered_candidates
    68	
    69	
    70	def _scan_checkpoint_root(root_path: str) -> list[tuple[int, str, str]]:
    71	    """Scan a single root path and return (step, timestamp, path) tuples."""
    72	    fs, plain_path = _get_fs_and_plain_path(root_path)
    73	    base_path_protocol = urllib.parse.urlparse(root_path).scheme
    74	
    75	    def maybe_unstrip_protocol(path: str) -> str:
    76	        if base_path_protocol != "" and urllib.parse.urlparse(path).scheme == "":
    77	            return f"{base_path_protocol}://{path}"
    78	        return path
    79	
    80	    checkpoint_dirs = [maybe_unstrip_protocol(d) for d in fs.glob(os.path.join(plain_path, "*")) if fs.isdir(d)]
    81	    checkpoint_dirs.append(root_path)
    82	
    83	    results: list[tuple[int, str, str]] = []
    84	    for candidate in checkpoint_dirs:
    85	        metadata_path = os.path.join(candidate, "metadata.json")
    86	        if not fs.exists(metadata_path):
    87	            continue
    88	
    89	        try:
    90	            with fs.open(metadata_path) as metadata_in:
    91	                metadata = json.load(metadata_in)
    92	        except Exception:
    93	            logger.warning("Skipping unreadable checkpoint metadata at %s", metadata_path, exc_info=True)
    94	            continue
    95	
    96	        step = metadata.get("step")
    97	        try:
    98	            step_num = int(step)
    99	        except (TypeError, ValueError):
   100	            step_num = -1
   101	
   102	        timestamp = metadata.get("timestamp")
   103	        timestamp_key = str(timestamp) if timestamp is not None else ""
   104	        results.append((step_num, timestamp_key, candidate))
   105	
   106	    return results

codex
The latest code fixes the two immediate CLI failures, but it introduces/retains a more dangerous silent path: an explicit restore path is optional at the trainer layer. If the checkpoint is absent or unloadable in the “no checkpoints found” case, training can start at step 0; both the watchdog and `score.py --relative` will accept and score steps 5–19 as if this were a step-30000 restore. I’m doing the final consistency pass now, including the profile-tail versus early-release conflict and the current dirty-tree gate.

  1288	    def _timeout_decisions(self, timeout_rows: Sequence[Row], now_ms: int) -> list[TerminalDecision]:
  1289	        """Turn execution-timeout rows from the snapshot into TIMEOUT decisions.
  1290	
  1291	        A row becomes a decision only once its attempt's
  1292	        ``started_at_ms + timeout_ms`` is already in the past.
  1293	        """
  1294	        decisions: list[TerminalDecision] = []
  1295	        for row in timeout_rows:
  1296	            if row.started_at_ms.epoch_ms() + int(row.timeout_ms) > now_ms:
  1297	                continue
  1298	            logger.warning("Task %s exceeded execution timeout, killing", row.task_id)
  1299	            decisions.append(
  1300	                TerminalDecision(
  1301	                    kind=TerminalKind.TIMEOUT,
  1302	                    task_id=row.task_id,
  1303	                    reason="Execution timeout exceeded",
  1304	                )
  1305	            )
  1306	        return decisions

   171	        logger.warning("Falling back to default Iris job user 'root': local user was empty")
   172	        return "root"
   173	    return resolved

   335	class WandbConfig(TrackerConfig):
   336	    """
   337	    Configuration for wandb.
   338	    """
   339	
   340	    entity: Optional[str] = None  # An entity is a username or team name where you send runs
   341	    project: Optional[str] = "levanter"  # The name of the project where you are sending the enw run.
   342	    name: Optional[str] = None  # A short display name for this run, which is how you'll identify this run in the UI.
   343	    tags: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)  # Will populate the list of tags on this run in the UI.
   344	    id: Optional[str] = None  # A unique ID for this run, used for resuming. It must be unique in the project
   345	    group: Optional[str] = None  # Specify a group to organize individual runs into a larger experiment.
   346	    mode: Optional[str] = None  # Can be "online", "offline" or "disabled". If None, it will be whatever W&B decides.
   347	    resume: Optional[Union[bool, str]] = "allow"
   348	    """
   349	    Set the resume behavior. Options: "allow", "must", "never", "auto" or None.
   350	    By default, if the new run has the same ID as a previous run, this run overwrites that data.
   351	    Please refer to [init](https://docs.wandb.ai/ref/python/init) and [resume](https://docs.wandb.ai/guides/runs/resuming)
   352	    document for more details.
   353	    """
   354	
   355	    save_code: Union[bool, str] = True
   356	    """If string, will save code from that directory. If True, will attempt to sniff out the main directory (since we
   357	    typically don't run from the root of the repo)."""
   358	
   359	    save_xla_dumps: bool = False
   360	    """If True, will save the XLA code to wandb (as configured by XLA_FLAGS). This is useful for debugging."""
   361	
   362	    replicate_path: Optional[str] = None
   363	    """If set, write config and summary to this path (local or GCS) on finish()."""
   364	
   365	    background: bool = True
   366	    """If True (default), forward all log calls through a background thread that catches
   367	    exceptions from W&B. This keeps long-running training jobs alive when W&B is
   368	    unreachable, runs out of storage quota, or returns transient errors. Set to False
   369	    only if you need synchronous, fail-fast behavior (e.g. from tests)."""
   370	
   371	    background_max_queue_size: int = 10000
   372	    """Max number of pending tracker calls. If exceeded, additional calls are dropped
   373	    with a rate-limited warning rather than blocking the trainer."""
   374

   380	    before abandoning it. wandb's own service-teardown atexit hook is unbounded, which can
   381	    hold a slice past the JAX distributed shutdown-barrier deadline and SIGABRT a

   385	        if run_id is not None and self.id is not None and run_id != self.id:

   222	    def job_preemption_policy(self, job_id: JobName) -> int:
   223	        """Resolve the effective preemption policy.
   224	
   225	        Defaults: single-task jobs → ``TERMINATE_CHILDREN``, multi-task →
   226	        ``PRESERVE_CHILDREN``.
   227	        """
   228	        cfg = self._snapshot.job_configs.get(job_id)
   229	        if cfg is None:
   230	            return job_pb2.JOB_PREEMPTION_POLICY_TERMINATE_CHILDREN
   231	        policy = cfg.preemption_policy
   232	        if policy != job_pb2.JOB_PREEMPTION_POLICY_UNSPECIFIED:
   233	            return policy
   234	        if cfg.num_tasks <= 1:
   235	            return job_pb2.JOB_PREEMPTION_POLICY_TERMINATE_CHILDREN
   236	        return job_pb2.JOB_PREEMPTION_POLICY_PRESERVE_CHILDREN

   159	def _terminal_job_reason(overlay: Overlay, job_id: JobName, terminal_state: int) -> str:
   160	    return overlay.job_delta_error(job_id) or TERMINAL_STATE_FALLBACK_REASONS.get(terminal_state, "Job finalized")

   163	def _finalize_terminal_job(overlay: Overlay, job_id: JobName, terminal_state: int, now_ms: int) -> None:
   164	    """Kill remaining tasks and optionally cascade to children when a job goes terminal."""
   165	    reason = _terminal_job_reason(overlay, job_id, terminal_state)
   166	    _kill_non_terminal_tasks(
   167	        overlay,
   168	        job_id,
   169	        reason,
   170	        now_ms,
   171	        action_reason=TaskActionReason.JOB_FINALIZED_TASK_KILLED,
   172	    )
   173	    should_cascade = True
   174	    if terminal_state != job_pb2.JOB_STATE_SUCCEEDED:
   175	        policy = overlay.job_preemption_policy(job_id)
   176	        should_cascade = policy == job_pb2.JOB_PREEMPTION_POLICY_TERMINATE_CHILDREN
   177	    if should_cascade:
   178	        _cascade_to_children(
   179	            overlay,
   180	            job_id,
   181	            now_ms,
   182	            PARENT_JOB_TERMINATED_REASON,
   183	            action_reason=TaskActionReason.PARENT_JOB_TERMINATED,
   184	        )
   185

/etc/profiles/per-user/marin/bin/bash -lc "nl -ba autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/score.py; nl -ba autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/arm.sh | sed -n '1,120p'; nl -ba autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/watchdog.sh | sed -n '1,180p'" in /home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop

     1	# Copyright The Marin Authors
     2	# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
     3	
     4	"""Score one arm of the ragged EP64 tuning loop from its W&B history.
     5	
     6	The metric is mean ``throughput/mfu`` over a fixed step window. The window is fixed for the whole
     7	loop: comparing arms scored over different windows compares warmup fractions, not transports.
     8	
     9	Also emits the two guard quantities that come from the same run -- the worst drop fraction and the
    10	loss at the window's last step -- so a faster arm that quietly dropped tokens or changed the model
    11	cannot be recorded as an improvement.
    12	
    13	usage: uv run --with wandb python score.py <run-id> [--lo 5] [--hi 19]
    14	"""
    15	
    16	import argparse
    17	import json
    18	import statistics
    19	
    20	import wandb
    21	
    22	MFU_KEY = "throughput/mfu"
    23	LOSS_KEY = "train/loss"
    24	DROP_KEY = "moe/drop_fraction"
    25	TOKENS_KEY = "throughput/tokens_per_second"
    26	PEAK_KEY = "memory/peak_gib"
    27	
    28	
    29	def main() -> None:
    30	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    31	    parser.add_argument("run_id")
    32	    parser.add_argument("--lo", type=int, default=5, help="first scored step")
    33	    parser.add_argument("--hi", type=int, default=19, help="last scored step")
    34	    parser.add_argument("--project", default="marin-community/marin_moe")
    35	    parser.add_argument(
    36	        "--relative",
    37	        action="store_true",
    38	        help="Read lo/hi as offsets from the run's first logged step. A restored run resumes at "
    39	        "the checkpoint's step, so an absolute window would miss it entirely.",
    40	    )
    41	    args = parser.parse_args()
    42	
    43	    run = wandb.Api().run(f"{args.project}/{args.run_id}")
    44	    rows = list(run.scan_history())
    45	    steps = [int(r["_step"]) for r in rows if r.get("_step") is not None]
    46	    by_step: dict[int, dict] = {}
    47	    for row in rows:
    48	        step = row.get("_step")
    49	        if step is None:
    50	            continue
    51	        by_step.setdefault(int(step), {}).update({k: v for k, v in row.items() if v is not None})
    52	
    53	    first_step = min(by_step) if by_step else 0
    54	    lo, hi = (first_step + args.lo, first_step + args.hi) if args.relative else (args.lo, args.hi)
    55	
    56	    def series(key: str) -> list[float]:
    57	        return [float(by_step[s][key]) for s in sorted(by_step) if lo <= s <= hi and key in by_step[s]]
    58	
    59	    mfu = series(MFU_KEY)
    60	    drops = series(DROP_KEY)
    61	    peaks = series(PEAK_KEY)
    62	    losses = [(s, by_step[s][LOSS_KEY]) for s in sorted(by_step) if lo <= s <= hi and LOSS_KEY in by_step[s]]
    63	    tokens = series(TOKENS_KEY)
    64	
    65	    result = {
    66	        "run_id": args.run_id,
    67	        "state": run.state,
    68	        "max_step": max(steps) if steps else -1,
    69	        "window": [lo, hi],
    70	        "first_step": first_step,
    71	        "scored_points": len(mfu),
    72	        # A window with holes in it is not the window the loop agreed to compare, so say so loudly
    73	        # rather than averaging whatever happens to be there.
    74	        "window_complete": len(mfu) == hi - lo + 1,
    75	        "mfu_mean": round(statistics.fmean(mfu), 4) if mfu else None,
    76	        "mfu_stdev": round(statistics.stdev(mfu), 4) if len(mfu) > 1 else None,
    77	        "tokens_per_second_mean": round(statistics.fmean(tokens), 1) if tokens else None,
    78	        # Drops are an outcome here, not only a guard: the whole point of the ragged transport is
    79	        # moving tokens the pooled one clips, so a throughput win that costs drops is not a win.
    80	        # Engagement/confound telemetry: HloRematerialization flips on allocator-limit crossings
    81	        # (issue #8054's +9.08% artifact); a memory-moving arm whose peak did not move did not
    82	        # engage, and an MFU delta with a big peak move may be a remat-boundary crossing instead.
    83	        "peak_gib_max": round(max(peaks), 2) if peaks else None,
    84	        "drop_fraction_max": max(drops) if drops else None,
    85	        "drop_fraction_mean": round(statistics.fmean(drops), 6) if drops else None,
    86	        # Guard-series completeness: a killed run can leave the last rows of less-frequent keys
    87	        # unflushed, so a "last loss" alone can silently come from an earlier step. Emit the full
    88	        # series; the loop compares max |delta| against the paired control (data is deterministic
    89	        # across arms at a fixed restore step, so the series pair pointwise).
    90	        "drop_points": len(drops),
    91	        "loss_points": len(losses),
    92	        "loss_series": [[s, round(float(v), 6)] for s, v in losses],
    93	        "loss_last": float(losses[-1][1]) if losses else None,
    94	        "loss_last_step": losses[-1][0] if losses else None,
    95	    }
    96	    print(json.dumps(result, indent=2))
    97	
    98	
    99	if __name__ == "__main__":
   100	    main()
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Submit one hero EP64 arm for the 8753-mfu loop (research/mcwitt/8753-mfu-loop, #8753 head base).
     3	#
     4	# Protocol (fixed for every iteration so arms stay comparable; see DESIGN.md):
     5	#   - one NVL72 rack on cw-us-east-08a, PRODUCTION priority allowed with job runtime <1h
     6	#     (user 2026-08-28): ARM_TIMEOUT 3500s outer backstop, watchdog.sh COMPILE_CEILING=1800 +
     7	#     STEP_BUDGET=1200 inner caps (20-min post-compile cap)
     8	#   - restore from the live hero's step-30000 checkpoint, mixture data (trained-router routing);
     9	#     NUM_STEPS is the ABSOLUTE stop step (checkpoint step + window), never a relative count
    10	#   - no checkpoints, no eval; profiler off on SCORED arms (a profile tail on an otherwise-scored
    11	#     draw is allowed: PROFILE_STEPS>0 with PROFILE_START_STEP as an ABSOLUTE step past the
    12	#     scoring window, e.g. 30021)
    13	#   - no MARIN_PJRT_WHEEL overlay: the branch's uv.lock pins jax_cuda13_pjrt 0.11.1+marin.c9526e8c0272
    14	#   - JAX_COMPILATION_CACHE_DIR rotated per run id (clique-init deadlock dodge); a RESUBMITTED
    15	#     arm must use a fresh RID (and VERSION): reusing one merges W&B histories, reuses the
    16	#     leader-populated compile cache (clique-deadlock recipe), and can vacuously reuse artifacts
    17	#
    18	# usage: RID=<run-id> VERSION=<calver> [REPO=<worktree>] [EXTRA_LAUNCH_ARGS="..."] arm.sh
    19	set -euo pipefail
    20	
    21	: "${WANDB_API_KEY:?set WANDB_API_KEY}"
    22	RID="${RID:?set RID}"
    23	VERSION="${VERSION:?set VERSION -- bump it per arm or the artifact layer reuses the last run}"
    24	
    25	LOOP_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
    26	REPO="${REPO:-$(cd "${LOOP_DIR}/../.." && pwd)}"
    27	
    28	# Iris bundles the working tree, not HEAD: uncommitted edits (including another session writing
    29	# to a shared checkout) would ship silently and break arm attribution. The loop dir itself is
    30	# exempt -- it accumulates logs between commits.
    31	if [ -z "${ALLOW_DIRTY:-}" ] && git -C "$REPO" status --porcelain | grep -v "autoresearch/" | grep -q .; then
    32	  echo "refusing to submit from a dirty tree (set ALLOW_DIRTY=1 to override):" >&2
    33	  git -C "$REPO" status --porcelain | grep -v "autoresearch/" >&2
    34	  exit 1
    35	fi
    36	
    37	PRIORITY="${PRIORITY:-production}"
    38	ARM_TIMEOUT="${ARM_TIMEOUT:-3500}"
    39	NUM_STEPS="${NUM_STEPS:-30030}"
    40	TRAINING_DATA="${TRAINING_DATA:-mixture}"
    41	MASTER_PARAMS="${MASTER_PARAMS:-device}"
    42	MOE_IMPL="${MOE_IMPL:-ragged_all_to_all}"
    43	RESTORE_FROM="${RESTORE_FROM:-s3://marin-us-east-02a/marin/grug/hero-12d8b6f0-dee637/2026.08.19.2/checkpoints/step-30000}"
    44	RESTORE_ARGS=()
    45	if [ -n "$RESTORE_FROM" ]; then
    46	  RESTORE_ARGS=(--restore-from "$RESTORE_FROM")
    47	fi
    48	# The learning-rate schedule's length; fixed across arms so losses stay comparable.
    49	SCHEDULE_STEPS=4470000
    50	
    51	read -r -a EXTRA <<<"${EXTRA_LAUNCH_ARGS:-}"
    52	
    53	cd "$REPO"
    54	uv run iris --config lib/iris/config/marin.yaml job run \
    55	  --no-wait --enable-extra-resources \
    56	  --target-cluster cw-us-east-08a --priority "${PRIORITY}" \
    57	  --cpu 2 --memory 8GB --disk 32GB --timeout "${ARM_TIMEOUT}" \
    58	  --job-name "${RID}-coord" \
    59	  -e IRIS_USER mwittmann \
    60	  -e WANDB_API_KEY "${WANDB_API_KEY}" \
    61	  -e WANDB_PROJECT marin_moe \
    62	  -e MARIN_PREFIX s3://marin-us-east-02a/marin \
    63	  -e IRIS_PORT_JAX 32703 \
    64	  -e AWS_MAX_ATTEMPTS 25 -e AWS_RETRY_MODE adaptive \
    65	  -e JAX_COMPILATION_CACHE_DIR "s3://marin-us-east-02a/marin/tmp/ttl=30d/jaxcache/${RID}" \
    66	  -e XLA_FLAGS "${ARM_XLA_FLAGS:-}" \
    67	  -- python -m experiments.grug.moe_hero_ep.launch_diagnostics \
    68	     --run-id "${RID}" \
    69	     --dp-racks 1 --num-steps "${NUM_STEPS}" --schedule-steps "${SCHEDULE_STEPS}" \
    70	     --capacity-factor 1.15 \
    71	     --moe-implementation "${MOE_IMPL}" \
    72	     --processes-per-task 4 \
    73	     --master-params "${MASTER_PARAMS}" \
    74	     --training-data "${TRAINING_DATA}" \
    75	     --profile-steps "${PROFILE_STEPS:-0}" --profile-start-step "${PROFILE_START_STEP:-30021}" \
    76	     --watch-interval 0 --eval-every 0 \
    77	     --no-save-checkpoints \
    78	     "${RESTORE_ARGS[@]}" \
    79	     "${EXTRA[@]}" \
    80	     --version "${VERSION}" --run >"${LOOP_DIR}/${RID}-submit.log" 2>&1 \
    81	  || { echo "submit FAILED, tail of ${RID}-submit.log:" >&2; tail -20 "${LOOP_DIR}/${RID}-submit.log" >&2; exit 1; }
    82	
    83	printf '%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\t%s\n' \
    84	  "${RID}" "$(git -C "$REPO" rev-parse --short HEAD)" "${VERSION}" "${MOE_IMPL}" "${TRAINING_DATA}" \
    85	  "${MASTER_PARAMS}" "${SCHEDULE_STEPS}" "${ARM_TIMEOUT}" "${RESTORE_FROM:-none}" \
    86	  "${EXTRA_LAUNCH_ARGS:-none}" >> "${LOOP_DIR}/arms.tsv"
    87	
    88	echo "submitted ${RID} at ${PRIORITY} priority (timeout ${ARM_TIMEOUT}s, commit $(git -C "$REPO" rev-parse --short HEAD), data ${TRAINING_DATA}, extra: ${EXTRA_LAUNCH_ARGS:-none})"
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Out-of-process runtime cap, early release, and anomaly alerting for one arm.
     3	#
     4	# Exits (each prints one WATCHDOG marker line; the harness alerts on process exit, so exiting
     5	# fast IS the alert):
     6	#   1. Step budget: cancel STEP_BUDGET seconds after the first step this watch observes. Compile
     7	#      time varies run to run; an arm that pays a slow compile still gets its full measurement
     8	#      window, but an arm whose change makes *steps* slow gets no extra rack time.
     9	#   2. Compile ceiling: cancel if no step has appeared COMPILE_CEILING seconds after submission.
    10	#   3. Early release: cancel as soon as W&B shows the last step the scoring window needs.
    11	#   4. Preemption or task failure: `job describe` publishes failures= and preemptions= counters.
    12	#      Either going nonzero invalidates the measurement (a rescheduled gang recompiles and the
    13	#      step timings straddle the gap), so cancel immediately instead of letting the retry burn
    14	#      the ceiling.
    15	#   5. Coordinator death before the train job exists (e.g. a config guard raising at dispatch):
    16	#      previously this burned the whole compile ceiling; now it exits within one poll.
    17	# Iris's own --timeout on the coord job is the outer backstop; it must cover
    18	# COMPILE_CEILING + STEP_BUDGET. This watchdog runs in a separate process so a wedged CLI or a
    19	# lost session cannot defeat it.
    20	#
    21	# State polls are cheap (one describe RPC), so they run every POLL=20s; the W&B step query is
    22	# heavier and runs every third poll.
    23	#
    24	# usage: RID=<run-id> [STEP_BUDGET=900] [COMPILE_CEILING=1200] [SCORE_MAX_STEP=19] watchdog.sh
    25	set -uo pipefail
    26	
    27	RID="${RID:?set RID}"
    28	STEP_BUDGET="${STEP_BUDGET:-1200}"
    29	COMPILE_CEILING="${COMPILE_CEILING:-1800}"
    30	SCORE_MAX_STEP="${SCORE_MAX_STEP:-19}"
    31	POLL="${POLL:-20}"
    32	STEP_POLL_EVERY="${STEP_POLL_EVERY:-3}"
    33	
    34	LOOP_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
    35	REPO="$(cd "${LOOP_DIR}/../.." && pwd)"
    36	IRIS=(uv run iris --config lib/iris/config/marin.yaml)
    37	COORD="/mwittmann/${RID}-coord"
    38	TRAIN="${COORD}/grug-train-${RID}"
    39	start=$(date +%s)
    40	# A production submission may queue behind another job, so the compile ceiling starts when the
    41	# train job is first observed running, not at submission. QUEUE_CEILING bounds the pending phase.
    42	QUEUE_CEILING="${QUEUE_CEILING:-10800}"
    43	deadline=$(( start + QUEUE_CEILING ))
    44	run_seen=-1
    45	first_step=-1
    46	steps=-1
    47	tick=0
    48	
    49	cancel_arm() {  # cancel_arm <reason>
    50	  echo "$(date -u +%H:%M:%S) cancelling ${COORD}: $1"
    51	  # Cancel the train job by its own path as well as through the coordinator. Iris's --timeout may
    52	  # already have taken the coordinator down, and a cancel routed only through a dead parent is
    53	  # exactly how a 16-node child gets orphaned on the rack.
    54	  ( cd "$REPO" && timeout 300 "${IRIS[@]}" job cancel "$TRAIN" 2>&1 | tail -2 )
    55	  ( cd "$REPO" && timeout 300 "${IRIS[@]}" job cancel "$COORD" 2>&1 | tail -2 )
    56	}
    57	
    58	state_line_of() {  # state_line_of <job path> -> "state failures preemptions" (empty if absent)
    59	  ( cd "$REPO" && timeout 120 "${IRIS[@]}" job describe "$1" 2>/dev/null \
    60	      | sed -n 's/^State: \([a-z_]*\).*failures=\([0-9]*\).*preemptions=\([0-9]*\).*/\1 \2 \3/p' | head -1 )
    61	}
    62	
    63	max_step() {
    64	  ( cd "$REPO" && timeout 180 uv run --with wandb python - "$RID" <<'PY' 2>/dev/null
    65	import sys, wandb
    66	try:
    67	    run = wandb.Api().run(f"marin-community/marin_moe/{sys.argv[1]}")
    68	except Exception:
    69	    print(-2); raise SystemExit
    70	steps = [row.get("_step", -1) for row in run.scan_history(keys=["_step", "throughput/mfu"])]
    71	print(max(steps) if steps else -1)
    72	PY
    73	  )
    74	}
    75	
    76	while :; do
    77	  now=$(date +%s)
    78	  if [ "$now" -ge "$deadline" ]; then
    79	    if [ "${first_step:--1}" -ge 0 ] 2>/dev/null; then
    80	      cancel_arm "step budget ${STEP_BUDGET}s past first step reached"
    81	      echo "WATCHDOG hard-cap step-budget"
    82	    else
    83	      if [ "${run_seen:--1}" -ge 0 ]; then
    84	        cancel_arm "no step within the ${COMPILE_CEILING}s compile ceiling"
    85	        echo "WATCHDOG hard-cap compile-ceiling"
    86	      else
    87	        cancel_arm "still queued after ${QUEUE_CEILING}s"
    88	        echo "WATCHDOG hard-cap queue-ceiling"
    89	      fi
    90	    fi
    91	    exit 0
    92	  fi
    93	
    94	  read -r train_state train_failures train_preemptions <<<"$(state_line_of "$TRAIN")" || true
    95	  if [ -z "${train_state:-}" ]; then
    96	    # No train job yet: the coordinator is either still dispatching or died before dispatch.
    97	    read -r coord_state coord_failures coord_preemptions <<<"$(state_line_of "$COORD")" || true
    98	    case "${coord_state:-}" in
    99	      succeeded|killed|cancelled|*failed)
   100	        cancel_arm "coordinator ${coord_state} before the train job existed"
   101	        echo "WATCHDOG coord-terminal state=${coord_state} failures=${coord_failures:-?}"
   102	        exit 0;;
   103	    esac
   104	  fi
   105	  if [ "${run_seen:--1}" -lt 0 ] && [ "${train_state:-}" = "running" ]; then
   106	    # Iris can report a gang "running" while another job still holds the rack; the W&B run is
   107	    # only created once distributed init actually completes, so gate the compile clock on it.
   108	    probe="$(max_step)"
   109	    if [ "${probe:--2}" -ge -1 ] 2>/dev/null; then
   110	      run_seen=$now
   111	      deadline=$(( now + COMPILE_CEILING ))
   112	      echo "$(date -u +%H:%M:%S) gang up (wandb run exists), compile ceiling ${COMPILE_CEILING}s starts now"
   113	    fi
   114	  fi
   115	  if [ "${train_preemptions:-0}" -gt 0 ] 2>/dev/null; then
   116	    cancel_arm "train job preempted (preemptions=${train_preemptions})"
   117	    echo "WATCHDOG preempted train=${train_state} preemptions=${train_preemptions} step=${steps}"
   118	    exit 0
   119	  fi
   120	  if [ "${train_failures:-0}" -gt 0 ] 2>/dev/null; then
   121	    cancel_arm "train task failed (failures=${train_failures})"
   122	    echo "WATCHDOG task-failed train=${train_state} failures=${train_failures} step=${steps}"
   123	    exit 0
   124	  fi
   125	  case "${train_state:-}" in
   126	    succeeded|killed|cancelled|*failed)
   127	      echo "WATCHDOG terminal train=${train_state} step=${steps}"; exit 0;;
   128	  esac
   129	
   130	  if [ $(( tick % STEP_POLL_EVERY )) -eq 0 ]; then
   131	    steps="$(max_step)"
   132	    echo "$(date -u +%H:%M:%S) t=$(( now - start ))s train=${train_state:-none} step=${steps}"
   133	    # A restored arm resumes at the checkpoint's step, so progress is counted from the first step
   134	    # this watch actually observed rather than from zero.
   135	    if [ "${first_step:--1}" -lt 0 ] && [ "${steps:--1}" -ge 0 ] 2>/dev/null; then
   136	      first_step="$steps"
   137	      deadline=$(( now + STEP_BUDGET ))
   138	      echo "$(date -u +%H:%M:%S) first observed step=${first_step}, step budget ${STEP_BUDGET}s starts now"
   139	    fi
   140	    if [ "${first_step:--1}" -ge 0 ] 2>/dev/null && [ $(( steps - first_step )) -ge "$SCORE_MAX_STEP" ]; then
   141	      cancel_arm "scored through step ${steps} (${SCORE_MAX_STEP} past first)"
   142	      echo "WATCHDOG scored step=${steps} first=${first_step}"
   143	      exit 0
   144	    fi
   145	  fi
   146	  tick=$(( tick + 1 ))
   147	  sleep "$POLL"
   148	done

exec
/etc/profiles/per-user/marin/bin/bash -lc "nl -ba autoresearch/loop-260828-8753mfu/DESIGN.md | sed -n '1,220p'; nl -ba experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/launch_diagnostics.py | sed -n '55,220p'; nl -ba experiments/grug/moe_hero_ep/launch_diagnostics.py | sed -n '290,535p'" in /home/marin/projects/marin-mfu-loop

     1	# Campaign: ragged EP hero MFU loop (loop-260828-8753mfu)
     2	
     3	Goal: raise hero MFU beyond the #8753 stack's frontier without fidelity loss
     4	(no added quantization; loss trajectory equivalent or better) and without
     5	breaking hero-checkpoint compatibility (every keep must hot-swap into the live
     6	hero run; checkpoint migration OK, restore from hero checkpoints must work).
     7	
     8	Cost model (user, 2026-08-29): rack capacity is use-it-or-lose-it — failed or
     9	extra arms are cheap (stay courteous to other rack users). The expensive
    10	failure is a WRONG CONCLUSION: being sent in a bad direction, or prematurely
    11	closing a promising one, because a flawed experiment misled the loop. Review
    12	cycles and arm design optimize for inferential validity first, rack frugality
    13	second. Concretely: every treatment arm pre-declares an ENGAGEMENT CHECK
    14	(evidence the lever actually engaged — a kernel name in a profile, an echoed
    15	flag, an HLO property, or a metric that cannot move unless the lever did);
    16	"no effect" results are only recorded as dead after engagement is confirmed;
    17	near-threshold results get more draws rather than a coin-flip call.
    18	
    19	## Base
    20	
    21	- Branch `research/mcwitt/8753-mfu-loop` @ 04026e94fb = PR #8753 head
    22	  (`mcwitt/8317-hero-step-optims`, based on xla-fork/#8684).
    23	- Contents: offload_carry remat + latency-hiding scheduler (forced
    24	  overlap-limit 1), bitcast pair-interleave, FA4 128x64 wide forward tile as
    25	  `gpu_fa4_cute_wide` (landed by implementation name; dispatch has passed unit
    26	  tests but has NOT yet run on a rack — the iteration-0 control validates it).
    27	- PJRT wheel: `jax_cuda13_pjrt-0.11.1+marin.c9526e8c0272` pinned in
    28	  pyproject/uv.lock (GitHub release, marin-xla-pjrt-20260828). No wheel
    29	  overlay in arms.
    30	- Reference numbers: 24k-restore same-night pair (2026-08-28): control (#8753
    31	  stack sans tile) 23.06/23.05, +tile 23.32/23.37 MFU. 30k-restore cross-ref:
    32	  plain-#8684 gate 22.31 median. Protocols are not comparable; this campaign
    33	  re-baselines at 30k.
    34	
    35	## Fixed protocol (every arm)
    36	
    37	- One NVL72 rack, cw-us-east-08a, `experiments.grug.moe_hero_ep.launch_diagnostics`,
    38	  one arm on the rack at a time.
    39	- Restore from the latest hero permanent checkpoint
    40	  `s3://marin-us-east-02a/marin/grug/hero-12d8b6f0-dee637/2026.08.19.2/checkpoints/step-30000`
    41	  (verified complete: manifest.json present). `--num-steps 30030` (ABSOLUTE
    42	  stop step: checkpoint step + 30; `--num-steps 30` exits vacuously right
    43	  after restore).
    44	- Mixture data (trained-router routing), cf 1.15, `ragged_all_to_all`,
    45	  `--master-params device` (this branch renamed the master-less mode
    46	  `disabled` -> `device`; in-process migration reads a master-bearing
    47	  checkpoint's fp32 master directly into device params), schedule-steps
    48	  4470000, no checkpoints/evals. Profiler off on scored arms EXCEPT an
    49	  allowed profile tail: `PROFILE_STEPS` few, `PROFILE_START_STEP` ABSOLUTE
    50	  (e.g. 30021) so it lands past the scoring window — profiler start_step is
    51	  a global step; a relative value like 5 silently never fires on a restore.
    52	- `JAX_COMPILATION_CACHE_DIR` rotated per run id (clique-init deadlock dodge)
    53	  → every arm cold-compiles ~9.5 min; pre-phase (env sync + restore +
    54	  migration) ~19 min cold, less when the object cache is warm.
    55	- Priority: production allowed (user 2026-08-28) with job runtime capped
    56	  under 1 hour: iris `--timeout 3500`. Watchdog: COMPILE_CEILING=1800 from
    57	  first W&B-run existence, STEP_BUDGET=1200 (20-min post-compile cap, user),
    58	  early release at +19 steps, cancel on preemption/failure/coord death;
    59	  QUEUE_CEILING bounds pending time (queueing doesn't count against the 1h).
    60	- SERIALIZATION: one arm on the rack at a time, and arm N+1 is submitted only
    61	  after arm N's watchdog exits — the coordinator's iris `--timeout 3500`
    62	  clock starts when the coordinator starts (it schedules instantly and waits
    63	  on its child), so an arm queued behind another arm burns its 1h budget in
    64	  the queue and gets killed just as its train job starts.
    65	- RESUBMISSION: an invalid arm (watchdog kill, preemption, incomplete
    66	  window) is resubmitted with a FRESH RID and VERSION, never reused — a
    67	  reused RID merges W&B histories (silent window mixing), reuses the
    68	  leader-populated compile cache (the documented clique-init deadlock
    69	  recipe), and can vacuously reuse the artifact layer.
    70	- Hero liveness: the live hero is NOT currently training (step-30000 is the
    71	  newest permanent checkpoint; no hero job in iris). Before each arm, check
    72	  the rack queue; if the hero ever reappears on this cluster, pause the
    73	  campaign and reassess priorities rather than contending with it.
    74	- Rack etiquette: queue arms back-to-back while work is pending; do not
    75	  submit anything if the loop is blocked >1h on analysis.
    76	- Timestamp bookkeeping (calibration): for each arm record W&B-run-created
    77	  -> first-step wall time. The compile ceiling starts at run creation, which
    78	  is BEFORE restore + master migration + compile; a ceiling kill on a cold
    79	  arm is not evidence against the arm until these timings say the ceiling
    80	  actually fits the work.
    81	
    82	## Metric and guards
    83	
    84	- Metric: mean `throughput/mfu` over scored steps +5..+19 relative to the
    85	  first logged step (`score.py --relative`). Higher is better.
    86	- Guards (from the same run): `moe/drop_fraction` max/mean not worse than the
    87	  paired control; `train/loss` at the window's last step equivalent to
    88	  control (bf16-noise band; 24k arms showed 1.29964-65 across four arms);
    89	  `window_complete` must be true.
    90	- Noise model: only ONE NVL72 rack exists, so there is no placement variance
    91	  (user, 2026-08-29) — residual noise is neighbor jobs, clocks/thermals, and
    92	  run-to-run scheduling jitter. Iteration 0 sizes it empirically: >=3 control
    93	  draws; the keep bar is max(0.15 MFU, 3 x the measured control sd). KEEP
    94	  decisions still use interleaved control/treatment draws (the control also
    95	  re-verifies the kept stack), and near-threshold deltas get additional
    96	  draws instead of a call. Periodic re-control draws guard against slow
    97	  drift across the campaign.
    98	- Fidelity gate for keeps: paired-window comparison; the loss guard compares
    99	  the FULL 15-step loss series against the paired control (max |delta|
   100	  within the bf16-noise band), not just the last step — data is
   101	  deterministic at a fixed restore step so the series pair pointwise. Any
   102	  keep that changes numerics beyond scheduling/layout gets flagged and needs
   103	  explicit reasoning about why the trajectory is unchanged.
   104	
   105	## Iteration accounting
   106	
   107	Per user: only hypotheses that reach the rack count toward the 24-iteration
   108	floor; review-killed ideas do not. Campaign runs 24 rack iterations or 24 h,
   109	whichever comes LAST. Iteration 0 = 30k-protocol baseline (control draw(s)).
   110	
   111	## Hypothesis ledger (priors from prior campaigns; refresh with a new profile)
   112	
   113	Excluded by constraint: fp8 dispatch wire #7665, MXFP8 (quantization);
   114	anything breaking hero restore.
   115	
   116	- H0 baseline+validation: >=3 control draws at 30k to establish the metric
   117	  and size noise. Draw 2 carries a profile tail (PROFILE_STEPS=3,
   118	  PROFILE_START_STEP=30021, past the scoring window) that (a) positively
   119	  confirms the `gpu_fa4_cute_wide` 128x64 kernel engaged on-rack — its
   120	  first rack outing, and cross-night MFU deltas cannot confirm dispatch —
   121	  and (b) refreshes the lever-sourcing profile (H1 folded in: no separate
   122	  profile arm). If the tail perturbs draw 2's scored window relative to
   123	  draws 1/3, drop draw 2 from the noise estimate.
   124	- H1 profile refresh: FOLDED INTO H0 draw 2 (see above); prior profile
   125	  (dk-hero, pre-offload) showed marshal 2.55 s as top lever and a dense-MLP
   126	  leg that turned out to be architecture (2 shared experts + remat) — both
   127	  priors need re-confirmation on the offload/LHS/tile stack.
   128	- H2 marshal-step levers (SO-H2): reduce the ~2.55 s marshal leg — kernel or
   129	  layout changes in the dispatch/marshal path. Needs H1 to confirm the leg
   130	  survived the offload/LHS stack.
   131	- H3 shared-expert/dense leg scheduling: overlap the shared-expert compute
   132	  with ragged transport more aggressively; profile-informed.
   133	- H4 capacity-factor titration at the restored router: hero drops at cf 1.15
   134	  are ~5e-5 — far under any budget; cf 1.10/1.05 shrinks transport+GEMM
   135	  buffers. GATE (review finding): a cf keep changes training dynamics and
   136	  its drop cost varies with data over the whole run — a 15-step window
   137	  cannot bound it. Pre-declared acceptance: drop_fraction_max <= 1e-3 in
   138	  the window AND loss series within the control band; even then a cf keep
   139	  is flagged for explicit user sign-off before hero promotion, and the
   140	  prior loop note (2026-08-12: CF moves are frontier-walking) is quoted in
   141	  the decision.
   142	- H5 overlap-limit re-titration: DEAD AS A FLAG ARM on this base —
   143	  `offload_carry` force-sets overlap-limit 1 non-overridably and
   144	  `_apply_hero_ep_runtime_defaults` silently strips conflicting
   145	  ARM_XLA_FLAGS (a flag-based treatment would run as an exact control).
   146	  Testing overlap>1 means changing the remat mode, which is a different
   147	  hypothesis (and the documented silent-corruption recipe with LHS +
   148	  offload — see activation-offload-lhs-overlap-race).
   149	- H6 slop-factor raise (user suggestion 2026-08-29, HIGH priority — cheap,
   150	  code-supported mechanism): `--xla_gpu_memory_limit_slop_factor=85` in
   151	  train.py flag_defaults was calibrated for the pre-offload posture (default
   152	  95 sized the temp arena at 125.7 GiB > the 120.2 GiB the pool can serve;
   153	  85 -> 113.6 GiB), and the comment itself says a smaller arena makes
   154	  HloRematerialization recompute more of the step. The carry offload since
   155	  dropped peak from ~138 to ~116.5 GiB, so the remat pressure the arena cap
   156	  creates may now be pure waste. The flag IS overridable via inherited
   157	  XLA_FLAGS (verified: only overlap-limit and ragged-required flags are
   158	  forced), so treatment = ARM_XLA_FLAGS with slop 90 (arena ~120.2, at the
   159	  documented bound; ladder to 88 on OOM, 92/95 on green+gain). Fidelity:
   160	  remat changes recompute scheduling only. Engagement check: peak-HBM
   161	  metric must RISE vs control (less remat holds more live); an unchanged
   162	  peak means the flag did not engage regardless of the MFU reading. Failure
   163	  mode is a named first-step NCCL allocation abort — cheap crash, ladder
   164	  down.
   165	- H7 attn-offload re-add: 23.14 but 3x stdev and the host allocation lands as
   166	  one contiguous 72.56 GiB block regardless of HOST_MEM_LIMIT_GB — ledger
   167	  says dead unless pod memory grows.
   168	- H8 marin_ep_mgpu_fused transport on this stack: mok-goal showed 22.83 vs
   169	  22.30 control at 18k restore with 16x fewer drops, but needs the kmax128
   170	  wheel (conflicts with the pinned c9526e wheel) and the backend isn't on
   171	  this branch. Big integration; park unless transport dominates H1 profile
   172	  and cheaper levers dry up.
   173	- Dead-lever ledger (do not re-run without new evidence): PGLE (null),
   174	  command buffers at hero shape (+7.5 GiB, negative), FA4 backward tile
   175	  (hard-locked 64x64), 128x128 forward tile (2.5x regression), fused shared
   176	  experts (~0), small-param FSDP (negative), unchaining per-chunk a2a
   177	  buffers (-0.13), NVLS/SHARP env (null), fa4-lse primal output (moot: XLA
   178	  already device-saves FA4 out/lse).
   179	
   180	## Decision rules
   181	
   182	- keep: same-night pair shows treatment > control by >0.15 MFU with guards
   183	  clean → commit stays, becomes the new control for later iterations.
   184	- discard: delta <= noise or guards fail → revert commit.
   185	- crash/invalid: watchdog kill, preemption, incomplete window → arm is
   186	  invalid, does not consume the hypothesis; resubmit once before discarding.
   187	- Every keep is re-verified implicitly by later same-night controls (the
   188	  control arm always runs the current kept stack).
   189	
   190	## Review protocol
   191	
   192	Before rack time each experiment's design gets adversarial review from (a) a
   193	fable agent and (b) `codex exec` CLI, both against the pinned worktree
   194	(`codex exec` needs stdin closed; `--base` incompatible with a custom
   195	prompt). Findings addressed or refuted in writing in the logbook before
   196	submission. Reviews of iteration N+1 overlap with rack time of iteration N.
    55	    _compute_flops,
    56	    run_grug,
    57	)
    58	
    59	DEFAULT_HERO_STEPS = 25
    60	HERO_CHECKPOINT_INTERVAL = timedelta(minutes=15)
    61	
    62	
    63	def build_diagnostic_run(
    64	    *,
    65	    run_id: str,
    66	    restore_from: str | None = None,
    67	    dp_racks: int,
    68	    num_steps: int,
    69	    schedule_steps: int | None = None,
    70	    seed: int = 0,
    71	    batch_size: int = HERO_EP_BATCH_SIZE,
    72	    num_experts: int | None = None,
    73	    num_experts_per_token: int | None = None,
    74	    intermediate_dim: int | None = None,
    75	    capacity_factor: float | None = None,
    76	    latent_dim: int | None = None,
    77	    moe_implementation: str | None = None,
    78	    master_param_mode: MasterParamMode = HERO_MASTER_PARAM_MODE,
    79	    processes_per_task: int = HERO_PROCESSES_PER_TASK,
    80	    eval_every: int = 0,
    81	    save_checkpoints: bool = False,
    82	    checkpoint_interval: timedelta = HERO_CHECKPOINT_INTERVAL,
    83	    checkpoint_path: str | None = None,
    84	    checkpoint_debug: CheckpointDebugConfig | None = None,
    85	    watch_interval: int = HERO_WATCH_INTERVAL,
    86	    watch_mode: WatchMode = WatchMode.INLINE,
    87	    profile_steps: int = 0,
    88	    profile_start_step: int = 5,
    89	    training_data_mode: TrainingDataMode = TrainingDataMode.MIXTURE,
    90	    version: str | None = None,
    91	) -> ArtifactStep[HeroThroughputResult]:
    92	    """Build a bounded diagnostic run for the production EP64 hero recipe.
    93	
    94	    The overrides sweep expert count, expert width, routed top-k, and routing capacity from the
    95	    hero spec. They keep the hidden dimension, so the compute-scaled optimizer values stay
    96	    comparable across a sweep. ``None`` keeps the hero value.
    97	
    98	    ``batch_size`` is the global batch across all data-parallel racks. It does not scale with
    99	    ``dp_racks``. ``batch_size`` and ``schedule_steps`` change the token budget for the heuristic.
   100	    """
   101	    if not run_id.strip():
   102	        raise ValueError("run_id must not be empty")
   103	    if dp_racks <= 0:
   104	        raise ValueError(f"dp_racks must be positive, got {dp_racks}")
   105	    if num_steps <= 0:
   106	        raise ValueError(f"num_steps must be positive, got {num_steps}")
   107	    if checkpoint_interval <= timedelta(0):
   108	        raise ValueError(f"checkpoint_interval must be positive, got {checkpoint_interval}")
   109	    if profile_steps < 0:
   110	        raise ValueError(f"profile_steps must be non-negative, got {profile_steps}")
   111	    if profile_start_step < 0:
   112	        raise ValueError(f"profile_start_step must be non-negative, got {profile_start_step}")
   113	    if profile_steps > 0 and profile_start_step >= num_steps:
   114	        raise ValueError(f"profile_start_step must be less than num_steps={num_steps}, got {profile_start_step}")
   115	    # `schedule_steps` sets the whole learning-rate schedule; `num_steps` is the absolute step the
   116	    # run stops at (a restore resumes mid-schedule, so it must lie past the restored step).
   117	    # Both matter, and they enter in different places. The optimizer heuristic scales learning rate,
   118	    # adam_lr, and epsilon from a token budget (`num_train_steps * batch * seq`), which fixes the
   119	    # peak. Warmup and decay are *fractions* of `TrainerConfig.num_train_steps`, so that field has to
   120	    # carry the schedule length too -- passing `num_steps` there warms up in `0.01 * num_steps` and
   121	    # decays to `min_lr_ratio` by the end of the short run, which is a whole miniature schedule
   122	    # rather than the head of a long one. Default keeps the two equal, which is the previous behavior.
   123	    if schedule_steps is not None and schedule_steps <= 0:
   124	        raise ValueError(f"schedule_steps must be positive, got {schedule_steps}")
   125	    if schedule_steps is not None and schedule_steps < num_steps:
   126	        raise ValueError(f"schedule_steps={schedule_steps} must be at least num_steps={num_steps}")
   127	    total_schedule_steps = schedule_steps if schedule_steps is not None else num_steps
   128	    _, optimizer = build_hero_configs(
   129	        num_train_steps=total_schedule_steps,
   130	        batch_size=batch_size,
   131	    )
   132	    model = HERO_MODEL_CONFIG
   133	    overrides = {
   134	        name: value
   135	        for name, value in (
   136	            ("num_experts", num_experts),
   137	            ("num_experts_per_token", num_experts_per_token),
   138	            ("intermediate_dim", intermediate_dim),
   139	            ("capacity_factor", capacity_factor),
   140	            ("latent_dim", latent_dim),
   141	            ("moe_implementation", moe_implementation),
   142	        )
   143	        if value is not None
   144	    }
   145	    if overrides:
   146	        model = dataclasses.replace(model, **overrides)
   147	    model = with_transport_remat_mode(model)
   148	    # A bank that is not divisible by the expert axis fails inside `moe_mlp`, which is after the rack
   149	    # is already allocated and the workspace is built. Reject it here instead.
   150	    if model.num_experts % HERO_EP_EXPERT_AXIS_SIZE != 0:
   151	        raise ValueError(f"num_experts={model.num_experts} must be divisible by {HERO_EP_EXPERT_AXIS_SIZE}")
   152	    local_experts = model.num_experts // HERO_EP_EXPERT_AXIS_SIZE
   153	    if local_experts % model.num_expert_waves != 0:
   154	        raise ValueError(
   155	            f"local expert count={local_experts} must be divisible by num_expert_waves={model.num_expert_waves}"
   156	        )
   157	    # The ragged transport requires one GPU per process; fail fast if this is not satisfied.
   158	    if model.moe_implementation == RAGGED_MOE_IMPLEMENTATION and processes_per_task != HERO_GPUS_PER_NODE:
   159	        raise ValueError(
   160	            f"{RAGGED_MOE_IMPLEMENTATION} needs one process per GPU: pass "
   161	            f"processes_per_task={HERO_GPUS_PER_NODE}, got {processes_per_task}"
   162	        )
   163	    pooled = model.moe_implementation == "fixed_pooled_wave_all_to_all"
   164	    if pooled and model.pooled_transport_capacity_factor is None:
   165	        raise AssertionError("the pooled-wave hero requires a transport capacity factor")
   166	    backend_tag = model.moe_implementation.replace("_", "-")
   167	    capacity_tag = f"capacity-{model.capacity_factor:g}"
   168	    # Only the pooled transport has a receiver capacity of its own to report.
   169	    transport_capacity_tags = (f"transport-capacity-{model.pooled_transport_capacity_factor:g}",) if pooled else ()
   170	    wave_tag = f"expert-waves-{model.num_expert_waves}"
   171	    size_tag = f"e{model.num_experts}-i{model.intermediate_dim}"
   172	    wandb_project = os.environ.get("WANDB_PROJECT") or DEFAULT_WANDB_PROJECT
   173	    grug_trainer = hero_grug_trainer_config(
   174	        replica_axis_size=dp_racks,
   175	        training_data_mode=training_data_mode,
   176	        watch_mode=watch_mode,
   177	        save_checkpoints=save_checkpoints,
   178	        master_param_mode=master_param_mode,
   179	    )
   180	    train_resources = ResourceConfig.with_gpu(
   181	        "GB200",
   182	        count=HERO_GPUS_PER_NODE,
   183	        cpu=HERO_NODE_CPU,
   184	        ram=HERO_NODE_RAM,
   185	        disk=HERO_NODE_DISK,
   186	        replicas=HERO_EP_NODES * dp_racks,
   187	    )
   188	    name = f"grug/{run_id}"
   189	    version = resolve_version(name, version)
   190	    validation = validation_datasets() if eval_every > 0 else []
   191	    flops_per_example, _ = _compute_flops(model_config=model)
   192	    experiment_flops = flops_per_example * batch_size * total_schedule_steps
   193	
   194	    def build_config(ctx: StepContext) -> GrugRunConfig:
   195	        trainer = hero_trainer_config(
   196	            run_id=run_id,
   197	            seed=seed,
   198	            train_batch_size=batch_size,
   199	            num_train_steps=total_schedule_steps,
   200	            master_param_mode=master_param_mode,
   201	            load_checkpoint_path=restore_from,
   202	            profiler=ProfilerConfig(
   203	                enabled=profile_steps > 0,
   204	                start_step=profile_start_step,
   205	                num_steps=profile_steps,
   206	                # One rank is enough for a step trace, and tracing all 64 multiplies the upload
   207	                # without adding signal.
   208	                process_index=0,
   209	                # Host scopes and HLO metadata identify compiled model regions in XProf.
   210	                profile_options=ProfileOptionsConfig(
   211	                    host_tracer_level=1,
   212	                    python_tracer_level=0,
   213	                    enable_hlo_proto=True,
   214	                ),
   215	            ),
   216	            tracker=WandbConfig(
   217	                entity="marin-community",
   218	                project=wandb_project,
   219	                tags=[
   220	                    "grug",
   290	        deps=(HARRIER_MIX_2026_08_18_STORE, *validation),
   291	        runtime_args={"train_resources": train_resources},
   292	    )
   293	
   294	
   295	@click.command()
   296	@click.option("--run-id", required=True, help="Run identifier for artifact and W&B names.")
   297	@click.option(
   298	    "--dp-racks",
   299	    type=click.IntRange(min=1),
   300	    default=1,
   301	    show_default=True,
   302	    help="Data-parallel NVL72 rack count. --batch-size stays global across all racks.",
   303	)
   304	@click.option(
   305	    "--num-steps",
   306	    type=click.IntRange(min=1),
   307	    default=DEFAULT_HERO_STEPS,
   308	    show_default=True,
   309	    help=(
   310	        "Number of steps at which training terminates. When restoring from a checkpoint, "
   311	        "the number of steps taken in this run will be less than --num-steps "
   312	        "by the number of steps at the checkpoint."
   313	    ),
   314	)
   315	@click.option(
   316	    "--schedule-steps",
   317	    type=click.IntRange(min=1),
   318	    default=None,
   319	    help=(
   320	        "Build the learning-rate schedule for this many steps instead of --num-steps. The optimizer "
   321	        "heuristic scales its rates from the implied token budget, so this trains the head of a long "
   322	        "run's schedule. Defaults to --num-steps."
   323	    ),
   324	)
   325	@click.option(
   326	    "--seed",
   327	    type=int,
   328	    default=0,
   329	    help="Trainer seed. Vary it across otherwise identical runs to measure run-to-run variance.",
   330	)
   331	@click.option(
   332	    "--num-experts",
   333	    type=click.IntRange(min=1),
   334	    default=HERO_MODEL_CONFIG.num_experts,
   335	    show_default=True,
   336	    help=(
   337	        f"Override the routed expert count. The count must be divisible by {HERO_EP_EXPERT_AXIS_SIZE}, "
   338	        f"and the local expert count must support {HERO_MODEL_CONFIG.num_expert_waves} waves."
   339	    ),
   340	)
   341	@click.option(
   342	    "--num-experts-per-token",
   343	    type=click.IntRange(min=1),
   344	    default=HERO_MODEL_CONFIG.num_experts_per_token,
   345	    show_default=True,
   346	    help="Override the routed top-k. Scales both active parameters and the EP dispatch buffers.",
   347	)
   348	@click.option(
   349	    "--intermediate-dim",
   350	    type=click.IntRange(min=1),
   351	    default=HERO_MODEL_CONFIG.intermediate_dim,
   352	    show_default=True,
   353	    help="Override the routed expert width.",
   354	)
   355	@click.option(
   356	    "--batch-size",
   357	    type=click.IntRange(min=1),
   358	    default=HERO_EP_BATCH_SIZE,
   359	    show_default=True,
   360	    help="Global sequences per step. This value does not scale with --dp-racks.",
   361	)
   362	@click.option(
   363	    "--moe-implementation",
   364	    default=None,
   365	    help="Override the MoE backend, e.g. ragged_all_to_all. Defaults to the hero spec.",
   366	)
   367	@click.option(
   368	    "--master-params",
   369	    type=click.Choice([mode.value for mode in MasterParamMode]),
   370	    default=HERO_MASTER_PARAM_MODE.value,
   371	    show_default=True,
   372	    help=("Where the authoritative fp32 weights live: on device, or as a pinned-host master."),
   373	)
   374	@click.option(
   375	    "--processes-per-task",
   376	    type=click.IntRange(min=1),
   377	    default=HERO_PROCESSES_PER_TASK,
   378	    show_default=True,
   379	    help="JAX processes per node.",
   380	)
   381	@click.option(

    60	- SERIALIZATION: one arm on the rack at a time, and arm N+1 is submitted only
    61	  after arm N's watchdog exits — the coordinator's iris `--timeout 3500`
    62	  clock starts when the coordinator starts (it schedules instantly and waits
    63	  on its child), so an arm queued behind another arm burns its 1h budget in
    64	  the queue and gets killed just as its train job starts.
    65	- RESUBMISSION: an invalid arm (watchdog kill, preemption, incomplete

    67	  reused RID merges W&B histories (silent window mixing), reuses the

    69	  recipe), and can vacuously reuse the artifact layer.
    70	- Hero liveness: the live hero is NOT currently training (step-30000 is the
    71	  newest permanent checkpoint; no hero job in iris). Before each arm, check
    72	  the rack queue; if the hero ever reappears on this cluster, pause the
    73	  campaign and reassess priorities rather than contending with it.

    75	  submit anything if the loop is blocked >1h on analysis.

    78	  is BEFORE restore + master migration + compile; a ceiling kill on a cold

    82	## Metric and guards
    83	
    84	- Metric: mean `throughput/mfu` over scored steps +5..+19 relative to the
    85	  first logged step (`score.py --relative`). Higher is better.
    86	- Guards (from the same run): `moe/drop_fraction` max/mean not worse than the
    87	  paired control; `train/loss` at the window's last step equivalent to
    88	  control (bf16-noise band; 24k arms showed 1.29964-65 across four arms);
    89	  `window_complete` must be true.
    90	- Noise model: only ONE NVL72 rack exists, so there is no placement variance
    91	  (user, 2026-08-29) — residual noise is neighbor jobs, clocks/thermals, and
    92	  run-to-run scheduling jitter. Iteration 0 sizes it empirically: >=3 control
    93	  draws; the keep bar is max(0.15 MFU, 3 x the measured control sd). KEEP
    94	  decisions still use interleaved control/treatment draws (the control also
    95	  re-verifies the kept stack), and near-threshold deltas get additional
    96	  draws instead of a call. Periodic re-control draws guard against slow
    97	  drift across the campaign.
    98	- Fidelity gate for keeps: paired-window comparison; the loss guard compares
    99	  the FULL 15-step loss series against the paired control (max |delta|
   100	  within the bf16-noise band), not just the last step — data is
   101	  deterministic at a fixed restore step so the series pair pointwise. Any
   102	  keep that changes numerics beyond scheduling/layout gets flagged and needs
   103	  explicit reasoning about why the trajectory is unchanged.
   104	
   105	## Iteration accounting
   106	
   107	Per user: only hypotheses that reach the rack count toward the 24-iteration
   108	floor; review-killed ideas do not. Campaign runs 24 rack iterations or 24 h,
   109	whichever comes LAST. Iteration 0 = 30k-protocol baseline (control draw(s)).
